    - **Chelsea's SYMPTOM is real; the mechanism was misdiagnosed.** A failed draft is imported perfectly and then **nothing acts on it**: the digest's "subscription not healthy" alert filters `SubscriptionStatus != active`, but ADN retries a declined card so the status stays `active` — **67 of 81 failing registrations are invisible to that alert by construction.** The three edge-case sections at the bottom of the digest are eCheck-scoped and structurally cannot hold an ARB draft (on 08-24, a morning with 10 declines, all three printed green all-clears). The counts line has no field for it. Behind-in-Payment computes the arrears correctly but is a manual per-job click. **Nobody — family or director — is ever notified.**
    - **Exposure: 81 registrations / $269,727 owed in 60 days; 30 dead plans still owing $53,856** that auto-draft can no longer collect. Worst case found: one plan declined **7 times across 9 months**, collected 3 of 10 installments, expired owing $3,605 — every failure sat in a sweep digest.
    - **Design settled with Todd 08-25 → `docs/Plans/ARB-FAILED-DRAFT-NOTIFICATION.md`** (notify on failure from the 4am sweep; 2nd/15th expiring-card send). **✅ BUILT 08-25/26 — see the RESOLVED block at the foot of this entry.**
  4. **Her stretch option**: *"One other amazing option would be that the system automatically sends this email out twice a month!!!"* — i.e. a **scheduled** send rather than a button. **That actually resolves the live-ADN cost problem rather than worsening it**: a twice-monthly job can sweep on a schedule, cache what it finds, and send from stored data — no operator waiting on hundreds of API calls. **If this is built at all, the scheduled shape is the better one**, and the manual SuperUser button becomes a "run it now" on the same machinery.
- **Severity**: 🟡 **Reliability, not convenience** — but **re-based 08-25, because the original rationale was factually wrong.** ~~an ADN General Error the sweep does not import, leaving TSIC's records wrong~~. **Records are NOT wrong — the sweep imports failed drafts correctly and always has (see CONFIRMED block above).** The reliability case stands on different and larger ground: **a correctly-imported failure reaches nobody.** 81 registrations / $269,727 owed in 60 days; **$53,856 on 30 dead plans is already uncollectable by auto-draft.** Currently mitigated only by one person doing it by hand, job by job — and by directors hand-writing decline emails, **300+ of them logged in `Jobs.emailLogs` since 2022 across ~24 subject variants.**
- ### ✅ RESOLVED BY THE SWEEP REFACTOR — Todd, 2026-08-26. Reviewed against the built code, decision by decision.
  **The feature Ann asked for is not being built, because the thing she wanted it to accomplish now happens without anyone pressing anything.** Chelsea no longer does this by hand, job by job — which was the entire reliability argument. Verified in `ArbNotificationService.NotifyExpiringCardsAsync` and `AdnSweepBackgroundService`.
  - **Shape**: the **scheduled** option, which the entry above already called the better one. Fires on the **2nd and the 15th** after the sweep, in its own try, so nothing here can touch the money code's verdict. **Not the 1st** — that morning belongs to the month-end close, and a send behind the close's `IsTrustworthy` gate would vanish whenever the gate held.
  - **Scope = every job holding a live subscription** (`GetJobIdsWithLiveSubscriptionsAsync`), which is Ann's *"YES ALL JOBS!!!!"* read exactly. Decision 2 answered.
  - **Decision 1 (sweep or cache)**: live, one ADN call per job with live plans — about 21 today, not the few hundred the original filing feared. **Note the estate-wide single call identified in the CORRECTED block was NOT taken**; per-job is simpler and 21 calls is not a cost. Revisit only if the ARB estate multiplies.
  - **Decision 3 (dedupe) — RULED: NONE, deliberately** (`46547302`). ADN matches on the card CURRENTLY attached to the subscription, so a family who updated theirs drops out of the 15th's set on its own. The second pass reaches only non-responders. **Deduping would withhold the reminder from exactly the people who still need it. Do not "fix" this.**
  - **Decision 4 (whose name)**: the owning job's default director is FromName plus Reply-To, so the mail looks like it came from the club, not from TSIC.
  - **Decision 5 (Email Log)**: one `emailLogs` row per job per email type, matching `EmailBatchService.CreateAuditRowAsync` — recipient tally, joined SendTo, body TEMPLATE rather than one family's rendered copy. `GetSentToAddressesAsync` still resolves "was this family written to?" from the batched row.
  - **✅ THE UNATTENDED-RUN BLOCKER THIS ENTRY RAISED IS FIXED.** The old `return null` on an ADN error is gone: `GetExpiringCardFlagsAsync` THROWS, and the pass catches per job and records a skip reading *"(whole job) — expiring-card lookup failed"*. **A dead ADN call can no longer render as a green "no cards expiring".** The summary prints jobs checked, found, emailed and NOT emailed, with the by-name skip list.
  - **✅ The latent partitioning bug this entry predicted never materialised.** It was `StartDefensiveEmailsAsync` handing every director one shared `notifiedNames` list; the new pass builds no cross-job director list at all, because directors are not recipients.
  - **⛔ The manual cross-job SuperUser button is DECLINED, not deferred.** The production hand trigger is deliberately absent — `AdnSweepController.ExpiringCardsDryRun` is sandbox-only — because that pass mails thousands of families across every job and is scheduled precisely so that no one has to decide to send it. A hand trigger is a way to send it twice, or on the wrong day, with no undo. **The per-job send on ARB Health remains** for a genuine off-cycle case.
- **✅ ANN'S POINT 2 IS SATISFIED — ⛔ and do NOT restate this as "the director is never told". That was claimed on 08-26 and is FALSE.**
  `FlushAuditAsync` writes one `emailLogs` row per job per email type — that job's `JobId`, the director's address as `SendFrom`, recipient count, `;`-joined `SendTo`, body template. **The director sees every notice their club sent, in their own Email Log**, and `GetSentToAddressesAsync` matches `%;addr;%` so the player panel and the family's own sent-mail list resolve it too. The director is not merely the From line — the send is on their books.
  **The one thing that writes no row is a family who could not be emailed at all** (opted out, no sendable address, no family username): a skipped registrant never reaches `Bucket()` and the flush filters `Recipients.Count > 0`, so those names appear only in the by-name list inside the `support@` summary.
  - **⛔ RULED — Todd, 2026-08-26: NO ACTION.** *"I don't really care re skipped email, extremely rare if ever."* The support digest already names them. **Do not build a per-job director summary or an ARB Health flag for unreachable families, and do not re-file this as a gap.**
- **⛔ THE DEAD-PLAN BACKLOG DOES NOT CLOSE WITH THIS ITEM.** The 53,856 dollars across 30 dead plans is **structurally out of reach of everything built here**: a dead plan never re-enters a batch, so the failure notifier never sees it, and an ended subscription is not in ADN's expiring-card set either. **Nothing reaches those families. Track it separately — do not let it close silently under AR-037.**
- **Status**: ✅ **RESOLVED BY BUILD — Todd, 08-26.** Filed 08-25, re-raised the same day after Ann's review with Chelsea, and resolved by the sweep refactor rather than by building the requested button. **ONE thread survives the close: the dead-plan backlog, which belongs to its own item.** Director visibility is RULED and closed above.
- ### 📣 ANSWER FOR ANN / CHELSEA (08-25) — the embedded defect claim, resolved
  **"It can lead to a General Error with ADN which isn't swept/imported."** Two halves, and they resolve differently:
  - *"This is infrequent"* — **correct.** 119 in six years, and none since May 2026.
  - *"which isn't swept/imported"* — **not correct.** The 4am sweep imports both `declined` and `generalError` drafts and has done since 2020. It books a $0 record against the registration, leaves the balances alone (no money moved), and refreshes the plan's status from Authorize.Net. **The accounting is right.**

  **What Chelsea is actually running into is real, and it is bigger than she thought.** The failure is recorded correctly and then **nothing happens with it** — no alert, no flag, no email to the family, nothing on the director's screen. It appears only as one line reading "declined / $0.00" inside a long table in a 4am email to TSIC support. So from her chair it looks exactly like the system missed it. It didn't; it just never told anyone.
  **We are fixing that**, in the sweep itself: when a scheduled payment fails, the family will be emailed that morning — under the club's name, replies going to the club's director — and the 4am report will show how many cards failed against how many families were reached, so a gap is visible instead of silent.
  **Chelsea's twice-monthly idea is being built too**, on the 2nd and the 15th, and **her instinct about cost was right where our objection was wrong** — it turns out to be a single call for the whole estate.
  **Nothing needs re-testing from Ann on this.** The remaining decision on the SuperUser cross-job button itself is Todd's, and is unchanged by these findings.
- **Disposition recommendation**: the embedded General-Error defect closes as **MISDIAGNOSED — not a records defect** — and is **reopened as the reporting-and-notification defect** (design filed, not built). **Do NOT close it as "no defect"**: the consequence Chelsea reported is real and measured at $53,856. **Part A (the SuperUser cross-job send) remains OPEN and untouched — Todd's call.**

### AR-036: [Job Clone] Display Name is overwritten with the new job name instead of carrying forward — and the field belongs beside the email settings
- **Topic**: Job Clone workbench → **Display name** · Configure → Job Settings → General → Display Name (`Jobs.DisplayName`)
- **Request (Ann, 08-25)**: *"The Display Name in Configure/Job Settings/General is not cloning forward to new jobs. Instead, Customer/Job is replacing what was there from the clone source. Also, in the Job Clone is it possible to move this field above the email section as that is what it pertains to."*
- **✅ BUG CONFIRMED — one line, and it is the seed, not the save.** `job-clone-workbench.component.ts:435`: **`this.displayName.set(this.jobNameTarget());`** — the form seeds Display name from the **new job name** (the `Customer:Job` string Ann is seeing), and **never looks at the source job's DisplayName**. The operator then submits that value and the backend writes it verbatim: `JobCloneResetRules.cs:68`, **`job.DisplayName = req.DisplayName;`** — with **no `?? source.DisplayName` fallback**, unlike every communications field immediately beneath it (`:74-81`, all `req.X ?? source.X`). So the source's Display Name is discarded on every clone.
  - **The source value IS available to seed from** — the clone-source DTO already carries `displayName` (`JobCloneSource*.ts:11`), so this is a **one-line change with nothing to plumb**.
  - **Recommended fix**: seed `displayName` from the source's value, falling back to the new job name **only when the source has none**. Consider matching the communications pattern on the backend too (`req.DisplayName ?? source.DisplayName`) so a null request cannot blank it.
- **✅ AND ANN'S REASONING FOR THE MOVE IS CORRECT — Display Name is an EMAIL field.** It is the **From display name** on outbound mail: `RegistrationSearchService.cs:1073-1075` — *"From display = the public job/org label (`Jobs.DisplayName`, falling back to `JobName`)"*, `var fromName = jobConfirmation?.DisplayName ?? jobConfirmation?.JobName;` — and again at `:1132`, plus `ArbDefensiveService.cs:219` (`senderName = senderInfo?.DisplayName ?? "TEAMSPORTSINFO.COM"`). **So this is not a cosmetic reshuffle: the field currently sits in the identity group (`job-clone-workbench.component.html:74-76`, beside Year and Season) while it actually governs what families see in their inbox.** Moving it above the email block puts it with the settings it belongs to — and makes the clone operator likelier to notice it is wrong.
  - **⚠ That is also WHY the bug matters more than it looks.** A clone silently replacing Display Name with `Customer:Job` changes **the From name on every email the new job sends** — so a family's confirmation arrives from a machine-looking label instead of the club's name, and nobody notices until someone reads their inbox.
- **Severity**: 🟡 Bug + placement — silent per-clone data loss on a field that determines outbound email identity
- **✅ BUILT — Todd, 08-26. Three changes, both parts of Ann's report.**
  1. **Seed (the bug)** — `job-clone-workbench.component.ts`: `displayName.set(source.displayName?.trim() || jobNameTarget())`. The source's value now carries forward.
  2. **Backend guard** — `JobCloneResetRules.cs:68`: `job.DisplayName = req.DisplayName ?? source.DisplayName`. Now matches the eight communications fields directly beneath it — **null keeps the source, empty string still clears**. The unguarded assignment let a null request blank it.
  3. **Field moved** — out of the Identity grid (beside Year/Season) to the **first field of the Communications panel**, where the settings it governs live.
  - **⚠ THE FALLBACK IS LOAD-BEARING, not defensive.** When the source has no Display Name it still seeds the **new job name**, because `canSubmit` (`:301`) **requires** a non-empty Display name — seeding blank would block the operator on a field they never chose. So a source with no Display Name behaves exactly as it does today.
  - **Accepted consequence, deliberately not solved**: in that NULL case the clone lands an explicit `DisplayName` equal to the new `JobName`. At send time identical (the mail path already reads `DisplayName ?? JobName`), but a later job rename leaves the copy stale where a NULL would have tracked. Fixing it means relaxing the required-field rule — scope creep on this workbench.
  - **⚠ A STYLING TRAP WAS HIT AND BACKED OUT — do not re-add it.** A per-field `<span class="jc-hint">` under the input renders in **ALL CAPS**: `.field-label` is a 10px uppercase caption class (`styles/_forms.scss:34`) and `.jc-hint` does not reset `text-transform`. This is the same trap the league-rename row comment in this file already warns about. The explanation lives in the panel's `<p class="jc-hint">` instead.
  - `dotnet build` clean; `ng build` clean. **Job-clone rule honoured — the diff was reviewed and approved before any edit.**
- **Status**: 🟡 **BUILT — Todd, 08-26. Source-only, awaiting deploy.** Filed 08-25 at Ann's request, built 08-26.
  - **HOW TO VERIFY (Ann)**: clone a job whose source has a real Display Name (not the `Customer:Job` string). On the clone workbench the **Display name** field now sits at the top of **Communications** and is **pre-filled with the source's value**, not the new job name. After the clone, check Configure → Job Settings → General on the new job — and, better, **send one email from it and read the From name.**

### AR-035: [Registration wizards] Rename the "Finish" button on confirmation screens to "Return Home" — the process is already finished
- **Topic**: player / team / adult registration wizards → terminal step action button
- **Request (Ann, 08-25)**: *"Can you replace 'Finish' badge on all Confirmation screens at the end of registration with 'Home' or 'Return Home'. The process is already finished when they reach the confirmation screen; this implies there is something more to do."*
- **She is right about the behaviour, not just the wording**: the button does **not** complete anything — it **navigates to the job home page**. Verified on the adult wizard: `onFinishConfirmation()` → `router.navigateByUrl('/{jobPath}')` (`adult.component.ts:206-208`). So "Finish" names an act that already happened, and a parent who has just paid is invited to perform one more step that isn't one.
- **✅ THREE PLACES, all confirmed — this is the whole surface:**
  1. **Adult/coach** — the only *literal* label: `adult/steps/confirmation-step.component.ts:73-74`, `Finish <i class="bi bi-arrow-right">`.
  2. **Player** — `player.component.ts:243`, `continueLabel` returns `'Finish'` when `currentStepId() === 'confirmation'`.
  3. **Team** — `team.component.ts:276`, `case 'review': return 'Finish'`. **⚠ Note the step is named `review`, not `confirmation`** — but it *is* the terminal step; the code says so at `:265-269` (*"Review is the terminal step — there is nothing to go 'Back' to once a registration is complete… mirroring the player confirmation step"*). **Easy to miss when grepping for "confirmation"; don't leave this one behind.**
- **⚠ CHANGE THE ICON WITH THE LABEL.** The adult button pairs its text with **`bi-arrow-right`** — a "next step" arrow, which carries the same false implication as the word. **`bi-house` (or no icon) fits "Return Home".** Renaming the text and keeping a forward arrow only half-fixes it.
- **Wording**: Ann offered "Home" or "Return Home". **Recommend "Return Home"** — "Home" alone reads as a label rather than an action, and the button is an action.
- **⚠ Check the other two for icons too** — player and team render through a shared action bar (`continueLabel`), so confirm whether that bar adds its own directional icon; if it does, the same reasoning applies there.
- **Severity**: 🟢 UX / copy — no defect; it misleads a user at the one moment they should feel finished
- **✅ BUILT — Todd, 08-26. "Return Home" (Todd's call; Ann offered "Home" or "Return Home").** The button is an action and every sibling in these wizards is verb-first, so a bare noun reads as a nav link.
  - **SIX files, not three — the entry undercounted, and the extra two are the ones that mattered.** Player and team do NOT own their button: they render through `app-wizard-shell`, which has **its own** forward button in the header **and** passes through to `app-wizard-action-bar`. **Both hardcoded `bi-chevron-right` AND `aria-label="'Proceed: ' + continueLabel()"`.** Renaming the label alone would have shipped a forward chevron and a screen reader announcing *"Proceed: Return Home"* — the same false implication, twice, in the two wizards that matter most.
  - **The fix is one input, not an icon swap.** `continueIntent: 'proceed' | 'done'` on both shell and action bar, defaulting to `'proceed'` so **every non-terminal step is byte-identical**. `'done'` swaps the chevron for `bi-house` and drops the "Proceed:" prefix from the aria label. Player binds it on `confirmation`, team on `review`. **Do not hardcode the icon in the shared bars — they serve every step of every wizard.**
  - **Adult never needed the input.** Its shell hides Continue on `confirmation` (`adult.component.ts:90`) and the step renders its own button, so that one was a literal edit: `Finish <i class="bi bi-arrow-right">` → `<i class="bi bi-house"></i> Return Home`. Icon moved to LEADING to match the `Resend Confirmation Email` button beside it.
  - **Team's step is `review`, not `confirmation`** — as the entry warned. Caught.
  - Stale comments naming the old label were refreshed in all four components; `processInsuranceOnlyFinish` and `onFinishConfirmation` are **method names, not labels**, and were left alone.
  - `ng build` clean; `verify:no-effect` passes. **No backend change.**
- **Status**: 🟡 **BUILT — Todd, 08-26. Source-only, awaiting deploy.** Filed 08-25 at Ann's request, built 08-26.
  - **HOW TO VERIFY (Ann)**: reach the end of a **player**, **team** and **adult/coach** registration. Each terminal screen's button should read **Return Home** with a **house** icon, and clicking it lands on the job home page. **Check the mobile width too** — the action bar renders a short label there, and "Return Home" has no "Proceed to " prefix to strip, so it should read identically at both widths.

### AR-034: [Search Registrations] The SuperUser "Delete Registration" control for Club Reps is hidden by *how you searched* — and it hides in exactly the case where deletion works
- **Topic**: Search Registrations → registrant fly-in → **Delete Registration** (Club Rep registrations)
- **Request (Ann, 08-25)**: *"SuperUser ONLY should have a RED Delete button to REMOVE Club Reps. Club Reps sometimes create a second account when they return to pay and inactivating doesn't remove this account. This function was available previously and used a lot, including removing the test registrations before releasing a job."*
- **✅ THE FUNCTION EXISTS AND IS ALREADY SUPERUSER-ONLY — front and back.** Backend `RegistrationSearchService.DeleteRegistrationAsync:1299-1303` refuses a Club Rep delete unless the caller is Superuser (same shape for Unassigned Adult, `:1295-1297`). Frontend renders a red `btn-outline-danger` "Delete Registration" (`registration-detail-panel.component.html:11-14`), greyed with a reason when blocked (`:16-19`). **So the ask is already built. The problem is that it is unfindable.**
- **🔴 THE DEFECT — visibility depends on the SEARCH, not the registration** (`registration-detail-panel.component.ts:317-332`):
  - `showDeleteControl` → for a Club Rep, `isSuperuser() && clubRepOnlySearch()`.
  - `clubRepOnlySearch` → true only when the **current search's role filter is exactly `[Club Rep]`** (`activeRoleIds()`, matched on the role GUID; the "ACTIVE, NOT WAITLISTED" sentinel is deliberately excluded).
  - **So the same rep shows a delete button or no delete button depending on how you navigated to them.** Nothing on screen explains this, and nothing about the registration has changed.
- **✅ ANN'S OBSERVATION IS THE PROOF, and it inverts what you would expect (08-25).** She created a **clean club rep with no teams and no accounting** on `lftc-fallshowcase-2026` and saw **no button at all** — while reps that **do** have teams/accounting showed the button greyed out. Reading the code, clean = `canDelete` **true** = the RED button; with-history = greyed. **The only way to get her result is that the two were reached by different searches.** ⚠ **So the control hides precisely in the case where deletion would succeed, and appears (disabled) in the cases where it cannot.** That is the worst possible arrangement of the two states.
- **The original rationale is sound but the remedy is wrong.** The comment explains the gate exists so the control is not surfaced on the "ACTIVE, NOT WAITLISTED" filter, whose reps own a team and *"can never fire"* the delete. **Fair goal — but it keys on the search rather than the row.** `canDelete` already answers "can this specific rep be deleted?" from the row itself (`noAccounting && clubRepTeamCount === 0`). **Recommend: drop `clubRepOnlySearch` from `showDeleteControl` and let `canDelete` govern**, keeping Superuser-only. The disabled-with-reason state then does the teaching, per house pattern, on the row's own facts.
- **🔸 SECOND, SEPARATE ASK INSIDE THE SAME REPORT — do not conflate them.** Ann also wants *"removing the test registrations before releasing a job"*. Test reps usually **do** carry accounting rows and teams, which `canDelete` refuses by design. **That is a real request to delete a rep WITH financial history** — it destroys money records.
- **✅ RULED — Ann, 08-25: NO. *"Deleting a club rep who holds payments should NOT be allowed."*** **`canDelete`'s existing `noAccounting && clubRepTeamCount === 0` rule is CORRECT and stays.** Do **not** build a cascade delete, a confirmation-that-names-what-it-destroys, or a "purge test registrations" tool. **The refusal is the feature.**
  - ⚠ **This does NOT weaken the visibility fix above — it strengthens it.** Ann's actual case (a duplicate account created when a rep returns to pay) carries **no payments and no teams**, so it satisfies `canDelete` and deletes cleanly. The bug was only ever that the button hides in exactly that case.
  - ⚠ **Consequence to accept, not solve:** a test rep that took a test payment can no longer be deleted. **That is the intended outcome of this ruling** — the answer for a polluted pre-release job is a fresh clone, not a destructive purge.
- **Severity**: 🟡 Bug / discoverability on an admin destructive action — the built capability is effectively unreachable in its working case
- **✅ BUILT — Todd, 08-26.** `clubRepOnlySearch` dropped from `showDeleteControl`; a Club Rep delete control is now **Superuser-only and nothing else**, and `canDelete` governs enabled-vs-greyed from the row's own facts. `showDeleteBlockedReason()` already names the blocker (teams first, else accounting-record count), so the disabled state does the teaching — no new copy needed.
  - **The dead coupling was removed too, on purpose.** `clubRepOnlySearch`, the `activeRoleIds` input and the parent's `[activeRoleIds]` binding are all gone. Leaving a live input whose only job was gating visibility on the search is an invitation to re-key the same decision the same wrong way. `RoleIds` is no longer imported by the panel.
  - **What Ann should now see**: her clean rep on `lftc-fallshowcase-2026` (no teams, no accounting) gets the **RED** Delete Registration button from any search; a rep with teams or payments gets the **greyed** one with the reason toast. Previously those two were backwards.
  - **Non-Club-Rep roles are untouched** — `showDeleteControl` still returns true for them, and the backend Superuser gates on Club Rep / Unassigned Adult (`DeleteRegistrationAsync`) were never in question. **No backend change; `ng build` clean.**
- **Status**: 🟡 **BUILT — Todd, 08-26. Source-only, awaiting deploy.** Filed 08-25, built 08-26. The "delete with history" half stays CLOSED by Ann's ruling above — do not build it.

### AR-033: [Header] Make the `Customer:Job` name in the header copyable — it is pasted into emails constantly
- **Topic**: client header bar → the job-name pill (e.g. **`Lax For The Cure:Fall Showcase 2026`**)
- **Request (Ann, 08-25)**: *"Is it possible to make the customer:job name in the header copyable. This would be used all the time in email communications."*
- **⚠ THE CATCH — that element is ALREADY a click target, and the click navigates away.** Both renderings carry `role="button"`, `tabindex="0"` and `(click)="goHome()"`: desktop `client-header-bar.component.html:51-58`, mobile `:62-73`. **So a user who tries to select the text today drags across a button and gets sent to the home page instead.** That is very likely why it reads as "not copyable" — the text is selectable in principle, but the navigation intercepts the gesture. **Any fix has to keep or deliberately replace that navigation**, not just add a copy affordance beside it.
- **The value is `jobService.currentJob()?.jobName`** (`client-header-bar.component.ts:52`) — already the exact `Customer:Job` string Ann wants, so there is nothing to compose. **⚠ Mobile splits it across two lines** via `jobNameLines()` (`:55`), so a copy must use the underlying `jobName()` value, **not** the rendered text, or a phone copy comes back broken in half.
- **Options for Todd, cheapest first:**
  1. **A small copy icon beside the pill** — clipboard write of `jobName()`, plus a toast or a brief "Copied" state. Leaves navigation untouched. **Least risk, and probably the right answer.**
  2. **Make the text itself selectable** and move the navigation onto a smaller target (or require the existing home icon, which is right there). Cleaner conceptually, but it changes a control users already know.
  3. **Both**: selectable text *and* a copy button, navigation moved to the home icon only.
- **⚠ Clipboard access needs a secure context** — fine on `https` and on `localhost`, but it fails silently on a plain-`http` origin. Use the async Clipboard API with a fallback, and **confirm the copy visibly**; a copy button that does nothing on failure is worse than no button.
- **Accessibility**: whatever is added needs a real `aria-label` (*"Copy event name"*), a `:focus-visible` state per the design system, and must not leave two adjacent controls with the same accessible name as the navigation target.
- **✅ BUILT — Todd, 08-26. Option 1, and gated to SuperUser alone.** Commits `b472e9cd` + `be3ab91d`. Frontend build clean. A copy button sits beside the job-name pill on **both** desktop and mobile; `goHome()` is untouched on every surface.
  - **The button is a SIBLING of the pill, never a child.** Nested inside it, a copy click would bubble straight into `goHome()` — it also calls `stopPropagation()`. This is the whole point of the trap filed above.
  - **Copies `jobName()`, never the rendered text** — mobile splits the name across two lines via `jobNameLines()`, so a text-based copy would come back broken in half on a phone.
  - **Failure is SHOWN, not swallowed.** `navigator.clipboard` is *undefined* outside a secure context and therefore **throws rather than rejecting**, so the guard is try/catch, not `.catch()`. Failure flips the icon to a warning triangle titled *"Copy blocked — the clipboard needs a secure (https) page"*; success flips to `bi-check-lg` for 2s. **Icon SHAPE carries the state, not colour alone.**
  - **⛔ THE GATE IS THE ACTIVE ROLE — `user.role === Roles.Superuser`, not `auth.isSuperuser`.** The shared helper also reads true when Superuser merely appears in the `roles` array; the token decoder already handles an array claim, so the day the server issues one, that helper would light the button for someone **acting as a Director**. **Todd, 08-26: role = SuperUser only, NO ONE ELSE.** Director and SuperDirector are deliberately OUT.
  - ⚠ **Consequence to accept, not solve:** if Ann does this pasting while signed in as a Director on a client job, she will not see the button. That is the ruling, not an oversight.
- **Severity**: 🟢 UX / small — no defect; a daily-friction fix for admin staff
- **Status**: 🟡 **BUILT — Todd, 08-26. Source-only, awaiting deploy.** Filed 08-25 at Ann's request, built the next day.

### AR-032: [Payments / ARB] A Director should NOT be able to enter a Correction against a LIVE ARB subscription — SuperUser only
- **Topic**: registrant fly-in → Add Accounting Record → **Correction** tab, where the registration holds an **active or suspended** ARB plan
- **Request (Ann, 08-25)**: *"On ARB sites, a Director ONLY should NOT have the ability to enter Corrections (SuperUsers should). This was a Legacy function and Directors make this error and think the change will modify the Subscription with ADN which it doesn't."*
- **✅ THE MISUNDERSTANDING IS REAL AND THE CODE CONFIRMS IT.** A Correction writes a **local `RegistrationAccounting` row only**. It does not call Authorize.net, does not touch `ARBUpdateSubscription`, and does not change what the plan will draft. So a Director "correcting" a balance on a live plan changes TSIC's ledger while **ADN keeps drafting the original schedule** — the two silently diverge, and the family keeps being charged the old amount.
- **Current state — there is NO gate of any kind (verified 08-25):**
  - **Frontend**: the Correction tab is rendered unconditionally — `accounting-ledger.component.html:305`, a plain button with no role or ARB condition. The only related input is `allowNegativeCorrection` (`:122`), which governs *sign*, not *permission*.
  - **Backend**: `POST /api/registration-search/{id}/record-payment` (`RegistrationSearchController.cs:357`) sits behind **`[Authorize(Policy = "AdminOnly")]`** at the controller level — which admits **Director, SuperDirector and Superuser alike** — and the handler does not distinguish `Check` from `Correction`, nor look at ARB status at all.
  - **⚠ So hiding the tab is not sufficient.** The endpoint would still accept a Director's correction from a replayed request. **Gate BOTH ends** — this is money, and the UI is not the control.
- **✅ THE PREDICATE ALREADY EXISTS — reuse it, do not invent a second one.** "Active or suspended" is exactly the **live** set already encoded by `ArbSubscriptionStatus.IsLive(id, status)` (built for AR-013, reused by AR-027). Gate on that, not on "is this an ARB site": a job-level notion would wrongly block corrections for the many registrants on an ARB-capable job who have **no plan**, and would miss a dead-plan registrant where a correction is legitimate.
- **📊 Scale (dev, 08-25): 1,892 registrations hold a live plan on jobs still open** — so this is a wide surface, not an edge case. **Team-side is moot today: ZERO teams carry an ARB subscription**, though the club-rep ledger shares the same component and would need the same rule if team ARB is ever used.
- **For Todd — decisions:**
  1. **Hide the tab, or show it disabled with a reason?** House pattern elsewhere (Quick Links toggles) is **disable + explain**, which teaches instead of puzzling. Given the whole item is a *misunderstanding*, a reason like *"A correction does not change the Authorize.net subscription — contact TSIC"* does more good than a missing button.
  2. **Where does SuperDirector land?** Ann said "a Director ONLY should NOT" and "SuperUsers should". **SuperDirector is unstated** — decide deliberately rather than by whichever policy is convenient.
  3. **Does the same rule apply to Check?** Ann named Corrections only. A check payment against a live plan is arguably the same divergence, but she did **not** ask for it — **do not widen without asking.**
  4. **What should a Director do instead?** The item exists because they are trying to solve something. If the answer is "ask TSIC", say so in the disabled reason; if there is a legitimate self-service path, name it.
- **✅ BUILT — Todd, 08-26. Both ends gated; the server is the control.**
  - **Backend (the gate)**: `RegistrationSearchService.RecordCheckOrCorrectionAsync` now takes `callerRole` and refuses a **Correction** when `ArbSubscriptionStatus.IsLive` says the registration's plan can still draft and the caller is not a Superuser. Placed on the shared `regForValidation` load, so it costs **no extra query**. The controller passes `User.FindFirstValue(ClaimTypes.Role)` — the same shape `SetActive` and `DeleteRegistration` already use in that file.
  - **Frontend (the explanation)**: the Correction button is **disabled, not hidden**, with a warning note underneath — *"this registration is on a live Authorize.Net payment plan. A correction changes the TSIC ledger only; it does **not** change what the plan will draft, so the family would keep being charged the original amount. Contact TSIC to change a plan."* Icon + text, never colour alone; `aria-describedby` ties the button to the reason; `:focus-visible` added to `.type-btn` while there.
  - **⚠ ONE rule, computed server-side — do not re-derive "active or suspended" in TypeScript.** `SubscriptionDetailDto` gained a get-only `IsLive => ArbSubscriptionStatus.IsLive(SubscriptionId, Status)`. Get-only was deliberate: it costs **zero construction sites** (the DTO is built in four places) and no caller can set it out of step with `Status`. Regen'd — the TS model carries `isLive?: boolean`.
  - **⛔ DECISION 2 ANSWERED — SUPERUSER ONLY. SuperDirector is LOCKED OUT, by decision, not by convenience.** Ann said *"a Director ONLY should NOT... SuperUsers should"* and left SuperDirector unstated. It is locked out because **the misunderstanding is a director's misunderstanding and a SuperDirector is a director over several jobs — the same belief, wider blast radius**. It also matches the two existing guards in this very service (`DeleteRegistrationAsync`, `SetActiveAsync`), both of which read `AdminOnly` as too wide for a consequential act and narrow to Superuser. And it **fails closed**: wrongly blocking a SuperDirector is a phone call, wrongly admitting one is a ledger that silently disagrees with what the family is charged. **If Todd wants SuperDirector in, it is one added name in the `string.Equals` — but say so deliberately.**
  - **Decision 3 answered as Ann asked — Check is NOT gated.** She named Corrections only, and a check is real money arriving rather than a belief about the plan. Not widened.
  - **Decision 4 answered in the reason text** — *"Contact TSIC to change a plan."* There is no director self-service path for changing an ARB schedule, and the disabled note says so rather than leaving them to guess.
  - **Per-registration, not per-scope — the family ledger was the trap.** ARB mints one subscription **per registration**, so in `app-family-payment` one sibling (or one event of one sibling) can be on a plan while the next is not. `LedgerAddTarget` gained `arbLive`, and the lock follows **the picked target**, so a plan on one event never blocks a legitimate correction on another.
  - **⚠ NAMING TRAP, twice over — `isLive` already meant something else in BOTH consumers.** `family-payment.liveRegIds()` and `registration-detail-panel.subscriptionIsLive()` mean *"a live Authorize.Net READ has succeeded"* — a fetch-state flag, Production-only. They are one word away from the new liveness rule and mean something completely different. The new members are deliberately named `arbLiveRegIds` and `arbPlanIsLive`. **Do not merge them.**
  - **Club-rep ledger untouched, and that is correct today.** It shares `app-accounting-ledger` but submits through `recordCheckForTeam` / `recordCheckForClub`, different endpoints entirely, and **zero teams carry an ARB subscription**. The new inputs default to unlocked, so its behaviour is byte-identical. **If team ARB is ever switched on, those two endpoints need the same gate — it does not come for free.**
  - **Tests**: 13 call sites in `PlayerCheckTests` updated for the new parameter, passing Superuser — they are accounting-math tests, not authorization tests, and no fixture there sets up an ARB plan. **No new tests written** (refactor rule). `dotnet build` clean; `ng build` clean; `verify:no-effect` passes.
- **Severity**: 🟡 Bug-adjacent / money-accuracy guard — no wrong charge is made by the correction itself, but it creates a **silent divergence between TSIC's ledger and what ADN will actually draft**, by a role that believes it is doing the opposite
- **Status**: 🟡 **BUILT — Todd, 08-26. Source-only, awaiting deploy.** Filed 08-25 at Ann's request, built the next day.
  - **HOW TO VERIFY (Ann, locally)**: open a registrant on an ARB job **who holds an active plan**, click **+ Add Accounting Record** → the **Correction** button is greyed out with the reason underneath; **Check and Credit Card still work**. Then open a registrant on the **same job with no plan** (or a canceled/expired one) → **Correction works normally**. As **Superuser**, Correction works in both cases.
  - **⚠ The frontend lock is NOT the test.** The gate is server-side; a passing UI check only proves the courtesy works. **1,892 live-plan registrations on open jobs** are in scope, so a regression here is wide.

### AR-031: [ASL Rosters] Region token is `ASL:New York Capital Region` but the real naming is **NY** — change it to `ASL:NY Capital Region`
- **Topic**: `AslRegionConstants.Regions` → the region token added for AR-018
- **Report (Ann, 08-24)**: *"When new regions were added for ASL, Utah worked great for rosters, however 'New York Capital Region' needs to be replaced with 'NY Capital Region' in that code so LADT converts correctly."*
- **✅ CONFIRMED AGAINST LIVE DATA (Claude, 08-24) — she is right, and the token as written can never match.**
  - Current constant, `AslRegionConstants.cs:42`: **`"ASL:New York Capital Region"`**.
  - **Every real artefact uses NY, not "New York"**:
    - job path — `americanselect-nycapitalregion-2027`
    - job name — `American Select Lacrosse:New York-Capital Region 2027`
    - **team names — `NY-CAPITAL REGION 2032 Team Offer`, `NY-CAPITAL REGION 2031 Team Offer`, `NY-CAPITAL REGION 2029 Tryout`, …**
  - `ResolveRegion` does a `Contains` scan of the token against the team name (`:58-68`). **`ASL:New York Capital Region` is not a substring of any of those**, so a Main Event team named for this region would fall through to the fallback and be labelled with its **entire team name** — exactly the failure mode AR-018 warned about, and the reason Utah worked while this one did not (`ASL:Utah` matches `ASL:Utah Black 2028` cleanly).
  - **This also supersedes the "naming trap" note in AR-018**, which flagged the spaces-vs-hyphen mismatch against the *job* name. The real-world form is **NY**, so the fix is the abbreviation, not the hyphen.
- **THE FIX — one string**: `"ASL:New York Capital Region"` → **`"ASL:NY Capital Region"`**.
  - **Move it in the list while editing.** The list is roughly alphabetical and the entry currently sits between `ASL:New Jersey` and `ASL:North Carolina` (`:41-43`). As `NY` it belongs beside its siblings at `:45-46` — `ASL:NY Downstate`, `ASL:NY Upstate`. Cosmetic, but this file is read by whoever adds the next region.
  - **✅ ORDERING CHECKED — no collision.** Matching is first-match-wins on `Contains`, so a token that is a substring of another must come first. `ASL:NY Downstate` and `ASL:NY Upstate` are **not** substrings of `ASL:NY Capital Region …`, and it is not a substring of them. Safe at any position among the NY entries.
  - **⚠ ASL NAMING CONFIRMATION — DELIBERATELY NOT SOUGHT. Ruling (Todd, 08-24): _"ASL will give us nothing."_ Do not re-propose asking them, and do not re-propose hardening this.**
    - Live `NY-CAPITAL REGION` rows (**hyphen**, uppercase) sit on the *region's own* 2027 job and carry **no `ASL:` prefix**, so they are never matched against this token — the board query is scoped `t.JobId == jobId` (`TeamRepository.cs:1934`). The names that matter are **Main Event 2027**, and per AR-018 those do not exist yet (8 teams entered, all Connecticut, ~127 outstanding). **The evidence above establishes ASL's house style, not the string.**
    - **Accepted risk, and it is bounded — this change CANNOT regress anything.** The literal token is simultaneously the dropdown label *and* the SQL `Contains` argument (`TeamRepository.cs:1944`), so it stays self-consistent: it either matches (grouping works) or it does not (today's behaviour, unchanged). Casing is safe (`OrdinalIgnoreCase`); a hyphen (`ASL:NY-Capital Region`) would still miss.
    - **Symptom if it misses, stated precisely** — the dropdown is `Distinct` over `ResolveRegion` across every team (`AslRostersController.cs:45-49`), so an unresolved region yields **one dropdown entry per team**, each labelled with that team's full name and selecting only itself. The grouping does not degrade, it disintegrates — and nothing errors, which is why it can sit unnoticed.
  - **🛑 BANKED — DO NOT BUILD UNBIDDEN.** A normalize-plus-alias resolver (strip non-alphanumerics, plus `New York`↔`NY` aliases) was written and reverted at Todd's direction: *"the whole roster business is bullshit and will never be applied to another customer."* **If it is ever revived, the trap is that the label and the filter are TWO matchers** — normalizing only `ResolveRegion` makes the label stop matching the SQL `Contains`, turning a fragmented dropdown into an **empty region page**, which is worse than the bug. Both must move together, behind a single `MatchesRegion` rule.
- **Severity**: 🟡 Bug — a region added in AR-018 cannot resolve, so its teams would display under their full team name instead of the region
- **Status**: ✅ **FIXED — Todd, 08-24.** Filed and fixed the same day at Ann's report. One string; `TSIC.Domain` builds clean. **Source-only — awaiting deploy.**
  - **⏸ VERIFICATION DELIBERATELY HELD (Ann, 08-25): "hold on verification for now."** Not a concern about the fix — **there is nothing meaningful to verify yet.** The teams this token must match are **Main Event 2027**, and per AR-018 they **do not exist**: that job holds 8 teams, all Connecticut, with ~127 still to be entered by ASL. Anything testable today exercises regions that already worked.
  - **THE REAL TEST ARRIVES WHEN ASL ENTERS THE MAIN EVENT 2027 TEAMS.** At that point check the ASL roster board's region dropdown for that job. **Pass** = one `NY Capital Region` entry grouping its teams. **Fail** = one dropdown entry *per team*, each labelled with that team's full name and selecting only itself (the dropdown is `Distinct` over `ResolveRegion`, so an unmatched region fragments rather than degrading). **Nothing errors either way**, which is why this must be looked at deliberately rather than waited on.
  - **Most likely cause of a fail: a hyphen.** `ASL:NY-Capital Region` will not match `ASL:NY Capital Region`. Casing is safe (`OrdinalIgnoreCase`); the hyphen is not. If it fails, the cheap fix is renaming the teams, not changing the matcher — see the banked normalize-plus-alias note above and its two-matcher trap.

### AR-030: [Team Registration] Let Directors and Club Reps change a registered team's LOP — put it in the rename popup
- **Topic**: Team Registration → Teams step → registered-teams grid → the rename (pencil) dialog · `Teams.LevelOfPlay`
- **Request (Ann, 08-24)**: *"Directors and Club Reps asking that they are able to change LOP on a registered team. I thought we had this function previously, however not apparent in the first image. Wondering if it makes sense to add it to the popup that allows a team name change (natural location for this function?)"*
- **✅ ANN'S MEMORY IS CORRECT — but only for Directors, and only somewhere else. This is a CLUB REP gap plus a Director *discoverability* gap.**
  - **Directors can already edit LOP in two places**: **LADT → team detail** (a `<select>` bound to `form.levelOfPlay`, `team-detail.component.ts:260`) and **Search Teams → team detail panel** (`team-detail-panel.component.html:92`, `editLevelOfPlay`). Backend writes exist for both — `LadtService.cs:869` (`if (request.LevelOfPlay != null) team.LevelOfPlay = …`) and `TeamSearchService.cs:248-249`.
  - **Club Reps cannot edit it at all.** On the registered-teams grid LOP is **read-only** — `registered-teams-grid.component.ts:73-75`, a plain `<span>` behind `[visible]="showLop()"`. The only edit affordance on that grid is the **rename pencil** (`:50-54`, `renameTeam` output).
  - **So nothing is broken — the function exists, just not on the screen where a rep (or a director working in the registration wizard) is actually looking.** That is why it read as missing in Ann's first image.
- **✅ HER INSTINCT ABOUT THE LOCATION IS WELL-FOUNDED — the rename dialog already solves LOP's hardest problem.**
  - **⛔ SCOPE IS SETTLED — PER JOB (Todd, 08-24).** `Teams.LevelOfPlay`, this event's row, nothing else. `ClubTeams.ClubTeamLevelOfPlay` is not touched and is not a question.
  - **`team-rename-confirm.component.ts` already answers exactly that question for the name** — it renames for this event and offers a *propagate to the club library* checkbox (`:113`, `:165`). **An LOP field dropped into that dialog inherits a solved scope model instead of inventing a second one.** Building LOP editing anywhere else on this screen would mean re-answering event-vs-library from scratch.
  - It also inherits the right **permission gate for free**: the pencil is already driven by the director's *Allow Edit* toggle (`:385-386`), so a director keeps control of whether reps may change anything — which is the correct default for a value that affects placement.
- **For Todd — decisions to make:**
  1. ~~Does the LOP change propagate to the club library?~~ **⛔ RULED — Todd, 08-24: THE ISSUE IS PER JOB. Do not ask this again.** The edit writes `Teams.LevelOfPlay` for **this event only**. No library write, no propagate checkbox, no fan-out — same as [Team rename = THIS-EVENT-ONLY](../../../memory/project_team_rename_ownership_gate.md). **⚠ CORRECTION (08-24): an earlier draft of this entry claimed the dialog has no event→library path. That was WRONG — it has one, a "Rename it in my Club Team Library too" checkbox at event origin (`team-rename-confirm.component.ts:163-172`, `showPropagate():383-389`), and `RenameRegisteredTeamRequest.AlsoRenameLibrary` carries it. It is NAME-ONLY and stays so: the ruling above is a decision, not an absence of plumbing. `ClubTeams.ClubTeamLevelOfPlay` is a seed for future registrations, not a mirror; it is out of scope. **Supports the ruling from the other side**: library grad year and LOP are ALREADY locked once a team is scheduled (`TeamRegistrationService.cs:975-979`, Todd 08-18) — only the label was ever meant to be editable there.
  2. **Free text or a fixed list?** LADT uses a `<select>`; the grid renders through a `formatLop()` helper and `normalizeLop()` exists in the search panel. **Use the same option list as LADT** — two vocabularies for one field is how the values drift.
  3. **Should a director editing here also get it, or only reps?** Ann says both are asking. The dialog serves whoever opens it, so this likely comes free.
  4. **Late changes affect placement.** LOP feeds division/placement decisions, so changing it after registration is not purely cosmetic. Consider whether it stays editable once pools or brackets exist, or whether the director's Allow Edit toggle is sufficient control.
- **✅ BUILT — Todd, 08-24.** LOP field added to the rename dialog on the Registered Teams pencil, event origin only. Backend + frontend build clean; `verify:no-effect` passes. **Source-only — awaiting deploy.** Ann verifies locally.
  - **⚠ A REAL DEFECT WAS IN THE PATH, not just a missing field.** `RenameRegisteredTeamAsync` opened with `if (!eventNameChanged && !alsoRenameLibrary) return;` — and an **LOP-only edit is the commonest use of this dialog**, so the method would have returned there, reported success, and **written nothing**. The guard now includes `lopChanged`. Anyone adding a third editable field to this method must extend that guard too, or inherit the same silent no-op.
  - **LOP is a plain column write**, deliberately NOT through `ITeamRenameService`: that chokepoint exists to carry a *name* onto the WAITLIST twin and the schedule, and LOP appears on neither.
  - **DRY (Todd, 08-24: "lop is used many places")** — no new vocabulary was introduced, and two pre-existing copies were folded in passing: `LevelOfPlayPickerComponent` moved `views/registration/team/components/` → **`shared/teams/`** (a shared dialog must not import from `views/`; 3 importers updated); `formatLop()` was **byte-identical in two components** and is now `shared/teams/lop-choices.ts`; LADT's hand-kept `<option>` 1–5 list now renders from `LOP_CHOICES`. **One vocabulary, four surfaces.** Note `normalizeLop` ≠ `formatLop` and the difference is load-bearing — editors want '' for off-scale junk (forces a real pick), read-only surfaces must never blank a legacy value.
  - **Blank never writes.** The picker has no clear affordance, so an empty draft means "stored value was off-scale and they haven't picked yet" — it is not sent, or a legacy level nobody touched would be erased. Server validates `'1'..'5'`; NULL means unchanged, so every pre-existing caller is unaffected.
  - **Labels move with the act**: title becomes *Edit Team for This Event*, button *Save Level of Play* on an LOP-only edit, and the success toast no longer reads "X is now X in this event."
  - Decisions 2 and 3 answered by the build (LADT's list; the dialog serves whoever opens it, so directors get it free). **Decision 4 — whether LOP stays editable once pools/brackets exist — is NOT addressed; the director's Allow Edit toggle is the only control today.**
- **Severity**: 🟢 Feature / parity-of-access — the capability exists for Directors elsewhere; Club Reps have none, and neither role has it where the work is being done
- **Status**: ✅ **VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 08-25) — CLOSED.** Tested on localhost as club rep `Dwaynewilkins` (club DEWLAX) on `lftc-fallshowcase-2026`, team **BLACK / AG 2028**. LOP set to 5 through the rename dialog; **confirmed in the database, not just on screen** — `Leagues.teams.level_of_play = '5'`, `lebUserID = Dwaynewilkins`, `modified 2026-08-25 06:11:52`. So the grid and the stored value agree; there is no display-vs-data mismatch. Filed and built 08-24, verified 08-25.
  - **⚠ FALSE ALARM WORTH RECORDING — it first appeared not to save, and the cause was a STALE LOCAL API, not this feature.** Ann changed the LOP, got the green toast with correct wording, and the value stayed at 4 in both the Registered Teams grid and the library. Diagnosis from the data: on the retry the row was **not written at all** (`modified` frozen at 05:51 while the clock read 06:04), which the backend only does when it believes the LOP did not change — i.e. **the server never received the value**. Her `ng serve` frontend had picked up Todd's commit on refresh; her local `dotnet` API was still running the pre-pull binary, whose `RenameRegisteredTeamRequest` has no `LevelOfPlay`, so the property was silently discarded and the guard returned early. **Restarting the API fixed it immediately.** This is the standing localhost trap — a stale API serving old code while the frontend looks current. **Suspect it first whenever a backend change appears to do nothing on localhost; nothing was wrong with AR-030.**
  - **Also confirmed by design, not a defect**: the **club library level is unchanged**. Todd's build writes `Teams.LevelOfPlay` for this event only and never touches `ClubTeams.ClubTeamLevelOfPlay` (that seeds future registrations and is locked once a team is scheduled). Ann's initial report included "or in the Library" — that half is intended behaviour.

### AR-029: [Reporting] "Tournament Keys — ALL" exported with no column headings — ✅ FIXED, one line
- **Topic**: `[reporting].[TournamentKeyAttributes-ALL]` → X-Job Report Library Excel export
- **Observation (Ann, 08-24)**: re-ran the report after AR-014 — *"No headings and I don't see tournament start and end dates."* Row 1 blank, row 2 `2026`, row 3+ data across columns A–V with nothing labelled.
- **✅ FIXED — Todd, 08-24. Applied live (staging + prod share one DB).** One line deleted from the proc:
  ```sql
  select convert(varchar, @thisYear)   -- DELETED
  ```
  It sat between the `QA Test:` sheet-name marker and the data, emitting an **unnamed single-column result set**. The Excel exporter took that set as the sheet's column definition, so the sheet got one blank column and the real ~22 column names were discarded. Deleting it restores the headers.
- **The dates were never missing.** `EventStart` / `EventEnd` are columns B and C — added by AR-014 and present the whole time. Ann couldn't identify them because there was no header row to read against. **Both halves of her report were the same defect.**
- **`@thisYear` is still used.** `insert into @listAllowedYears select convert(varchar, @thisYear)` is the legitimate use and is untouched — only the bare `select` was removed.
- **Repo now matches live**: [scripts/12-reporting-tournamentkeys-live-only.sql](../12-reporting-tournamentkeys-live-only.sql) has the line stripped and the change noted in its header.
- **Rule worth keeping** — the SP-Excel contract is documented at `scripts/11-reporting-migrate-sprocs.sql:22-28`: a report proc emits **paired** result sets, `SELECT 'QA Test: <TabName>'` then that sheet's rows, repeated per tab. **Any extra single-column select silently eats the headers.** That is what happened here.
- **⚠ Do NOT close AR-014's outstanding heading question off this item.** Different problem: this was *no headers at all*; AR-014's open question is whether the headers that now appear (`countCR`, `bVIEnabled`, …) should be renamed — which conflicts with Chelsea's build-QA use of the same report. Ask Ann what she uses it for.
- **Severity**: 🔴 Bug — a live report was unreadable; all data was present and correct, just unlabelled
- **Status**: ✅ **VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 08-25) — CLOSED.** She re-ran "Tournament Keys — ALL": header row present, and `EventStart` / `EventEnd` readable in columns B/C — the dates she originally reported missing were only ever unlabelled. Fixed by Todd 08-24, live in staging + prod (stored-proc change, no deploy needed). Filed 08-24 after Ann re-ran the report post-AR-014.
  - **⚠ NOTE THE FIX SITE, because it differs from the filing and it matters for the next occurrence.** The filing recommended fixing the **exporter** (`ReportingService.cs:944-980`), on the grounds that the proc's year select was harmless SQL and the export path held the wrong assumption. Todd fixed the **proc** instead — deleting the stray `select convert(varchar, @thisYear)`. That resolves this report, but **the exporter still behaves the same way**: any future proc written with an extra single-column select before its data will silently lose its headers again. The guard is now a documented contract (`scripts/11-…:22-28` — report procs emit PAIRED result sets, marker then rows), not code. **If this recurs on another report, fix the exporter rather than the proc.**
### AR-028: [Help] No help on the job landing page — ⛔ DECLINED 08-24; the analysis and a ~45-minute outline are banked below
- **Topic**: job landing (`/{jobPath}`) → help launcher
- **Observation (Ann, 08-24)**: *"I just logged in and selected a role and it brought me to the home screen of that job. Shouldn't the help icon show here. It only shows if I click on My Registrations. Not intuitive."*
- **⛔ WILL NOT DO — Todd, 08-24.** *"You'd have to be familiar with quicklinks, smart bulletins, and the current user role — I think this isn't worth the effort."* **Do not re-propose authoring landing-page help, and do not propose declaring the route keys on their own as a down payment.**
- **Why the decline holds up**, recorded so it is not re-argued from scratch:
  1. **The *inventory* version of this page is genuinely unwritable — and would rot.** Describing what is on screen means describing Registration Links (varies by role and by which paths the job has open), Manage rows (per-role: Pay Balance Due, Update CC Info, RegSaver, View Roster), Smart Bulletins (derived from job flags) and per-job callouts. Any such page is wrong for half its readers on the day it ships, and wrong for the rest the first time a job flag changes. **That is the version Todd declined, and declining it is correct.**
  2. **There is no half-measure.** The launcher hides itself when the manifest has no content for the key — deliberately, so the "?" never opens to nothing. So "add the `helpKey`s now, author the content later" ships **literally nothing** to staging or prod. It is content or it is not done; there is no cheap partial win to bank goodwill with.
  3. **The page is largely self-describing.** It is big labelled cards — *Register*, *Pay Balance Due*, *View Roster*. A "?" that opens onto three sentences restating those labels is worse than no "?", because it teaches users that the help icon is not worth clicking.
  4. **The signal is weaker than it looks.** Ann found this as a tester walking surfaces and noticing an inconsistency — **not as a family who was stuck.** No end user has reported being lost here.
- **⚠ The one real argument on the other side, on the record so the decline is honest**: **audience asymmetry.** All 57 authored help folders serve admins, directors and club reps — people who will hunt for an answer and know a manual exists. The landing page is the only significant surface whose readers are *families arriving cold*, the population least likely to self-serve. That case is real. It is simply **not urgent**, and it is not evidenced by anything beyond a tester's observation.
- **Reopen bar — narrow, and it is not what filed this item**: a **real end user** reports being confused or stuck on the landing page. **A tester noticing the "?" is absent is NOT the bar** — that is this item, and it has been answered. Do not reopen on a second observation of the same kind.
- **✅ BANKED — if the bar is ever met this is a ~45-minute job, one file, and nothing below needs re-deriving:**
  1. **Two gaps, both must close for the "?" to appear.** (a) **No `helpKey` on the landing routes** — `HelpContextService` walks the activated route to the leaf and takes the last-defined `helpKey` (`help-context.service.ts:12-28`); the launcher reads it at `help-launcher.component.ts:69`. `:jobPath` → `path: ''` → `LandingRouterComponent` declares none (`app.routes.ts:60-62`), and neither does its `home` alias (`:113-116`) — **same component, two registrations, both need it.** (b) **No content exists** — none of the 57 folders under `public/help/` serves a landing page (no `job-landing`, `home` or `landing`).
  2. Author `public/help/job-landing/overview.html`. The manifest regenerates on `prestart`/`prebuild`, so no manifest edit. **`faq.html` is optional and probably unnecessary** at this size.
  3. **⚠ Write to what the visitor is trying to do — register, pay what I owe, find my roster — never to a fixed inventory of cards.** Those three intents are stable across every role, every job flag and every conditional surface, which is precisely what makes the short version writable when the inventory version is not. **This instruction is the whole reason the job is 45 minutes rather than a day; if it is ignored, decline it again.**
  4. Audience is **families and club reps**, not admins — the plainest voice in the manual.
- **Related, same root**: the `dashboard` route already has `helpKey: 'dashboard'` with content, so admins are served; it is only the non-admin landing that has nothing.
- **Severity**: 🟡 Help coverage gap — no defect; declined on effort-vs-evidence, not on merit
- **Status**: ⛔ **WILL NOT DO — Todd, 08-24.** ✅ **Acknowledged (Ann, 08-25)** — she has seen the decline and accepts it; the banked outline stays for whenever landing-page help is wanted. Filed 08-24 at Ann's observation, declined the same day. ORIGINAL: 🔴 OPEN — for Todd.

### AR-027: [Payments / ARB] "Update CC Info" is offered to families whose plan is dead — a false path. Gate it on liveness, everywhere.
- **Topic**: user menu / Smart Bulletins → **Update CC Info** for ARB registrants · split out of **AR-013** (Ann, 08-24)
- **Found while verifying AR-013 (Ann, 08-24)**: on a YJ Players 2027 player with a **terminated** subscription, the AR-013 fix correctly surfaced *Pay Balance Due* — **but "Update CC Info" is still offered too**, for a family who no longer has a plan. Ann: *"if it's a dead plan, this creates a false path for them. Recommend we hide it in these situations."*
- **✅ DECIDED — HIDE IT (Ann, 08-24).** Not an open question. Keep her framing: it is a **false path** — an action offered to someone that cannot do what they need.
- **Why it matters** (Ann called it "not a game changer", and it isn't — but it is a live dead end for people who owe money):
  1. **Two competing money actions for one debt.** The family sees *Pay Balance Due* and *Update CC Info* with no way to tell which is right; the dunning email names one, the menu offers two.
  2. **The destination can't help them.** `arb-update-cc` loads a subscription to work against; a dead plan likely has nothing to load — and the FAQ already carries an entry for that outcome: *"The page says it couldn't load my subscription."*
  3. **It contradicts the parent-facing help** (now corrected — see below).
- **THE FIX — one line. The rule and the flag already exist**, both shipped with AR-013:
  - `client-header-bar.component.ts:152` — `if (pulse.myAdnSubscriptionId && user.regId)` → change the gate to **`pulse.myHasLiveArbSubscription`**, the same condition line `:139` already uses for Pay Balance Due.
  - **Keep `MyAdnSubscriptionId` in the DTO** — the route still needs the regId link. It is the **gate** that changes, not the field.
- **✅ SWEEP AUDITED 08-24 — this is the ONLY remaining id-gate.** Everything else already moved to liveness with AR-013 (`client-header-bar.component.ts:139`, `:165`; `registration-panel.component.ts:225`). There is exactly one Update CC entry point in the app (`:153`); the `arb-health` occurrences are director-facing **email templates**, not menu gates.
- **⚠ STANDING RULE, per Ann (08-24): *"this needs to be handled everywhere possible, meaning any menu / smart bulletin."*** Treat liveness — not "an id exists" — as the gate for **every** ARB-driven menu item, Smart Bulletin, Manage row or CTA, now and in future. The id means only *this registration once had a plan*. That distinction is the whole of AR-013 and it must not have to be rediscovered a third time.
- **✅ PARENT-FACING HELP ALREADY UPDATED (Claude, 08-24)** — deliberately worded to be true **both before and after** this ships, so no second edit is needed:
  - `public/help/registration-wizard/faq.html` — the "both are the same screen" claim is now scoped to *"while your plan is still running"*; added a callout sending cancelled/ended plans to **Pay Balance Due**; the "Don't see Update CC Info?" warning now gives three causes instead of two.
  - `public/help/arb-update-cc/faq.html` — added a note that the page serves a running plan, and that stopped plans should use **Pay Balance Due**.
  - The wording tells families what to **use** rather than what they won't see, which is correct today (Ann verified Pay Balance Due appears) and stays correct once the false path is hidden.
- **⚠ One risk, on the record**: hiding this means a dead-plan family can no longer touch their stored card. That only bites if a plan can read dead while ADN is still drafting — Todd's 08-23 analysis says no path writes a dead status while billing continues. Judged theoretical, but it is the thing to be wrong about.
- **🔴 A REAL DEFECT WAS FOUND UNDERNEATH THIS ONE (Claude, 08-24) — it is the reason the item shipped as TWO changes, and it is the one that mattered.**
  - **"Update CC Info" is not a card screen. It is the catch-up collections path.** `ArbDefensiveService.UpdateSubscriptionCreditCardAsync:407` runs penny-auth verify → `ARBUpdateSubscription` → **charge the arrears**, and that third step is gated **only on `BalanceDue > 0`**. It does **not** check whether the subscription update succeeded. It posts a real `RegistrationAccounting` row stamped *"Autocharge of previously failed ARB transactions"* (`:475-525`). **This is AR-010's "behind-in-payments catch-up, legacy parity" — it already exists and is live.**
  - **The page also loads fine on a dead plan.** `GetSubscriptionInfoAsync:355` returns null only when the **id** is missing; status is never consulted, and the balance is schedule math, not status. **So the filing's "a dead plan likely has nothing to load" is wrong** — it loads, shows a correct balance, and the submit **collects the money**.
  - **The result screen branched on `subscriptionUpdated` ALONE** (`arb-update-cc.component.html:24-40`), and the amount-charged block lived **inside the success branch**. On a dead plan step 2 always fails ⇒ the family was **charged for real** and shown a red ✗ **"Update Failed"** with **no dollar figure**. The next thing they do is click **Pay Balance Due** — which AR-013 has just made visible to exactly this cohort — and **pay a second time.** Only ADN's duplicate-transaction window stands in the way, and it is not designed for a deliberate retry minutes later.
  - **The charge AMOUNT was never wrong.** Schedule math converges to `fee_total − paid_total` and cannot exceed it. The reporting was wrong, in the direction that invites a second payment.
- **🟡 BUILT (Todd, 08-24) — TWO changes, in priority order:**
  - **R1, the fix — `arb-update-cc.component.html`: three result states instead of two.** New middle branch `@else if (result()!.balanceCharged)` renders **"Payment Received"** with the amount and transaction id, plus a warning that the card could not be stored for future automatic payments. Display only — **no charge logic, no accounting write, no backend touched**; the same money moves either way.
  - **R2, the cleanup — Ann's original ask — `client-header-bar.component.ts:152`:** gate flipped from `pulse.myAdnSubscriptionId` to **`pulse.myHasLiveArbSubscription`**. **R2 was NOT shipped alone**: without R1 the same red screen stays reachable from the emailed deep link, which is un-gated.
  - **Confirmed exactly ONE entry point** in the whole frontend (`arb/update-cc` appears once); `myAdnSubscriptionId` now has no frontend consumer but **stays in the DTO**.
- **📉 This RE-OPENS then RESOLVES the 08-23 "do not re-propose" ruling.** That ruling rested on cohort size — `expired` **4,581** vs canceled/terminated **386** — but cohort size measures *reach, not harm*: a finished plan has no future draft, so nobody in it loses a capability. The cohort the screen actually exists for is a **declined draft**, which ADN calls **`suspended`**, and `ArbSubscriptionStatus.Dead` is `canceled/terminated/expired` **only** — so suspended keeps the row **and** the catch-up charge. Ruling reversed on the evidence, not on the ask.
- **⚠ THE THING TO BE WRONG ABOUT, restated after reading the writers**: R2 leans on `adnSubscriptionStatus`, and that column is refreshed only by (a) our own cancel operations, (b) the admin-clicked per-job *Refresh statuses*, and (c) the nightly sweep — **which walks SETTLED batches only** (`AdnSweepService.cs:428`, fed by `GetSettleBatchList`). **A declined draft never settles, so it never produces a transaction, so the status is never touched.** Dev DB: only **4** rows read `suspended`, against **342** that read `active` while raw schedule math says they are behind — but that raw figure is overstated: **honouring the 48-hour grace the code itself applies (`GetBehindInPaymentFlagsAsync:107-117`), it is 38 on live jobs ($24.5k, 3 of them 2+ drafts down) and 192 on past jobs ($147k)**. Staleness fails the safe way — a stale row reads *more alive*, so the item stays visible — but that asymmetry is the whole reason R1 ships regardless of R2.
- **Severity**: 🔴 Bug (upgraded from 🟡) — a family on a dead plan was charged and told the payment failed. Ann's original UX point rides along as R2.
- **✅ HOW TO VERIFY — written 08-25 at Ann's request; DEPLOYED as of 08-25, so all of this is testable now.**
  - **PART 1 — the menu gate (Ann's original ask). No charge.** Three checks, and the third is the regression risk:
    1. **Dead plan** → *"Update CC Info" must be ABSENT, "Pay Balance Due" PRESENT.* Case: `stepsboys-players-2025-2026`, owed **$280**, `regId 8033190D-3318-4B7A-9C87-E0F82790567F`.
    2. **Live plan behind in payments** → *Update CC Info PRESENT, Pay Balance Due ABSENT.* Ann verified this under AR-013, but **the same flag now drives both rows**, so re-confirm.
    3. **⚠ SUSPENDED MUST STILL SHOW "Update CC Info".** `suspended` is what a **declined draft** looks like and is deliberately **not** in the dead set — that family needs this screen to fix their card. **If the row has vanished for them, the fix went too far.** Case: `stepscalifornia-players-2025-2026`, `regId F1B7C699-805C-4E3B-83AE-187DBA9B90DD`.
  - **PART 2 — the double-charge defect (R1). This is the half that matters and it needs a real submit.**
    - The menu item is now hidden for dead plans, so reach the screen the way a dunned family does — **the un-gated deep link**, which is precisely why R1 had to ship with R2: `/{jobPath}/arb/update-cc/{registrationId}` → e.g. `/stepsboys-players-2025-2026/arb/update-cc/8033190D-3318-4B7A-9C87-E0F82790567F`.
    - Complete the card form and submit. **EXPECT: "Payment Received" showing $280 charged**, with wording that the card could not be stored for future automatic payments. **MUST NOT APPEAR: the old red ✗ "Update Failed" with no dollar figure** — that screen is what sent a charged family on to Pay Balance Due and made them pay twice.
    - Then confirm the money landed: balance drops, Pay Balance Due stops offering, and a `RegistrationAccounting` row appears stamped *"Autocharge of previously failed ARB transactions"*.
  - **💰 SAFE TO CHARGE ON DEV/STAGING — verified in code 08-25.** `HostEnvironmentExtensions.IsSandbox() => !IsLiveProduction()`, and `AdnApiService.cs:42` selects `AuthorizeNet.Environment.SANDBOX` on that basis. **Only live Production reaches real Authorize.net.** No real money moves.
- **Status**: 🟡 **BUILT (Todd, 08-24), DEPLOYED 08-25.** Frontend build clean. Split out of AR-013 on 08-24 at Ann's instruction. **OPEN**: Ann verify — steps above.

### AR-026: [Banner / Mobile] The job banner is illegible on phones — scaled whole into an ~86px strip, with the overlay text landing on the artwork
- **Topic**: `client-banner` widget → custom background banner at handset widths
- **Observation (Ann, 08-23)**: reviewing the Florida Launch Rising banner on a phone, the artwork is unreadable and the overlay text sits on top of it. Her question, which is the right one: **"Do banners really belong on the phone?"**
- **Why it happens (Claude, 08-23)** — three things compounding:
  1. **`.banner-background-only` holds `aspect-ratio: 50/11` at every width** (`client-banner.component.scss:21`). On a ~390px handset that is an **~86px-tall strip**, and `background-size: cover` on a 50:11 image means the **entire 1920-wide design is scaled into it**. Nothing survives — not the wordmark, not the players, not the years.
  2. **The text overlay reserves the left 60%** on mobile (`:111-113`) and is absolutely positioned, so it does not grow the banner — it just lands on the artwork.
  3. **The identity job is already done above it.** The client header already shows the club logo, "Launch Girls Lacrosse" and "Florida Launch Rising 2026". The banner then consumes ~10% of the first screen, directly above the Registration Links card — the thing the visitor actually came for.
- **⚠ Placement in the IMAGE cannot fix this — it's arithmetic.** Ann's artwork is 890px on a 1920 canvas (46%). Even pushed flush right it starts at **53.6%**, which is still inside the 60% the mobile text reserves. **No horizontal position clears it.** Any fix has to be in the component (or in shortening the overlay text).
- **PROPOSED FIX — crop instead of shrink. Written, compiled clean, then REVERTED at Ann's instruction (08-23): "don't do it yet… we can get by with what's there but we'll take a look."** Nothing is committed; the component is untouched. The recipe, ready to re-apply:
  - **`.banner-background-only`** — add a mobile override. `cover` already scales to fill, so a squarer box simply reveals *less width at a usable size*:
    ```scss
    @media (max-width: 768px) { aspect-ratio: 2 / 1; background-position: 62% center; }
    ```
  - **`.banner-text-overlay`** — replace the `left / max-width: 60%` mobile block with a bottom-anchored, full-width band over a scrim, so text stops competing with the artwork for the same horizontal space:
    ```scss
    @media (max-width: 768px) {
      top: auto; bottom: 0; left: 0; right: 0; max-width: none;
      gap: var(--space-1); padding: var(--space-5) var(--space-3) var(--space-3);
      background: linear-gradient(to top, rgba(0,0,0,.78) 0%, rgba(0,0,0,.45) 55%, rgba(0,0,0,0) 100%);
    }
    ```
  - **`.banner-title` / `.banner-subtitle`** — on mobile drop their own `background` / `border` / `box-shadow` (set to `none`, `padding: 0`). The scrim supplies the contrast; two stacked translucent layers read muddy at handset size.
- **⚠ THE TRADE-OFF, stated plainly: this makes the banner TALLER on phones**, roughly **195px** at 390px wide versus ~86px today. It buys legibility with vertical space, it does not save space. **`5 / 2` gives ~156px** if 2:1 feels heavy — one-number change.
- **⚠ AND IT AFFECTS EVERY JOB.** The `62%` focal point is tuned to a banner whose artwork sits right-of-centre. **A job with left-weighted artwork would be framed wrong.** The correct general answer is a **per-job focal point** stored with the banner (Branding tab), with this as the default — worth scoping if this is taken seriously rather than shipping a single hardcoded percentage.
- **The cheaper alternative, not to be forgotten**: shorten the overlay text. At 86px tall the banner can carry one headline and maybe one short line, not four. Ann's current text duplicates the years already printed in the artwork behind it.
- **Severity**: 🔵 UX / mobile — not a defect; a deliberate design decision affecting every job's landing page
- **Status**: ⛔ **CLOSED — WILL NOT CHANGE (Ann, 08-25): "mark closed; will not change this one."** She reviewed the banner on a phone, considered the options, and is keeping the current behaviour. **Not a deferral — a decision.** The full recipe (2:1 crop, 62% focal point, bottom-anchored text over a scrim, panel-title/subtitle cleanup) stays banked in this entry, compiled-clean and reverted, in case the question ever returns — **but do not re-propose it.** The two trade-offs that decided it are on the record above: the fix makes the banner *taller* on phones (~195px vs ~86px), and the 62% focal point suits right-weighted artwork only, so a per-job focal point would be the correct general answer. ORIGINAL: 🔵 REVISIT — parked by Ann 08-23.

### AR-025: [Header] Surface "Switch Role" as a visible badge in the header — same function, same name, just discoverable
- **Topic**: client header bar → user chrome (left of the home / dark-mode icons)
- **Request (Ann, 08-23)**: add a small **"Switch Role"** badge to the header. **Explicitly: the same function as the existing Switch Role and the same naming** — *"just more obvious and easy for end users."*
- **📍 PLACEMENT — Ann marked two candidate spots and asked which is better (08-23).** Her screenshot boxes (a) a slot in the band **above** the app header, and (b) a slot **inline with the home / dark-mode / user-menu cluster**.
  - **Recommend (b), and specifically immediately LEFT OF the user menu ("Todd"), not at the far left of that group.** Rationale: home / dark-mode / user-menu form the session-and-account cluster, and Switch Role is an identity action — it belongs in that family. Sitting adjacent to the name reinforces what it changes (*who you are acting as*), and a user confused about which player they're on is already looking at the name.
  - **Against (a)**: the upper band is a different strip. A control placed there is divorced from the chrome it relates to and reads as page content rather than account chrome.
  - **✅ CONFIRMED (Ann, 08-23): "I like it next to the name as well."** Placement settled — **immediately left of the user menu**, inside the existing control cluster. No further decision needed.
- **Feasibility: trivial. The function already exists — this is purely surfacing it.**
  - `switchRole()` (`client-header-bar.component.ts:318-322`) just closes the menu and navigates to `/{jobPath}/role-selection`. **No new logic, no service, no backend.** The badge calls the same method.
  - Today it is reachable **only from inside the user dropdown** — desktop `client-header-bar.component.html:351`, mobile `:176` — gated on `user()?.regId`.
- **For Todd:**
  1. Add a header button labelled **"Switch Role"** (Ann's wording — keep it verbatim) calling the existing `switchRole()`. Suggested icon `bi-people-fill`, matching the menu entry it mirrors.
  2. **Gate it the same way the menu entry is gated** (`user()?.regId`) unless there's reason to do otherwise — Ann asked for the same function, so same visibility rules is the least surprising choice.
  3. **Keep the dropdown entry as well.** Two routes to one page is fine and deliberate — the badge serves people who scan, the menu serves people who hunt. Removing the menu item would be a change Ann did not ask for.
  4. **Responsive**: that strip is tight on narrow screens. Decide whether the badge collapses to icon-only or drops out below a breakpoint — it must not push the user menu off-screen on mobile.
  5. Design-system tokens only; needs a `:focus-visible` state like every other interactive element in the chrome.
