- **💡 Optional, NOT the request — recorded because it came up and may be worth revisiting later.** Claude suggested the badge instead display the **current role** (`Player ▾` / `Director ▾`) and act as the switch, on the grounds that the header currently shows *who* you are but never *what* you are — the role appears only inside the dropdown as *"Signed in as **Player**"* (`:151`, `:332`). That would address orientation as well as discovery, and would help the multi-player families behind the "I don't see Update CC Info" support call (see AR-013/AR-017 territory — wrong-player confusion). **Ann considered it and chose the literal "Switch Role" badge. Build what she asked for; treat this only as a future option.**
- **Severity**: 🟢 UX / discoverability — no defect
- **✅ RESPONSIVE QUESTION ANSWERED — point 4 above needed no work.** That control cluster is already `d-none d-md-flex` (`client-header-bar.component.html:236`), i.e. **desktop-only**. Below 768px it does not render at all, and the mobile hamburger already carries its own Switch Role entry (`:174`). **Todd, 08-23: "you cannot do this in mobile view (no room up there), restrict to web view" — that is what the existing structure already enforces**, so the badge needed no breakpoint of its own to stay off mobile.
- **Status**: 🟡 **BUILT (Todd, 08-23).** New `.switch-role-btn` placed immediately left of the user menu, after `<app-help-launcher />`, calling the **existing** `switchRole()` — no new logic, no service, no backend. Gated on `user()?.regId`, matching the menu entry exactly. **Dropdown entry kept**, desktop and mobile both. Styled as a labelled pill sibling of `.theme-toggle-btn` (same background/border/hover/active treatment, `bi-people-fill`, design tokens only) with `:focus-visible` and a `prefers-reduced-motion` guard.
  - **One judgement call worth knowing**: between **768px and 992px** the header cluster is tight, so the button drops to **icon-only** there — `aria-label` and `title` still carry the meaning. Above 992px it shows the full "Switch Role" wording Ann asked for verbatim. If Ann would rather see the words at every desktop width, delete the `max-width: 991.98px` block in `client-header-bar.component.scss`.
  - The "show the current role instead" variant remains **not built** — Ann considered and declined it. Still recorded above as a future option only.
  - ✅ **VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 08-24) — CLOSED.** Badge sits immediately left of her name on desktop and lands on role-selection; same wording, same function, dropdown entry unchanged. Tested on a current dev deploy. Desktop-only and the 768–992px icon-only fallback both stand as built — she did not ask for the words at every width, so the `max-width: 991.98px` block stays. ORIGINAL: 🔴 OPEN — filed 08-23 at Ann's request.

### AR-024: [Administrators] SuperUser needs a DIRECT Director registration — creating one via Coach Registration makes us accept waivers on a third party's behalf
- **Topic**: Configure → Administrators → Add Administrator · vs the adult/coach registration wizard
- **Request (Ann, 08-23)**: *"It would be much better to have a direct new Director registration option. Currently going through a Coach Registration is not good."* Her four reasons: a Director shouldn't have to **agree to waivers**, shouldn't have to **add a team**, may be **hard-blocked by a required USA Lacrosse number**, and — the serious one — **"if a SuperUser creates a Coach account, they are signing waivers for a third party."** Recommends a **separate registration under Add Administrator** collecting only **login / demographic / contact** details.
- **✅ ALL FOUR CONFIRMED IN CODE (Claude, 08-23).**
  - **Add Administrator is promote-only — it cannot create an account.** `AdministratorService.AddAdministratorAsync` (`:97-105`) does `FindByNameAsync` and **throws `User '…' not found`** if the person has no account. The modal's own empty-state copy says it outright: *"The person needs a coach registration under their own account — then accept them here"* and *"Register the person as a coach (any event site), then accept them here"* (`admin-form-modal.component.ts:119-155`). **The coach prerequisite is designed in, not a workaround staff invented.**
  - **Waivers** — the adult wizard has a dedicated `waivers-step.component.ts`: *"Read each waiver carefully and check to accept"*, with Accepted / Not Accepted state per waiver.
  - **Team selection is required** — `profile-step.component.ts:45-63`, gated on `state.needsTeamSelection()`: *"Required — select every team you coach."*
  - **USA Lacrosse can hard-block** — the same step renders `isUsLaxField(field)` with a verification panel, and honours `field.validation?.required`. Where a job marks USAL required, a Director with no USAL number cannot complete the form at all.
- **🚨 THE CONSENT PROBLEM IS THE REAL POINT, and it is not merely friction.** The waiver step records **legal acceptance against the registrant's account**, but the person clicking is the SuperUser. So today the only supported route to a new Director has TSIC staff **accepting waivers in another person's name**. That is a consent/compliance issue, not a UX complaint — it should be weighted accordingly when this is prioritised.
- **What a Director actually needs to exist (the minimum the new path must create):**
  1. An **`AspNetUsers` account** (username, password or invite, name, contact details).
  2. A **live `Registration` row granting Director on the job** — `bActive`, and the job unexpired on **`Jobs.ExpiryAdmin`** (admin roles ride the admin expiry lane, `RoleConstants.cs:26-33`).
  - **⚠ Both halves are required. AR-021 proved what happens with only the role**: `PellucidAdmin` holds an admin role with no live registration and gets *"No active registrations on this account"* at role selection. **Creating the user without the registration produces exactly that dead end.**
  - **Explicitly NOT collected**: waivers, team selection, fees/payment, USA Lacrosse number.
- **For Todd — decisions to make while building:**
  1. **Password vs invite** — does the SuperUser set an initial password, or does the new Director get an email invite to set their own? Invite is cleaner for consent (the Director acts for themselves) and avoids staff knowing the credential.
  2. **Which demographic fields are genuinely required** — keep it to what admin contact actually needs; do not import the coach field set by habit.
  3. **Does the same path serve other admin roles** (SuperDirector, RefAssignor, StoreAdmin, STPAdmin) or Director only? A generic "create admin account" would also unblock **AR-021**, which needs a provisioned STPAdmin.
  4. **Keep the existing promote-an-existing-user flow** — this is an *additional* path for people who have no account yet, not a replacement.
- **🔗 History**: **AR-002** surfaced this same seam — *"on Tournament/League there is no UnassignedAdult step at all… Todd to confirm whether the 'register as Coach → become Director' path is expected to exist"* — and was closed 08-19 as resolved with no work taken. **This item is the concrete follow-through on that question**, now with the consent argument attached.
- **Severity**: 🔴 **Compliance + workflow** — the current route requires staff to accept waivers on someone else's behalf, and can be outright blocked by a required USAL number
- **🔴 RE-OPENED BY ANN, 08-24 — NEW EVIDENCE. PLEASE RECONSIDER.** *"There is only one club that doesn't verify USA Lacrosse numbers for coach which is prohibitive. And STEPS is the one. There is a risk of email going to STEPS admin when registration is completed."*
  - **This is no longer just a consent argument — it is CROSS-CUSTOMER DATA DISCLOSURE.** Because a required USA Lacrosse number blocks the coach form elsewhere, the workaround forces the registration onto whichever site does not require one — Ann says that is **STEPS, and only STEPS**. So creating a TSIC Director means creating a coach registration **on a paying customer's job**.
  - **✅ THE EMAIL LEAK IS VERIFIED — code AND data (Claude, 08-24).** `AdultRegistrationService.cs:741-747` applies the job's copy audience on every genuine confirmation: `JobConfirmationCopies.Apply(..., Job.RegForm_from, Job.RegForm_ccs, Job.RegForm_bccs, ..., includeCopies: !isRedelivery)`. Copies are suppressed **only** on redelivery.
  - **✅ STEPS HAS A POPULATED BCC LIST ON EVERY LIVE JOB.** Dev query 08-24: **10+ current STEPS jobs** each carry `RegForm_bccs` holding real STEPS staff addresses (`@stepslacrosse.com`) **plus personal gmail/yahoo mailboxes**. (Not reproduced here — this file is committed.)
  - **Concrete harm**: TSIC staff register a prospective Director as a coach on a STEPS job → **STEPS staff and personal mailboxes receive a confirmation for someone unconnected to STEPS**, carrying that person's name and email — and a junk Staff registration lands on STEPS's roster and approval queue.
  - **⚠ ONE PREMISE UNVERIFIED, stated honestly.** Claude could **not** confirm from dev that STEPS is the *only* club not requiring USAL — no field matching `sportAssnId` / `%lax%` / `%assn%` / `%member%` exists in `forms.RegFormFields` at all, and dev is a stale prod backup, so absence proves nothing. **Treat "only STEPS" as Ann's domain knowledge. The leak mechanism above is independently verified and does not depend on it** — if two clubs skip USAL, the leak is identical, just aimed elsewhere.
  - **📈 SECOND ARGUMENT — VOLUME (Ann, 08-24): *"SuperUser does this, not end user, and with the number of new job builds and requests for this, it needs to be scaleable."*** Two distinct points, and they pull together rather than against each other:
    - **Low-risk surface**: only SuperUsers ever reach this, so the blast radius of building it is small — no end-user-facing behaviour changes.
    - **High-frequency need**: **every new job build can need a Director account**, and the volume of builds and requests makes the manual workaround routine rather than exceptional. **Each repetition re-runs the whole harm** — waivers accepted in someone else's name, and a confirmation mailed to STEPS's staff and personal mailboxes (see the evidence block above). This is not an edge case being tidied up; it is a recurring operational cost that scales with new business.
  - **🎯 ANN'S CONCRETE DESIGN (08-24) — "Recommend adding a field at bottom of popup that says Create New Admin which Superuser then fills out fields."**
    - Target is the **Add Administrator** modal (`admin-form-modal.component.ts`), whose footer sits at `:191-193` (Cancel / Add).
    - **It fits the modal's existing shape.** The flow is already **role-first** — ROLE is chosen, and only then does USERNAME unlock ("Select a role first…") with a Search. A *Create New Admin* affordance below that search inherits the already-chosen role, so the new account is minted **with the right role from the outset** rather than created and then granted.
    - It also slots naturally where the current dead ends live: the modal already tells a SuperUser *"the person needs a coach registration under their own account — then accept them here"* when a search finds nothing. **That message is precisely where "or create them here" belongs** — the failure path becomes the entry point.
    - **⚠ Still must create BOTH records** (unchanged from the original filing): an `AspNetUsers` account **and** a live Director `Registrations` row on an `ExpiryAdmin`-current job. AR-021 is the standing proof that an account with a role but no registration dead-ends at *"No active registrations on this account"*.
  - **⚠ THIS INVALIDATES THE PARKING RATIONALE, which is why it is worth re-reading.** It was parked as *"SuperUser functionality"*, not consumer-facing. **It is consumer-facing**: the recipients are a customer's staff, and the person exposed is an unrelated third party. Re-rank on that, not on the original consent argument.
---

#### 📣 ANSWER FOR ANN — AR-024 (Todd, 08-24): ⛔ WILL NOT DO. **The route you're missing already exists, and it cleans up after itself.**

**Create the Director from a PLAYER or CLUB site — not from a tournament site.** That one change removes every harm in the filing, and needs no new code.

**Why it works** (`AdministratorService.AddAdministratorAsync`, verified in code 08-24):
1. **The pending-coach funnel is cross-job AND cross-customer.** It looks for exactly one active **Unassigned Adult** registration across **every registration on the account — any job, any customer, no expiry gate**. So the coach registration does **not** have to be on the job you're granting Director on, and it does **not** have to be on a customer's job at all. Use any player/club site, including one of our own.
2. **The coach registration is DELETED when the admin is minted — same transaction.** `Add(newAdminRegistration)` and `Remove(pendingCoachRegistration)` go through **one `SaveChangesAsync`** on one scoped context. There is no "junk row left behind on their roster and approval queue": the Unassigned Adult row leaves the coach-approval queue by being deleted, not by being ignored. (It never was a roster row — Unassigned Adult sits in the approval queue; a Staff *team* hat, if one exists, is deliberately left alone.)
3. **It MUST be an Unassigned Adult row, which is exactly why the site type matters.** The funnel matches on `RoleConstants.UnassignedAdult`. A **tournament/league** coach registration mints **Staff** instead (`e6d3bd08`, two-regime direct placement), and Staff **does not qualify** — you'd get *"has no pending coach registration."* **This is the real reason the STEPS route felt like the only one: a tournament site can't feed this funnel at all.**

**What survives of the concern, and how the same fact answers it:** the confirmation email with `Job.RegForm_bccs` does fire at registration time, and deleting the row afterwards does not unsend it. **But since the funnel doesn't care which job the coach row is on, you never need to touch a paying customer's job** — register on a site whose BCC list is empty and no customer's staff ever receives anything.

**The procedure, then:**
1. Person registers as a coach/staff adult, under **their own account**, on a **player or club site** (a site that mints Unassigned Adult) with an empty BCC list.
2. Configure → Administrators → **Add**, choose the role, search their username, Add.
3. The Director registration is minted on **your** job and the coach row is deleted in the same stroke.

**Also worth knowing** (added 08-23, and it narrows the gap further): an account with **no registrations at all** is now a valid third shape and mints a new admin row directly — so a returning admin whose history has aged out needs no coach registration at all. Step 1 is only for a person who has **no TSIC account yet**, since that is what creates the `AspNetUsers` record.

*(The entry below is kept intact as filed; the "junk Staff row" and "must use STEPS" premises are superseded by the three code facts above.)*

---

- **🔴 ANN REJECTS THE PROPOSED ROUTE — DO NOT CLOSE (Ann, 08-25): *"do not close. It is cumbersome. Coach club sites almost all require USA Lax validation and this won't work. Right now STEPS is the only club that doesn't require this and it may in the future. Also, coach registration requires selecting a team and agreeing to waivers that are not relevant."*** Three objections; **two of them are independent of USA Lacrosse entirely and are not answered by the ANSWER FOR ANN block above.**
  1. **The player/club-site route is blocked by the same USAL wall.** Todd's answer turns on registering the coach on a site that mints Unassigned Adult. Ann says **club coach forms almost all require USA Lacrosse validation**, so the wall simply moves — it does not disappear. **STEPS is the only exception she knows of, and she expects that to change.** ⚠ **Claude could NOT verify the distribution**: the requirement lives in `Jobs.AdultProfileMetadataJson` (parsed by `UsLaxMetadataPolicy.RequiresUsLax` — a required field named `sportAssnId`/`uslax`, or whose label contains "lacrosse"), and **exactly ONE job in dev has that column populated at all**. Dev is a stale prod backup; its silence proves nothing either way. **Treat the distribution as Ann's domain knowledge and confirm against PROD before relying on the route.**
  2. **⚠ THE WAIVER PROBLEM SURVIVES UNTOUCHED — and it is the argument Todd already conceded.** His route changes **which site** the coach registration happens on. It does not remove the **waivers step** (`waivers-step.component.ts`), so a SuperUser still **accepts waivers in another person's name**. That is the original filing's core, which he called *"accepted as correct"* on 08-23 and parked on priority. **The 08-24 answer dissolves the email leak and the junk row; it does not dissolve this.**
  3. **Team selection is still required and still irrelevant.** `profile-step.component.ts:45-63` gates on `needsTeamSelection()` — *"Required — select every team you coach."* A Director coaches nothing. The route makes staff pick teams for a person who will never use them.
  - **Net**: the answer resolves 2 of the 4 harms (BCC leak, junk roster row) by relocating the registration, and leaves 2 (waiver consent, irrelevant team selection) plus a dependency on a single club's configuration that Ann expects to change. **Her word for it is "cumbersome", and the volume argument from 08-24 still applies — this is per new job build.**
  - **Question that would settle the route's viability, for whoever picks this up**: run `UsLaxMetadataPolicy.RequiresUsLax` over **production's** `AdultProfileMetadataJson` for live Club and Player jobs. If it is "almost all", the proposed route is a one-club dependency and the decline needs revisiting on that basis alone.
- **🧭 SCENARIO WALKED END-TO-END (Todd + Claude, 08-26) — the route works, and three of the filing's premises do not survive it.** Todd walked the real path: SuperUser on a tournament site → switch role → register the person as a coach on a **player** site → back to the tournament site → Configure → Administrators → Add → Director. It completes.
  - **✅ THE JOB WAIVERS ARE DELETED WITH THE ROW — Ann's objection #2 as filed is wrong on the record-keeping.** `BWaiverSigned1/2/3` (refund policy, release of liability, code of conduct) are written **on the `Registrations` row** (`AdultRegistrationService.cs:1261-1268`), and that is the exact Unassigned Adult row the funnel `Remove`s in the same `SaveChangesAsync`. **No persisted consent artifact survives.** What survives is a SuperUser *clicking through* waiver screens that are then thrown away — ceremony, not compliance. **Do not re-file this as a legal/consent issue.**
  - **⚠ ONE consent artifact DOES survive, and it is a build detail, NOT a reason to build.** `UpdateTosAcceptanceByUserIdAsync(user.Id)` (`AdultRegistrationService.cs:279`) stamps TSIC ToS acceptance **on the user record**, which no deletion touches — so the new Director never gets prompted, on an acceptance they never made. It is **TSIC's own terms and TSIC's own risk** (Todd, 08-26: *"who cares"* — correct). Recorded only because the build spec's banked deviation (do NOT stamp it) is the fix, and this is the evidence for why that deviation exists.
  - **❌ THE VOLUME ARGUMENT IS REFUTED — this is once per PERSON WITHOUT A TSIC ACCOUNT, not once per job build (Todd, 08-26).** New jobs are overwhelmingly **new jobs for existing customers**, and that Director's username already exists. Confirmed in code: **shape 1, lane-pure** (`AdministratorService.cs:143-153`) mints a new Director row on the new job **directly — no coach registration, no waivers, no second site** — because every live registration on the account is already in the admin lane. Ann's *"with the number of new job builds… it needs to be scaleable"* does not hold: the dance runs once, when the human has no account at all.
  - **🆕 UNDOCUMENTED THROW that will bite whoever runs the procedure**: the funnel requires **exactly one** active Unassigned Adult row **across the whole account, any job, any customer** (`:181-185`). A stale coach registration the person left on some other site **years ago blocks the grant** — *"has N pending coach registrations — it is ambiguous which one the grant consumes."* Fix is to delete/deactivate the extras in Search Registrations first. Also throws on: zero pending rows (the tournament-site trap — tournament coach regs mint **Staff**, `e6d3bd08`), already holding the role on this job (use the grid's Active toggle), and `PaidTotal != 0` on the pending row (rare; coach regs are normally fee-free).
  - **🔴 SEPARATE DEFECT FOUND, NOT PART OF THIS ITEM — intent and implementation disagree on the third shape.** Lane purity is computed over **live** registrations only (admin rows ride `Jobs.ExpiryAdmin`), so a **returning Director whose prior jobs have all aged out** has `liveRegistrations.Count == 0` → `isLanePure` false; and the third shape is coded as `allRegistrations.Count == 0` (**empty** footprint), which an aged-out account is not. It falls through to the pending-coach funnel and throws *"has no pending coach registration"* — **so a returning admin must redo the whole dance.** The comment at `:122-123` says the third shape exists precisely for *"returning admins whose history has aged out"*. Candidate one-line change (`liveRegistrations.Count == 0`; the duplicate guards are already vacuous on that set) — **auth path, so it is Todd's call. NOT FILED as its own AR item pending that call.**
  - **The one open question is unchanged and only PROD can answer it**: run `UsLaxMetadataPolicy.RequiresUsLax` over production's `AdultProfileMetadataJson` for live Club and Player jobs. If Ann is right that club/player adult forms almost all require USAL, step 2 eventually has **no site to run on** and the route has no door. Dev cannot answer — exactly one job has that column populated.
- **Status**: 🅿 **PARKED — DEFERRED ON TIMING, NOT DECLINED ON MERIT (Todd, 08-26).** *"There is no issue about building this in the future, it's just that now isn't the time."* **This supersedes the 08-24 ⛔ WILL NOT DO.** The item is agreed as worth building; the working route above is the interim procedure and Todd rules the scenario **extremely rare** (see the volume refutation — once per person with no TSIC account, not per job build). **Do not close it, and do not re-raise it as a compliance or volume issue** — both premises are answered in the walkthrough block above. Re-raise only on: the PROD USAL query coming back *"almost all"* (which removes the interim route entirely), or the rollout queue turning to internal tooling. Previous ruling retained for context: ⛔ **WILL NOT DO (Todd, 08-24)** — re-opened by Ann, reconsidered, declined **on new evidence rather than on priority**: the working route already exists (player/club site → cross-job pending-coach funnel → coach row deleted in the same transaction), so the filing's harm chain does not occur when it is used. See the ANSWER FOR ANN block above; **do not re-raise without a case the funnel cannot serve.** ⚠ One stale claim in the text below: *"a zero-registration account fails both arms of the existing eligibility wall"* — a **third shape** was added 08-23 and mints an admin row directly for an account with no registrations. Previous states kept for context: 🔴 **RE-OPENED (Ann, 08-24)** — evidence block above. Before that: 🅿 **PARKED — Todd, 08-23.** *"At this stage of the rollout we’re addressing errors and consumer-based issues, not our SuperUser functionality."* The consent argument is accepted as correct; it is **not a rollout-stage priority** — nobody outside TSIC staff hits this path, and the workaround (coach registration → accept) works. Do **not** re-raise until the rollout queue turns to internal tooling. **Zero collateral:** AR-021 is explicitly not blocked by this (see AR-021 — `PellucidAdmin`’s account already exists, promote-only serves it). **Everything needed to restart cold is in this entry** — the four confirmations, the two-records requirement (`AspNetUsers` + a live Director `Registrations` row on an `ExpiryAdmin`-current job), the `ClubService.cs:169` precedent to copy, the deliberate deviation (do NOT stamp `UpdateTosAcceptanceByUserIdAsync` — leaving it unstamped makes the new Director accept the TSIC terms himself at first login), and the fact that a zero-registration account fails both arms of the existing eligibility wall so this must be a sibling write path. ORIGINAL: 🔴 OPEN — for Todd. Filed 08-23 at Ann’s request.

### AR-023: [Payments / Payment Ledger] Make the ledger column headings sortable — date, Due, Paid (keep team as the default)
- **Topic**: Payment Ledger (`accounting-ledger` component) → column headings
- **Request (Ann, 08-23)**: make the headings sortable. **Keep the current team grouping as the default — "now by team which is great"** — and add **sort by date**, plus **Due** and **Paid**.
- **Feasibility (Claude, 08-23): small — a few hours. Frontend only, no backend, no DTO.** All four sort values are already on the row: `record.date`, `record.dueAmount`, `record.paidAmount`, and the existing team key.
- **What's there now** (`accounting-ledger.component.html:30-56`, `.ts:148-180`):
  - A **hand-rolled `<table class="ledger-table">`** — *not* a Syncfusion grid, so sorting is not a config flag; it needs sort state + comparators + clickable headers.
  - Columns are **`Transaction | Due | Paid | (actions)`**.
  - Rows render in **two buckets as separate `<tbody>`s** — `activeRecords()` and `otherRecords()` (waitlist / dropped / inactive), split by a separator row.
  - Both buckets are ordered by `byTeam()`: numeric-aware `localeCompare` on the team key, **stable**, so equal teams keep their incoming chronological / `AId` order.
- **Implementation sketch**: a `sortColumn` + `sortDir` signal pair; replace `byTeam()` with a comparator switch (team = existing key; date = `record.date`; due/paid = numeric); make each `<th>` a button carrying `aria-sort` and a direction caret. Default stays `team`/asc so nothing changes until a heading is clicked.
- **~~⚠ THREE DECISIONS BEFORE BUILDING~~ — ALL THREE ANSWERED, see the BUILT block below.**
  1. ~~There is no Date column to click.~~ **✅ DECIDED (Ann, 08-23): add a real Date column.** *"Adding this column won't be a problem, especially if we adjust the width of this flyin upon opening as recommended in a previous item."* The date currently lives inside the Transaction cell (`{{ record.date | date:'M/d/yy' }} · {{ paymentMethod }}`); split it out into its own sortable column.
     - **~~🔗 COUPLED TO AR-016~~ — ORDERING DEPENDENCY DISSOLVED 08-23; the width tension is NOT.** AR-016 shipped on 08-23 with a **percentage-of-viewport opening width** (`[defaultWidthPct]="0.62"`, `maxWidth` 1400), not a measured pixel count — so there is nothing to re-measure when the Date column lands and **AR-023 can be built in either order**. What still stands:
       - **On small screens the extra column makes the no-scroll goal harder, not easier.** Below 768px the panel is already full viewport width — and `.panel-body` sets `overflow-x: hidden` there, so an over-wide table **clips rather than scrolls**, which is worse. Decide whether the Date column collapses back into the Transaction cell below a breakpoint.
       - **The ledger has no measurable minimum width.** `.ledger-table` is `width: 100%` with no `min-width` and no fixed px in its 734 lines of SCSS, and the Transaction cell truncates with an ellipsis. Adding a Date column adds an incompressible `nowrap` cell — that is a real width change and the first one this table will have.
  2. **Do the two buckets stay?** Sorting can apply **within** active and "other" separately (keeps waitlist/dropped from intermingling with live money) or **flatten** them into one list. *Recommendation: keep the split* — but note that sorting by date then yields two chronological runs separated by the divider, not one.
  3. **Sort within team, or replace team?** Today team is the *only* key and ties fall back to chronological. Middle option: **team first, then the chosen column inside each team** — preserves the grouping Ann likes while making dates useful. Alternative: a date sort ignores team entirely and reorders the whole table.
  - **✅ BOTH DELEGATED TO TODD (Ann, 08-23): "Todd can answer those if addressing this."** He owns 2 and 3 outright — no need to come back to her. The recommendations above are defaults, not constraints.
- **⚠ Shared component — lands in three places at once**: the registration detail panel, `family-payment`, and `club-rep-payment` all embed `<app-accounting-ledger>`. One change fixes all three, but **verify all three**, including a club rep with multiple teams (the bucket split and team keying are most visible there).
- **Severity**: 🟢 Feature / usability — no defect
- **🟡 BUILT (Todd, 08-23) — commit `13008804`, PUSHED, not deployed.** Headings are `Date · Transaction · Due · Paid`, each a real `<button>` inside a `<th>` carrying `aria-sort`; the date moved out of the Transaction cell into its own column.
  - **Decision 2 — the two buckets STAY split.** They sort independently and never merge. Flattening would let a dropped or waitlisted registration's charges sit between two live transactions with only a row colour to distinguish them, forcing a director reading a balance to re-derive which rows count. **Expected consequence: a date sort yields two chronological runs separated by the divider, not one.** Documented on `activeRecords` so it is not "fixed" later by someone who reads it as a bug.
  - **Decision 3 — ONE sort key at a time; team is just the default column.** Clicking Date/Due/Paid dissolves the team grouping; clicking **Transaction** restores it (that cell leads with the team/owner line, so it is the team column in everything but name). **The filing's middle option — team always primary, chosen column secondary — was rejected**: on a club rep with eight teams it turns "sort by date" into eight chronological runs to scan, which is precisely what Ann wanted the date sort for. The standard single-key model is also the one every grid user already has.
  - **Nothing moves until a heading is clicked.** Default stays `team`/asc and ties keep incoming order (`Array.sort` is stable), so the opening render is byte-identical to today's.
  - **First-click direction is per-column** (`SORT_DEFAULT_DIR`): dates and amounts open **desc** (newest money, biggest amounts first), team opens **asc**. Clicking the active column flips it.
  - **Null handling**: an unparseable/absent date sorts last in both directions; a null `dueAmount`/`paidAmount` orders as zero — it renders blank and is an *absent* charge, not an unknown one. (Distinct from the AR-011 `?? 0` on the amount **input**; nothing in the comparator writes a value back.)
  - **Narrow widths**: `.col-date` is `display: none` below 768px and the date reappears inline in the Transaction cell where it used to live. It is an incompressible `nowrap` cell, and the fly-in body sets `overflow-x: hidden` there — an over-wide table **clips** rather than scrolls, which would have silently eaten the actions column. The Date heading stays visible and sortable.
- **Verification**: `ng build` clean (95s). `verify:no-effect` OK, `verify:mobile` OK.
- **OPEN — Ann verify, all three hosts**: registration detail panel, `family-payment`, and `club-rep-payment` — **a club rep with multiple teams is the one that matters** (bucket split + team keying are most visible there). Confirm the default order is unchanged on open, then click each heading.
- **Status**: ✅ **VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 08-24) — CLOSED.** Sortable Date · Transaction · Due · Paid confirmed on a current dev deploy, with the Date column split out of the Transaction cell as she decided on 08-23. Both delegated calls stand as Todd ruled them: the active / waitlist-dropped buckets **stay split** and sort independently, and sorting is **one key at a time** (clicking Date/Due/Paid dissolves the team grouping; Transaction restores it) — the "team primary, chosen column secondary" middle option Claude had recommended was **rejected**, correctly: on a multi-team club rep it turns a date sort into N chronological runs to scan, which defeats the ask. Filed 08-23, built 08-23.


### AR-022: [Help system] "Print this screen's help" — printable Help / FAQ / Pro Tips
- **Topic**: help drawer (`?` launcher) → print
- **Request (Ann, 08-23)**: a way to **print the help for the screen you're on** — Help, FAQ, Pro Tips. Scope is **per-screen**, not a whole-manual export.
- **Feasibility (Claude, 08-23): small job. No backend, no content changes, no schema.** Help content is already static HTML rendered into `.help-content`; this is a print stylesheet plus a button.
- **🔑 THE ONE TRAP — collapsed accordions do not print.** Every FAQ answer is a `<details class="faq-item">`, and **not one of the 57 stored `faq.html` files carries an `open` attribute** (checked 08-23). Browsers do not render the contents of a collapsed `<details>`, so a naive Ctrl+P produces **a page of questions with no answers**. Print CSS must force them open:
  ```scss
  @media print { .help-content details.faq-item > *:not(summary) { display: block !important; } }
  ```
  This is not guessable from looking at the screen — it is the single thing most likely to be missed, and the failure is silent (it looks like a clean printout).
- **Second structural issue — the drawer is a fixed overlay with its own scrollbar** (`.detail-panel.help-panel`, the shared slide-out pattern). Printed as-is it yields **one clipped screenful**. Print CSS must set the panel `position: static`, full width, `height: auto`, `overflow: visible`, and hide the app chrome (header, nav, backdrop, tab strip, launcher button, editor controls).
- **Also needed, all in the same stylesheet:**
  - **Force light colours + `print-color-adjust: exact`.** A director printing in dark mode otherwise gets white-on-black, and the callout backgrounds and the yellow highlight silently drop out.
  - **`break-inside: avoid`** on `.faq-item`, `.help-callout`, `.help-card`, `.help-menu-row` so items don't split across pages.
  - **A print header** — screen name + topic — or the printout is unidentifiable once it leaves the desk.
- **TWO TIERS — recommend doing both as one piece:**
  1. **Print the active tab.** Button + the stylesheet above. **~2 hours, mostly testing** (8 palettes, light/dark, a long FAQ that spans pages).
  2. **Print all tabs for the screen as one document** (Help + FAQ + Pro Tips together). The drawer only holds the **active** topic in the DOM and fetches per tab, so this needs the other available topics prefetched into a print-only container. **The plumbing already exists** — `HelpManifestService` knows which topics a screen has, and `HelpService.getContent` already fetches one; the launcher even caches per topic. **Small addition on top of tier 1; ~half a day for both.**
  - Tier 2 is the one a director actually wants — "print this screen's help" reading as one document rather than three separate printouts.
- **NOT in scope**: a whole printable manual across all 57 sections. That is a dedicated route with contents, ordering and section breaks — days, and worth its own conversation. **Do not let it creep in here.**
- **Note**: `pro-tips.html` exists on only **2** screens today (`ladt-editor`, `search-registrations`), so in practice most printouts will be Help + FAQ.
- **Severity**: 🟢 Feature / usability — no defect, frontend only
- **Status**: 🅿 **PARKED — Todd, 08-23.** *"Park this, not essential at this time."* No defect behind it; it loses to the rollout queue. **Do not re-raise** until Ann asks a second time or a director actually asks for paper. Nothing is blocked by this.
  - **Everything needed to restart cold is already in this entry** — the collapsed-`<details>` trap and its one-line CSS fix, the fixed-overlay problem, the dark-mode / `print-color-adjust` requirement, the `break-inside` list, and the tier-1 vs tier-2 split. All re-verified 08-23: **57 `faq.html` files, zero carrying `open`**; `pro-tips.html` on 2 screens; `HelpLauncherComponent` holds one topic in the DOM with a per-topic cache.
  - **Scale — the question Todd was actually asking.** Tier 1 ≈ **40-60 lines** of `@media print` in `help-launcher.component.scss` plus ~20 lines of button/TS calling `window.print()`. Tier 2 adds ~30 lines to prefetch the other topics into a hidden print-only container; the manifest and fetch already exist. **Half a day for both — and most of that is verifying output** across 8 palettes, light/dark, and a page-spanning FAQ. **The code is not the cost.**
  - **⚠ Scope the print rules to `.help-panel`, NOT `.detail-panel`.** That is the shared slide-out class the search fly-ins use (see AR-016) — broad rules would hit every fly-in in the app. Not noted in the original filing.
  - ORIGINAL: 🔴 OPEN — filed 08-23 at Ann's request.

### AR-021: 🟡 BUILT (Todd, 08-23) · [Stay-to-Play] STP Admin login doesn't work — the entire STP admin area was never ported. Paid feature, in live use.

#### ~~🔴 ANN'S REPLY, 08-25 — KEEP OPEN.~~ ⛔ **SUPERSEDED — Todd ruled a HARD NO on 08-26 and closed this to discussion. Kept below for the record only; nothing in it is actionable.** See the FINAL block at the end of this entry. Ann, 08-25: *"The client had the email capability on our site previously, and their current fees to us include this function."*
- **She is holding the item open on the same commercial ground, having now seen the answer below.** The dispute has narrowed, so record what is and is not actually contested:
  - **Not contested**: the *data* deliverable shipped, and it exceeds legacy's.
  - **Not contested**: a **Director** can still batch-email club reps — Search Registrations → Club Rep role → Email All / Email Selected, with editor, test send and Email Log.
  - **⚠ GENUINELY LOST, and this is the crux**: in legacy the email screen was reachable by **STPAdmin as well as Director** (Todd's own 08-23 finding — `STPClubRepsController` carried **no `[Authorize]`**, and the legacy policy was `Superuser, Director, STPAdmin`). **So the vendor could send, and now cannot.** If Top Threat's paid expectation is that the STP vendor conducts outreach from the console, that capability *is* gone — the Director path does not restore it, it relocates it to TSIC's customer.
  - **⚠ ALSO LOST, concretely**: the Director path's tokens are `!PERSON` / `!JOBNAME` / `!JOBLINK` / `!TEAMNAME` / `!EMAIL` — **no `!CLUBNAME`, no `!ZIPCODE`**, which are two of the three Ann specified and the two a housing vendor's mail actually needs. Todd names this as *"a separate and much smaller ask on the Director surface"*. **That smaller ask is the concrete, uncontested deliverable sitting inside this dispute — it could be taken now regardless of how the larger question lands.**
- **THE QUESTION TO SETTLE, and it is commercial rather than technical**: does Top Threat's fee buy *the data* (delivered, and improved) or *the outreach capability* (relocated to their Director, removed from their vendor)? **Todd's position answers the first; Ann's objection is about the second.** Neither party is arguing about code. **Do not re-litigate this in the punchlist — it needs a decision from whoever owns the client relationship.**

#### 📣 ANSWER FOR ANN — AR-021 batch email (Todd, 08-24): ⛔ **WILL NOT DO. This applies to Stay-to-Play ONLY.**

**Todd, verbatim:** *"I will not be providing email services to a third party I have no contract with. This is purely a data transfer issue."* And, so there is no room to read it wider: *"I need to be ABSOLUTELY clear on this — I am speaking with regards to Stay to Play (STP) ONLY."*

**Read that as two separate statements, because both matter:**
1. ⛔ **No email-sending facility will be built inside the STP console.** Pellucid is a third party TSIC holds **no contract with**. Stay-to-Play is a **data hand-off**: the vendor receives the club-rep list per job, on screen and as an Excel export, and conducts its own outreach on its own systems, under its own contract with the client.
2. ✅ **This is NOT a position on batch email generally.** Nothing about any other email surface changes. Directors keep every sending capability they have today. The refusal is specific to standing up outreach machinery on behalf of an un-contracted vendor.

**Answering the fee argument head-on, since that was your ground and it deserves a direct answer** — *"this is the function the client has used in the past and we are charging them increased fees for"*:
- **The fee buys the data, and the data is being delivered.** Top Threat's Stay-to-Play deliverable is the club-rep roster per job — club, zip, active/waitlisted/dropped team counts, rep name and contact — on screen and in Excel. That is what shipped in `e5d4db3c`, and it is more than legacy's screen gave, which had **no `[Authorize]` attribute at all** (`STPClubRepsController` — the club-rep roster and its email endpoint were effectively anonymous in legacy).
- **The paying client has not lost the ability to email its own club reps.** Verified in code 08-24: **Search Registrations → filter to the Club Rep role → Email All / Email Selected**, with an email editor, test send, and the send recorded in **Email Log** like every other send. Top Threat's Director can do this today, unchanged.
- **One honest gap in that path:** its token set carries `!PERSON` / `!JOBNAME` / `!JOBLINK` / `!TEAMNAME` / `!EMAIL` — **not** `!CLUBNAME` or `!ZIPCODE`. If a Director genuinely needs those two in a club-rep mailing, **that is a separate and much smaller ask on the Director surface**, and it does not run into this ruling at all — TSIC emailing on behalf of its own contracted customer is the ordinary case.

**So the practical answer for Top Threat:** the STP vendor pulls the list; the Director does any TSIC-side mailing from Search Registrations. Nothing is withheld from the customer.

**Filing note:** the 08-23 *"solely a data transfer"* framing **stands** and is now reinforced by the contract ground. Do not re-raise batch-email-in-STP without a **contract** between TSIC and the vendor — that, not the fee, is the condition that would change the answer.
- **⛔ BLOCKED ON ONE PREREQUISITE — dev state checked directly, 08-24 (Claude):**
  | Prerequisite | State on dev |
  |:--|:--|
  | Code shipped | ✅ `e5d4db3c6` is in master, so it is on a current dev deploy |
  | `PellucidAdmin` holds an STPAdmin registration | ✅ **Nine**, all Top Threat jobs — **five with a live user window**: `topthreat-knockout-2026`, `topthreat-fivestar-2026`, `topthreat-platinumgames-2026`, `topthreat-floridawish-2026`, `topthreat-tasteofthesouth-2027` |
  | Nav rows seeded for the role | ❌ **ZERO rows in `nav.Nav` for `CE2CB370-…`** |
  - **⚠ TODD: `scripts\5) Re-Set Nav System.sql` has NOT been run on dev.** It does contain the seeding (6 references — the `@StpAdmin` declaration, the role-level `nav.Nav` insert, and the section-10 leaf), but nothing has been applied. **Until it is, `PellucidAdmin` signs in to an empty menu and it will look exactly like the original failure** — do not let that be mistaken for the build not working.
- **⛔ SCOPE ADDED BY ANN 08-24 — BATCH EMAIL — DECLINED (Todd, 08-24). See the ANSWER FOR ANN above.** The request and its full spec are kept below **for the record only**; nothing here is a build instruction. **Ground for the decline is NEW, not the 08-23 principle restated**: *"I will not be providing email services to a third party I have no contract with — this is purely a data transfer issue"*, and Todd flagged it **STP-ONLY**, not a position on batch email anywhere else. The fee argument is answered directly in the ANSWER block (the fee buys the data, which shipped; and the paying Director can still batch-email club reps from Search Registrations, logged in Email Log). **Reopen condition: a contract between TSIC and the STP vendor — not a fee.**
  - **Ann, having logged in and used the screen**: *"I can login, see the jobs, and see the Club Reps per job. What is needed is the ability to email all or some of these club reps with an email editor (similar to ARB Behind in Payments email, for example). The email text should be brought forward from legacy and autofill and tokens that need to be included are: person's name, club name, and zip code. When sent, these emails should show under Email Log like other emails from the site."*
  - **💰 COMMERCIAL GROUND FOR THE REVERSAL (Ann, 08-24) — this is the decisive point, not a preference:** *"this is the function the client has used in the past and we are charging them increased fees for."* **The client used batch email in legacy, and TSIC is charging them INCREASED FEES for Stay-to-Play.** So its absence is not a scope trim — it is **a paid, previously-delivered capability withdrawn while the price went up**. That reframes the whole item: AR-021 was already flagged as a paid feature never ported; this makes the gap larger than "one screen missing a button".
  - **⚠ THE RULING THIS WOULD HAVE OVERTURNED — IT STANDS (Todd, 08-23, reaffirmed 08-24)**: legacy's batch-email half was dropped on purpose — *"Stay-to-Play should SOLELY BE A DATA TRANSFER to a third party. I am not willing to do their work for them."* Reaffirmed 08-24 on the stronger contract ground. **The "data transfer only" framing is NOT retired — it is now load-bearing.**
  - **✅ THE LEGACY IMPLEMENTATION SURVIVES AND IS THE SPEC** — `reference/.../Controllers/STP/Admin/STPClubRepsController.cs`:
    - **Tokens (`:64-76`), exactly the three Ann named plus two more**: `!CLUBREPNAME` · `!CLUBNAME` · `!ZIPCODE` · `!JOBLINK` · `!JOBNAME`, plus a template picker token `!EMAILTEMPLATE-STP-CLUBREPS`.
    - **Substitution (`:296-327`)**: each value is wrapped in `<strong>`; `!JOBLINK` expands to a full *"Please click here then:"* anchor at `teamsportsinfo.com/{jobPath}`, not a bare URL.
    - **Default subject (`:99-101`)**: `Stay to Play: {JobName}` — that is the autofill Ann is asking to carry forward.
    - **Send loop (`:159-224`)**: one message per rep, `To` = the rep's own address (validated), `From`/`Reply-To` = the sending admin, sender name = the job display name. **Not a single blast with everyone in one header** — worth preserving, since a shared To/CC would leak club reps' addresses to each other.
  - **Build against the ARB Behind-in-Payments editor, as Ann asked** — `arb-health.component.ts` already composes `EmailBodyEditorComponent` + `TestSendButtonComponent` + `TsicDialogComponent`, with per-template subject/body autofill and `!TOKEN` substitution. **Reuse that pattern rather than inventing a second composer.**
  - **⚠ EMAIL LOG IS A HARD REQUIREMENT, and it is the part most easily missed.** Sends must land in `Jobs.emailLogs` so they appear under Email Log with everything else (`EmailLogController`, `IEmailLogRepository`). **Legacy sent via MimeKit directly and did NOT write the new system's log** — so a faithful port would silently skip it. Route the send through the standard email service so logging, failure capture and Email Log visibility come for free.
  - **Selection**: *"email all or some"* — the grid must support choosing a subset, not just send-to-everyone. Mirror the Search Registrations pattern (Email All vs Email Selected, selection accumulating across pages).
  - **⚠ Do NOT reintroduce the rep's home address.** It stays dropped per Todd's 08-23 ruling; `!ZIPCODE` is the only location token, which is exactly what a housing vendor needs.
- **✅ HOW TO VERIFY, once the nav script has been run (Ann, 08-24 — she asked for this):**
  1. **Sign in as `PellucidAdmin`** (get the password from Todd — and change it; legacy's add path set it equal to the username, which is where it came from). Pick one of the five live-window jobs above; `topthreat-knockout-2026` is the cleanest.
  2. **The role picker must offer STPAdmin at all.** That alone clears two of the three original gates — a registration exists *and* the role is now offered by the login lookup. If the picker is still empty, the nav script did not take.
  3. **A Stay-to-Play nav item must appear**, and open the **club reps grid** (`GET /api/stp/club-reps`).
  4. **Check the grid content** — it shows one row per club rep with: Id, **Club**, **Zip**, **Active / Waitlisted / Dropped team counts**, and the rep's name/contact. **Confirm the team counts look right for the job**, since that is the whole value of the export to a housing vendor.
  5. **Use the Excel toolbar button** — the point of the screen is a data hand-off, so the download is part of the feature, not a nicety.
  6. **⚠ NEGATIVE TEST, the important one: the rep's HOME street address / city / state must NOT appear** — Todd deliberately dropped it (a housing vendor needs to reach the rep and know where the club travels *from*, not where they live). Zip is present by design; a street address is not.
  7. **Scope test**: the grid must show only the club reps of the job she selected. It is job-scoped off the caller's own registration, so signing in against a different Top Threat job must change the list.
  8. **⚠ Do NOT expect batch email.** Legacy's email half was deliberately not ported (Todd: *"Stay-to-Play should SOLELY BE A DATA TRANSFER to a third party. I am not willing to do their work for them"*). Its absence is the design, not a gap.
  - **Access note**: the endpoint sits behind a narrow `CanViewStpClubReps` policy, **never `AdminOnly`** — STPAdmin is a third-party vendor login. Legacy's policy also admitted Superuser and Director, so a Director seeing this screen is correct, not a leak.
- **Topic**: Stay-to-Play → STP Admin login (`STPAdmin` role)

- **🟡 BUILT 08-23 — commit `e5d4db3c`. Not pushed, not deployed. Two steps before Ann can test:**
  1. **Run `scripts\5) Re-Set Nav System.sql`** — seeds the STPAdmin `nav.Nav` row and its single leaf. Without it the role logs in to an empty menu.
  2. **Give `PellucidAdmin` a live STPAdmin registration** on a job whose **`ExpiryUsers`** is in the future (the filing said `ExpiryAdmin`; the picker actually rides `ExpiryUsers`, matching StoreAdmin / RefAssignor / ApiAuthorized — a vendor's access should not outlive the season). **And change that password.**

- **📉 THE SCOPE SHRANK ONCE LEGACY WAS ACTUALLY READ.** The filing said "rebuild the three legacy screens." Only **one** of the three carried function:
  - **STPDashboard** — `Index()` calls `GetRegKeys` then `return View()`. The view is a purple card whose body is the literal text **"dashboard body here"**, under a "Sales Rollup Pivot Table" header. It is also **unreachable**: `_STPAdminMenu` is rendered only by the dashboard's own view, and nothing in `TSICHeader` links to `STPDashboard`. Dead in legacy, not merely unported. **Nothing to port.**
  - **STPAdminAdd** — a 6-column jqGrid CRUD over STPAdmin registrations. The Administrators page already grants the role, and legacy's add path does **`CreateAsync(user, password: Username)`** with hardcoded `Gender="F"` and `Dob=1980-01-01`. **That is where `PellucidAdmin`'s password came from.** Porting it would carry the anti-pattern forward. **Not ported.**
  - **STPClubReps** — the real screen. Ported.

- **⚠️ TWO THINGS THE FILING GOT WRONG.**
  - It said all three controllers are `[Authorize(Policy = "STPAdmin")]`. **`STPClubRepsController` has no `[Authorize]` attribute at all**, and legacy `Startup.cs` sets no fallback policy — the club-rep roster and the batch-email endpoint were effectively anonymous. Not carried forward.
  - Legacy's `"STPAdmin"` policy was `Superuser, Director, STPAdmin` (`Startup.cs:131`). **Directors always had this.** The affected population was never one stranded account.

- **🔑 ANN'S FAILURE WAS THREE MISSING THINGS, NOT ONE.** The filing found the registration. Two more gates were also empty, and they are keyed differently — the registration decides which role you can *hold*; the menu is keyed by **role**, and the endpoint by **role + job**:
  1. **No registration** — the reported symptom, *"No active registrations on this account."*
  2. **No menu** — `nav.Nav` had **zero rows** for `STPAdmin` (verified against the dev DB; `ApiAuthorized` has NavId 117 with one leaf).
  3. **Role never offered at login** — `RoleLookupService` listed Superuser…ApiAuthorized but not STPAdmin.
  **So handing her a registration as a quick fix would have produced the same failure on retest**, with an empty role picker instead of an error message.

- **What was built**:
  - `GET /api/stp/club-reps` behind a new **NARROW** policy `CanViewStpClubReps` (Superuser, Director, SuperDirector, STPAdmin) — deliberately never `AdminOnly`, same reasoning as `CanRunThirdPartyExport`: STPAdmin is a third-party vendor. Job scope comes from the caller's registration, **never a route parameter**.
  - `GetStpAdminRegistrationsAsync` + `RoleLookupService` entry (role name string is the **DB** name — the JWT role claim is minted from it).
  - Nav leaf in `5) Re-Set Nav System.ps1`, beside ApiAuthorized's, so a re-seed reproduces it.
  - Angular screen at `stp/club-reps`: ej2 grid + its own **Excel Export** toolbar button; `STPAdmin` registered in `Roles`, `NAV_SERVED_ROLES`, and the display-override map (shows as *"Stay-to-Play"* at role selection).
  - Team counts bucket on agegroup name via `AgegroupConstants`, not the raw `Contains("WAITLIST")` strings legacy used.

- **✂️ DELIBERATELY DROPPED — Todd rulings 08-23, do not re-raise**:
  - **The batch-email half.** *"Stay-to-Play should SOLELY BE A DATA TRANSFER to a third party. I am not willing to do their work for them."* It does not move to the Director side either — directors already have email compose.
  - **The rep's home street address, city and state.** The existing `reporting.JobClubRepContacts` export carries them; a housing vendor needs to **reach** the rep (email, cell) and know **where the club travels from** (zip), not where they live. Same class as AR-020's director address going to Vertical Insure.
  - Legacy's `!EMAILTEMPLATE-STP-CLUBREPS` token was in the dropdown list with **no case in `ReplaceSubstitutionVariables`** — inert — and the substitution-variable picker was populated into `ViewBag` but **never rendered on the page**. Both moot now.

- **Rulings that shaped the design (do not re-litigate)**:
  - **`STPAdmin` stays its own role.** An event can have both an STP vendor and a SportsRecruits-style vendor, *"in fact, likely"* — and the 3rd-Party Data Access console is 0/1 login per job, so one login cannot be two vendors.
  - **A component, not a report.** First pass proposed the ApiAuthorized shape (report + entitlement row + nav leaf). Todd: *"why not self-contained in component?"* — correct. The vendor's registration already scopes them to one job, so the `reporting.JobReports` row was a second gate on something the login already enforces. Dropping it removed a whole piece of work.
  - **Grant stays on the Administrators page**, not the 3rd-Party console. The console's minors-data framing doesn't transfer — club-rep data is adults — and the per-job Director is the one with the STP relationship.
  - **Not blocked by AR-024.** That item cites AR-021 as needing a provisioned STPAdmin, but `PellucidAdmin`'s account already exists; promote-only is fine here. Only a *future* vendor with no account at all would need AR-024's create path.

- **Report (Ann, 08-22)**: STP Admin login doesn't work — account **`PellucidAdmin`**. **"Stay-to-Play is something Top Threat Tournaments has paid for, so this is a currently used function in the system."**
  - 🔒 **Credential deliberately NOT recorded here.** Ann supplied the password pattern in chat; it is **not** written into this file because the punchlist is committed to git. Whoever tests should get it from her directly. **If that account's password really is trivially derived from its username, change it** — it is an admin-role account on a paid customer feature.
- **✅ ROOT CAUSE: this is not a broken password. The STP admin function does not exist in the new app.**
  - **Legacy had a whole STP admin area** — `reference/.../Controllers/STP/Admin/` contains **`STPDashboardController`**, **`STPClubRepsController`** and **`STPAdminAddController`**, each `[Authorize(Policy = "STPAdmin")]`.
  - **The new app has none of it**: no STP controller, **no route** (nothing matching stp/stay in `app.routes.ts`), **no component** (no view folder), and no nav entry.
  - **The role exists but leads nowhere.** `RoleConstants.cs:21` defines `StpAdmin = CE2CB370-5880-4624-A43E-048379C64331`, name `"STPAdmin"` (`:57`), and it **is** counted in `AdminRoleIds` (`:33`). The Administrators screen can **grant** it (`administrators.component.ts:195`, `admin-form-modal.component.ts:241`) — so an admin can hand out a role that opens nothing.
  - **The frontend doesn't know the role at all.** `roles.constants.ts` `Roles` has no `StpAdmin` entry, and **`NAV_SERVED_ROLES` excludes it** — so even where authentication succeeds, the nav system serves that role no menu.
- **✅ SYMPTOM CONFIRMED (Ann, 08-22): "it simply said I had no registrations."** She adds she never suspected the password.
  - **The credentials are accepted.** That message is **"No active registrations on this account"**, rendered by the **role-selection** screen (`role-selection.component.html:106-110`) when `registrations().length === 0` (`role-selection.component.ts:35-40`). So **phase 1 of the two-phase login succeeded** and phase 2 had no role to offer. This is not an authentication failure.
  - **Why the list is empty**: STPAdmin sits in `AdminRoleIds` (`RoleConstants.cs:33`), so under the AM-004 lane model (`:26-31`) it only counts from a **live** registration — `bActive` true **and** the job unexpired on **`Jobs.ExpiryAdmin`** (admin roles ride the admin expiry, not `ExpiryUsers`). So `PellucidAdmin` either has no STPAdmin registration row, has one that is inactive, or has one on a job whose **admin expiry has passed**.
- **🔑 BOTH THINGS ARE TRUE, AND THE PORT IS THE REAL WORK.** Granting `PellucidAdmin` a live registration would clear the message and then land her **on nothing** — there is no STP route, screen or menu to reach. **Ann's read is correct: the rebuild is what's needed.** The registration gap is not the bug; it is a **prerequisite for testing the rebuild** once it exists.
- **For Todd**:
  1. **This is a feature port, not a bug fix.** Rebuild the three legacy surfaces — **STP Dashboard**, **STP Club Reps**, **STP Admin Add** — plus a nav entry and registration of `STPAdmin` in the frontend `Roles` map and `NAV_SERVED_ROLES` (it is absent from both today, so the role would get no menu even once screens exist).
  2. **Scope it against the legacy controllers** in `reference/.../Controllers/STP/Admin/` — they are the specification, and all three are `[Authorize(Policy = "STPAdmin")]`.
  3. **Before testing, provision the account**: `PellucidAdmin` needs a live STPAdmin registration on a job whose **`ExpiryAdmin`** is in the future, or the role picker stays empty regardless of what has been built.
  4. **Decide the commercial exposure**: Top Threat Tournaments has **paid** for this. If they need it before it can be ported, that is a conversation to have early rather than after they try to use it.
  4. Note the non-admin side **does** partially exist: `Jobs.BenableStp` → `JobPulse.EnableStayToPlay` (`JobRepository.cs:674`) surfaces a **Stay-to-Play** item in the user menu that routes to `store` (`client-header-bar.component.ts:181-182`). So the customer-facing entry point ships while the admin side behind it does not — confirm what that link actually reaches today.
- **Severity**: 🔴🔴 **HIGH — a paid, in-use customer feature with no admin surface in the new system.** Not a defect in ported code; an unported area discovered late.
- **⛔⛔ BATCH EMAIL FOR `STPAdmin` — FINAL, CLOSED TO DISCUSSION (Todd, 08-26): *"I will not respond further to this, I have a hard no."*** This ends the 08-24 → 08-25 exchange. **The STP vendor does not get to send mail through TSIC's infrastructure — not on a fee, not on legacy precedent, not on "they had it before."** The commercial argument was heard twice and rejected twice; **it is not to be re-raised, re-framed, or re-costed by Ann, by Todd's own future sessions, or by anyone picking this up cold.** ⚠ **The 08-24 "reopen condition = a contract with the vendor" is Todd's own to invoke if he ever chooses — it is NOT an invitation and NOT a workflow anyone else may start.** Rationale, recorded once so it never has to be re-argued: sends leave under **TSIC's SES identity and deliverability reputation** and land in the job's Email Log, so this is not a UI permission — it is lending TSIC's sending reputation to a party TSIC has no contract with. STP is **a data transfer**; the data shipped and exceeds legacy's. **Do not re-open this half of AR-021.**
  - **Unaffected and still live** (do not let the hard no swallow them): the **nav re-seed** and **`PellucidAdmin`'s password** below are operational, not commercial. The **`!CLUBNAME` / `!ZIPCODE` tokens on the DIRECTOR email surface** are a separate, uncontested, much smaller ask on **TSIC's own customer's** surface — **unruled, not declined**, and untouched by this ruling.
- **Status**: 🔴 OPEN — for Todd. Filed 08-22. **Promotion candidate — a paying customer's feature being absent is not after-release material.** ⛔ **The batch-email half is FINAL/CLOSED as of 08-26 — see the block directly above.** What remains open is operational only: run `scripts\5) Re-Set Nav System.sql` on dev (STPAdmin has 9 registrations but **zero `nav.Nav` rows**, so Ann sees an empty menu identical to the original failure), and change `PellucidAdmin`'s password (legacy set it equal to the username).

### AR-020: 🟡 FIXED (Todd, 08-22) — awaiting Ann verify · [RegSaver / Team Insurance] Vertical Insure is sent the DIRECTOR'S PERSONAL ADDRESS as the event location — PII disclosure + wrong weather-payout basis
- **Topic**: Team RegSaver (Vertical Insure) → team-registration policy → `job_event.address`
- **Concern (Ann, 08-22)** — *"Major concern from a policy standpoint."* VI writes a policy against the **actual location of the event**, and **a bank of addresses was created for exactly this purpose**. Currently it appears to send a **Director's** address instead. Two harms: **(1)** it discloses personal information to VI that was never authorized for release; **(2)** it is simply wrong — a Director can live in another state, and **VI determines weather-related payouts from the event location**. *"This should be the same as it was previously."*
- **✅ CONFIRMED IN CODE — Ann is right on both counts.** `VerticalInsureServiceTeamExtensions.cs:340-355` builds the event block as:
  ```
  job_event = new VIEventDto {
      name     = jobName ?? contextName,
      type     = "Tournament",
      location = director?.OrgName ?? string.Empty,
      // "Director runs the event, so the event address is the director's
      //  address from AspNetUsers. Empty strings here cause VI's team-
      //  registration endpoint to 400 with 'Invalid zip code'."
      address  = new VIAddress {
          city   = director?.City,   state  = director?.State,
          zip    = director?.PostalCode, street = director?.StreetAddress }}
  ```
  The **director's `AspNetUsers` record** — street, city, state, postal code — is transmitted to a third-party insurer as the **event address**. The comment shows this was a **deliberate workaround for a VI 400 ("Invalid zip code")**, not an oversight, which matters: whoever fixes it must also handle the empty case that motivated it.
- **✅ LEGACY BEHAVIOUR RECOVERED — and it is exactly what Ann described.** `reference/.../IRegistrationService.cs:1022-1032` sourced the event address from the **field/venue bank**, not from any person:
  ```
  var eventKeys = await _context.FieldsLeagueSeason
      .Where(fls => fls.LeagueId == leagueId && fls.Season == regKeys.Season)
      .Select(fls => new { fls.Field.Address, fls.Field.City, fls.Field.State, fls.Field.Zip })
      .FirstOrDefaultAsync();
  ```
  then `street/city/state/zip = eventKeys.*` (`:1515-1528`). **"The bank of addresses" = the `Fields` table** (`Fields.cs` — Address, City, State, Zip, plus lat/long), joined to the job through **`FieldsLeagueSeason` on LeagueId + Season**.
- **🔑 AND LEGACY FAILED LOUD RATHER THAN SUBSTITUTING** (`:1037-1040`):
  > `if (eventKeys == null || String.IsNullOrEmpty(eventKeys.Address)) return "At least one field must be attached to event -- used for event location...";`
  **That is the answer to the 400 the current comment is working around.** Legacy's contract was *no field ⇒ no policy, tell the user to attach a field* — never *substitute a person's home address*. Restoring the field lookup **without** restoring this guard just moves the failure.
- **TWO MORE DRIFTS in the same block — fix in the same pass:**
  | Field | Legacy | Current | Effect |
  |:--|:--|:--|:--|
  | `job_event.location` | `eventKeys.State` | `director?.OrgName` | VI's "location" now carries an org name, not a place |
  | `job_event.type` | `"team-registration"` | `"Tournament"` | product-type string no longer matches what legacy sent VI |
- **For Todd**:
  1. Source `job_event.address` from `FieldsLeagueSeason → Field` (LeagueId + Season), matching legacy exactly. **Stop sending any `director.*` address field to VI.**
  2. Restore the **fail-loud guard** when no field is attached, with a message that tells the director to attach a field. Do **not** fall back to any personal record.
  3. Realign `location` → event state and `type` → `"team-registration"`.
  4. **Check the player path too**: `VerticalInsureService.cs:343-375` sends director **email, first/last name, phone, org name** as `Organization`, though `street` there is `string.Empty` (`:352`). Business contact details are defensible where a home address is not — **but confirm that is intended and authorized**, since it is the same disclosure question one product over.
  5. **Ask whether any already-issued team policies carry a director address**, and whether VI must be notified / policies re-issued. A policy written against the wrong state is a payout dispute waiting to happen, not just a data-quality nit.
- **Severity**: 🔴🔴 **HIGH — dual harm.** (a) **Personal data disclosed to a third party without authorization**, and (b) **weather payouts adjudicated against the wrong geography**, which can void or misprice a claim a club has paid for. **Promotion candidate — this is not "after release" material; it is live behaviour with a real counterparty.**
- **Status**: 🟡 **FIXED (Todd, 08-22) — awaiting Ann verify.** Commits `e6765609`..`f3cc7548`, pushed; dev current as of 08-24. Verified end-to-end by Todd in the VI sandbox (quote → purchase → policy `CS-VEC-RPT-260822-0002708` ACTIVE → confirmation email). Event address now comes from the `*{jobPath-minus-year}` field row per `EventLocationFieldNaming`; `location` = event state; the offer **fails closed** when no complete address is on file. Four further defects fixed that the filing never knew about (misnamed org-contact wire fields that made VI drop every organization contact, `tsic_registrationid` always `Guid.Empty`, blank `customer.street`, and the composite `Org:Event` name in both `organization_name` and `event.name`). **Todd owns the data half — 3 jobs have no field row and go dark for team insurance until one is added.**
  - **⏳ LEFT OPEN AT ANN'S REQUEST (08-24): "I will test this with Chelsea and return to verify if it works as stated."** She has read the fix and knows where to add the row — **Scheduling → Manage Fields**, where the screen now shows an *"Event location not assigned" / "No event location on file"* banner and an **Address** row type (surfaced by `87f661ec`, warning cleared by `8543034a7`). Name is derived, never typed: `*` + jobPath minus the trailing year, e.g. `launchlacrosse-floridalaunchrising-2026` → **`*launchlacrosse-floridalaunchrising`**.
  - **The three dark jobs to fix while testing**: `launchlacrosse-floridalaunchrising-2026`, `topthreat-merrylaxmas-north-2026`, `topthreat-bigdawgsclassic-2027`. **Two are Top Threat's** — the customer that pays for Stay-to-Play — so they currently cannot sell team insurance.
  - **⚠ Use the VENUE address, not the club's mailing address.** The entire point of the fix is that VI adjudicates weather payouts from where the event physically happens; a mailing address reintroduces the defect in a quieter form.
ORIGINAL: 🔴 OPEN — filed 08-22; root cause confirmed in code, legacy behaviour recovered, fix fully specified.

### AR-019: ✅ FIXED (Todd, 08-22) · [TSIC-Teams / Mobile auth] "Reset my password" from the app login screen 404s
- **Topic**: TSIC-Teams mobile app → login screen → reset/forgot password
- **Observation (Ann, 08-22)**: before the recent mobile changes, asking to reset a password from the TSIC-Teams login screen returned a **404**. *"Not sure if this has been fixed yet."*
- **✅ ROOT CAUSE — it was never a missing endpoint. It was a hardcoded legacy URL in the CLIENT.** The earlier filing here assumed `reference/TSIC-TEAMS-2025` was empty; it is not. The shipped client is in the repo, and the button makes **no API call at all**:
  ```typescript
  // reference/TSIC-TEAMS-2025/src/app/pages/login/login.page.ts:134
  async forgotPassword() {
    await Browser.open({ url: `${WWW_URL}/tsic/Account/ForgotPassword` });
  }
  // WWW_URL (environment.prod.ts:19) = https://www.teamsportsinfo.com
  ```
  It opens an in-app browser at `www.teamsportsinfo.com/tsic/Account/ForgotPassword` — the **legacy MVC page**, which no longer serves. The 404 is an HTML 404 from a dead web route, not a route-table miss on the API. `MobileAuthController` having no reset route was a true observation but not the cause.
- **✅ FIXED in the TSIC-TEAMS rewrite** (Todd, publishing 08-22). No backend work was done and none was needed.
- **Backend was already ready** — recorded so nobody re-derives it:
  - `POST /api/auth/forgot-password` (`AuthController.cs:526-580`) has existed since `e3d54f80` (2026-02-27). It is **anonymous** (no class-level `[Authorize]`), always returns 200 regardless of match (no account enumeration), and sends one email **per matching account** — never `FindByEmailAsync`.
  - `capacitor://localhost` is already in `Cors:AllowedOrigins` in **Development, Staging and Production** — the app's webview origin can call it from every environment.
  - Sandbox gate: real email only in Production. In **Development** the response body carries the reset links (`ForgotPasswordResponse.DevResetLinks`), so the flow is walkable without a mailbox; Staging logs the link server-side only.
- **⚠ One design consequence to keep in view**: `AuthController.cs:562` builds the link as `{FrontendSettings.BaseUrl}/reset-password?token=…&userId=…` — the **Angular web app**. A TSIC-Teams user who resets leaves the app, resets in a browser, and comes back to log in. That hand-off is fine, but the app's copy should set the expectation. A mobile-prefixed endpoint (`api/mobile/auth/forgot-password`) is only worth adding if a deep-link-back-into-the-app reset is ever wanted.
- **Severity**: 🔴 was user-blocking on mobile
- **Status**: ✅ FIXED — client-side, in the rewrite. Filed 08-22, closed 08-22.


### AR-018: [ASL Rosters] Carry the roster board forward to 2027, and change the region set (NorCal→Utah, SoCal→New York Capital Region, restore California)
- **Topic**: public ASL roster board — `teamsportsinfo.com/{jobPath}/aslrosters` (screenshot: `americanselect-mainevent-2026/aslrosters`)
- **Request (Ann, 08-21)**: 1) bring rosters forward to **2027**; 2) **NorCal → Utah**, **SoCal → New York Capital Region**, and **add back California**.

---

### 📣 ANSWER FOR ANN — AR-018 (08-23)

**Short version: our side is done. The 2027 sites already exist, the region change is already in the code, and the only thing standing between you and a working 2027 board is the teams themselves.**

**1. The region change is built.** Utah and New York Capital Region are now recognised regions, and California was always there. This goes out with the next deploy — nothing for you to do.

**2. The 2027 sites already exist.** They were built back on 08-13. You have `americanselect-utah-2027`, `americanselect-nycapitalregion-2027` and `americanselect-california-2027`, and **there is no NorCal or SoCal 2027 site at all** — so "remove NorCal and SoCal from 2027" is already how it is. **Please do not clone the 2026 site to make 2027** — it already exists, and cloning it would drag NorCal and SoCal back in.

**3. What's left is roster data, and it's ASL's to enter.** The 2027 Main Event site currently has **8 teams — all Connecticut**. The 2026 site has **135**. Until the rest of the 2027 teams are created, the board will only show Connecticut, because the region dropdown is built from whatever teams actually exist. That's Chelsea's/ASL's data entry, not a code change on our end.

**4. ⚠ One thing that will silently break it — please pass this on verbatim.** The board works out a team's region **by reading its name**, so the name has to match exactly. **Use spaces, not a hyphen:**

  - ✅ `ASL:New York Capital Region Blue 2029`
  - ❌ `ASL:New York-Capital Region Blue 2029`  ← the *site* is named this way, the teams must not be
  - ✅ `ASL:Utah Black 2029` · ✅ `ASL:California Grey 2030`

  Keep the `ASL:` prefix, and keep the **grad year last**. If a team name doesn't match a known region, it doesn't disappear — it shows up on the board with its **whole team name** where the region should be, which is exactly the mess we're fixing.

**5. A bonus you'll see on 2026.** The two Utah teams already on the **2026** board (`ASL:Utah Black 2028`, `ASL:Utah Grey 2028`) have been showing their full team names instead of "ASL:Utah". That's fixed by the same change. **After the next deploy, check the 2026 board first** — if those two now read "ASL:Utah", the change is live and 2027 will behave the same way as teams get added.

---

- **⚠ This is mostly DATA, not code — and the two halves must land together or the board mislabels itself.**

**1) Forward to 2027 — pure job/data work, no code.**
- **Nothing in the ASL code carries a year.** Grepped `AslRostersController.cs` and the whole `views/rosters/asl-rosters/` component: **zero occurrences of 2026 or 2027**. The board is `jobPath`-scoped, so it renders whatever job you point it at.
- ⇒ Create the 2027 Main Event job (clone of `americanselect-mainevent-2026`) with its teams/rosters, and `/{new-jobPath}/aslrosters` works with no deploy.

**2) The region changes — one data half and one code half.**
- **How regions work**: region is **not a column**. It is parsed out of the team name — every ASL team is `ASL:{Region} {qualifier} {gradYear}` — by `AslRegionConstants.ResolveRegion` (`AslRegionConstants.cs:58-68`), which does a `Contains` scan over an ordered token list, first match wins.
- **The dropdown lists only regions that actually have teams**: `AslRostersController.cs:45-47` builds it as `teams.Select(ResolveRegion).Distinct()`. The constants list is a **matching vocabulary**, not the menu.
- **✅ CONFIRMED NOT RENAMES (Ann, 08-21): "they are NOT renames — simply add them new and remove NorCal and SoCal from the 2027 board."** So **Utah** and **New York Capital Region** are **brand-new regions with fresh teams and fresh rosters**. Nothing carries over from NorCal/SoCal; those two simply do not exist on 2027.
- **DATA half — create teams, don't rename any.** Name the new teams `ASL:Utah …` and `ASL:New York Capital Region …` (plus `ASL:California …`), each ending in the grad year. Until teams exist under those names, nothing appears on the board.
- **✅ "Remove NorCal/SoCal from the 2027 board" costs NO code and NO deletion of constants — it is purely an absence of teams.** The dropdown is built from teams that exist (`AslRostersController.cs:45-47`), so a region with no 2027 teams simply never appears. Leave the constants alone.
  - **⚠ BUT if 2027 is created by CLONING the 2026 job, the clone brings the NorCal and SoCal teams with it.** They must be **deleted from the 2027 job after the clone**, or they will show on the 2027 board exactly as before. This is the one step most likely to be missed.
- **CODE half — add the two new tokens** to `AslRegionConstants.Regions` (`:26-53`). **If you skip this, `ResolveRegion` matches nothing and falls back to the full team name**, so the region label and dropdown entry become e.g. `ASL:Utah Blue 2028` instead of `ASL:Utah`. Renaming without the constant is the failure mode to avoid.
- **✅ California needs NO code — it is already there.** `"ASL:California"` is the first entry in the list, present since `1383df543`. It is missing from the board only because no 2026 team is named for it. **"Add back California" = create California teams in the 2027 job**, nothing more.
- **⚠ Do NOT delete `ASL:NorCal` / `ASL:SoCal` from the constants.** They cost nothing to keep, and the 2026 job and any archived ASL job still resolve through them. Removing them would leave every historical NorCal/SoCal team falling back to its full team name.
- **⚠ Ordering is load-bearing** (stated in the file's own doc comment): first `Contains` match wins, so a token that is a substring of another must come first. Checked for this change — **`ASL:Utah` and `ASL:New York Capital Region` collide with nothing** in the existing list (`New Jersey`, `North East`, `NY Downstate`, `NY Upstate` are all distinct strings). Safe to append. **Re-check this if anyone later adds a shorter token like `ASL:New York`.**
- **⚠ Keep the grad year LAST in the team name.** `ResolveGradYear` (`:74-78`) takes the **trailing 4 characters**. `ASL:New York Capital Region Blue 2028` is fine; anything appended after the year silently breaks the grad-year grouping.
- **NET WORK — the whole item, now that it's scoped:**
  1. **Code (one file, two lines)**: append `"ASL:Utah"` and `"ASL:New York Capital Region"` to `AslRegionConstants.Regions`. Nothing else in code changes. **Do not remove NorCal/SoCal.**
  2. **Data**: create the 2027 job; create Utah, New York Capital Region and California teams (fresh rosters); **if cloned from 2026, delete the copied NorCal and SoCal teams.**
  3. **Verify**: open `/{2027-jobPath}/aslrosters` — dropdown shows Utah, New York Capital Region and California; shows **no** NorCal or SoCal; each region label reads as the region alone, not a full team name (that check is what proves step 1 landed).
  4. **Regression check**: the **2026** board still lists NorCal and SoCal correctly — proves the constants weren't stripped.

**✅ CURRENT STATE — verified against the dev DB, 08-23. Most of this item is already done; read this before acting on the steps above.**
- **CODE HALF: DONE and PUSHED — `e4c73dc2`.** Both `"ASL:Utah"` and `"ASL:New York Capital Region"` are in `AslRegionConstants.Regions` today. NorCal/SoCal untouched, as instructed. Not yet deployed.
- **It fixed a LIVE 2026 mislabel, not just 2027 prep.** `ASL:Utah Black 2028` and `ASL:Utah Grey 2028` already exist on **Main Event 2026**, and before `e4c73dc2` they resolved to their **full team names** on the 2026 board. The filing's "seasonal / for 2027" framing understated it — this ships a visible 2026 correction on the next deploy, and that is the cheapest way to verify the change is live.
- **THE 2027 JOBS ALREADY EXIST.** The 08-13 ASL regional fan-out built them (25 ASL 2027 jobs, incl.
