Main Event). **`americanselect-utah-2027` and `americanselect-nycapitalregion-2027` are both there, `americanselect-california-2027` is there, and there is NO NorCal or SoCal 2027 job.** So Ann's region reconfiguration already landed at the job level. **Step 1's "create the 2027 job by cloning 2026" is WRONG — do not clone anything**, and the "delete the copied NorCal/SoCal teams" warning is moot.
  - ⚠ **Never use `utility.CloneJobII`** if any ASL job ever does need creating — it writes nothing to `fees.JobFees`. The 08-13 fan-out used `cloneASLRegionsFromCA2027.sql`. That ruling stands.
  - ⚠ Note this **supersedes the 08-13 decision** recorded as *"NorCal + SoCal both KEPT for 2027"* — Ann ruled the opposite on 08-21 and the DB already matches Ann.
- **WHAT IS ACTUALLY MISSING — the roster board itself.** `americanselect-mainevent-2027` has **8 ASL teams, all Connecticut**, against **135 on Main Event 2026**. The 2027 board is effectively empty. **~127 teams of data entry — ASL's/Chelsea's work, not a code task.** Until they exist, the dropdown stays at Connecticut (it is built from teams that exist, `AslRostersController.cs:45-47`).
- **⚠ NAMING TRAP — the one thing that will silently break this.** The matching token is `ASL:New York Capital Region` (**spaces**), but the 2027 job is named `New York-Capital Region` (**hyphen**). 2026's convention proves teams are named for the token, not the job (`ASL:DC - Virginia`, `ASL:NY Upstate Blue 2029`). **If the new teams are typed as `ASL:New York-Capital Region …`, `Contains` will not match and every one of them will display its full team name as its region** — the exact Utah symptom `e4c73dc2` just fixed. Give ASL the literal string before they start typing.
- **Severity**: 🟢 Seasonal rollover + region reconfiguration — code change DONE, remaining work is data entry
- **Status**: 🟢 **DATA-ONLY — code done and pushed 08-23 (`e4c73dc2`), awaiting deploy.** Nothing left to build. Remaining: ASL creates the Main Event 2027 teams using the exact `ASL:{token} …{gradYear}` strings; verify the 2026 board's two Utah teams label as `ASL:Utah` after deploy. ORIGINAL: 🔴 OPEN — filed 08-21 at Ann's request.

### AR-017: [Communications / Bulletins] Red and blue text set in the editor renders as plain body text on the displayed bulletin
- **Topic**: Communications → Bulletins → RTE text colour → displayed bulletin card. Job: **ODU Lacrosse Camps and Clinics 2026**
- **Observation (Ann, 08-21)**: colours applied while editing don't appear once the bulletin is active. **Matched editor/display screenshots supplied for the SAME two bulletins**, so this is now a confirmed loss, not two unrelated records:
  | Text | Editor | Displayed |
  |:--|:--|:--|
  | `COST: $225/clinic` (Prospect Day) | **red** | plain body text ❌ |
  | `COST: $50` (August Youth Clinic) | **red** | plain body text ❌ |
  | `September 13th, 2026` | **blue** | plain body text ❌ |
  | `Parking is available at lot 43…` | orange | **orange** ✅ |
  | no-refunds line | yellow highlight | **yellow highlight** ✅ |
- **✅ ROOT CAUSE — PINNED 08-23 off the stored HTML. `.bulletin-body strong` was eating the colour.** The one piece of data the filing asked for was pulled (`Jobs.Bulletins.text`, both ODU records — note the table is `Jobs.Bulletins` and the column is lowercase `text`). Both bulletins carry the identical shape:
  ```html
  <span style="color:#FF0000;font-size:17px;"><strong>$225/clinic</strong></span>
  <span style="color:#0000FF;font-size:17px;"><strong>September 13th, 2026</strong></span>
  ```
  **The colour is on the WRAPPER; the visible text is inside `<strong>`.** `.bulletin-body strong { color: var(--brand-text) }` (`_component-overrides.scss:264-266`) matched that `<strong>` **directly**, and a direct match always beats an **inherited** value — so the wrapper's red never reached a single glyph. Same for `em` at `:269-271`.
- **⛔ THE FILED DISCRIMINATOR WAS A COINCIDENCE — do not act on it, and do not re-derive it.** The red/blue-vs-orange split is real but explains nothing: the `.bl-c-*` class was **never** the failing part. An inline `color` on that same span loses to `.bulletin-body strong` in exactly the same way, so **deleting the `COLOR_CLASSES` map would have fixed nothing.** Orange survived for an unrelated reason — the RTE emitted `<i>`, and there is no `.bulletin-body i` rule:
  ```html
  <span style="…color:hsl(30,75%,60%);…"><i>Parking is available at lot 43…</i></span>
  ```
  Red and blue happened to be **bold**; orange happened to be **italic**. That is the entire split. The real discriminator is *"is the coloured wrapper's text inside `strong`/`em`"*, not *"is the hex in the pipe's map"*.
- **THE FIX (2 declarations, CSS only)**: drop `color: var(--brand-text)` from `.bulletin-body strong` and `.bulletin-body em`; `font-weight` / `font-style` stay. Uncoloured emphasis still inherits `--brand-text` from `.bulletin-body` itself, so **nothing that renders correctly today changes**. Red/blue still arrive as `var(--bs-danger)` / `var(--bs-primary)` through the `.bl-c-*` classes, so the pipe's palette/dark-mode job is preserved intact — **pipe and sanitizer untouched.**
- **Checked, not assumed**: `mark { background:#ffff00; color:#1a1a1a }` (`_utilities.scss:10`) is a direct rule on the `mark`, so the highlighted no-refunds line still renders dark-on-yellow — **the one ✅ line Ann called correct does not move.** No `strong`/`em` rules exist in `_theme-dark.scss`, so light and dark behave the same.
- **Scale**: 756 of 835 active bulletins contain both a `color:` declaration and a bold/em tag (crude `LIKE` upper bound, not the true hit rate). Not a two-bulletin problem.
- **KNOWN GAP, deliberately NOT fixed (different shape, nobody has reported it)**: a colour applied to a whole `<p>` rather than a span. `.bulletin-body p { color: var(--brand-text) }` (`:244-246`) kills it in light mode, and in dark mode `[data-bs-theme='dark'] p` (`_theme-dark.scss:146`) scores **0-2-1** and outruns `.bl-c-red` at **0-2-0**, so that variant needs a dark-mode carve-out as well. Neither ODU bulletin uses it. File separately if it ever surfaces.
- ~~**🔑 THE DISCRIMINATOR — it is exactly the colours the legacy pipe rewrites that fail.**~~ **SUPERSEDED — see above.** `translate-legacy-urls.pipe.ts:27-30` maps **`#0000ff`/`#0000cd` → `.bl-c-blue`, `#ff0000` → `.bl-c-red`, `#000000` → `.bl-c-black`**. **Red and blue are in that map; orange is not.** So:
  - red/blue → declaration removed from `style`, replaced with a **class** → **lost on render**
  - orange + highlight → not in the map → left as **inline `style`** → **render correctly**
  - **⇒ inline styles are surviving; the `.bl-c-*` CLASSES are what fail to paint.** This is the opposite of the usual "sanitizer ate the styles" story, and it means the 08-05 sanitizer fix (`333d40ec6`) is working — the failure is downstream of it.
- **Ruled OUT (Claude, 08-21) — do not re-investigate:**
  - Sanitizer: `style` is in `GLOBAL_ATTRS` (`rich-text-sanitizer.ts:79`), `color` in `ALLOWED_CSS_PROPS` (`:119`), guard `UNSAFE_CSS_VALUE` (`:135`) doesn't reject `rgb(`. **And the orange proves inline colour reaches the page.**
  - Missing class hook: the body div **does** carry `class="bulletin-body"` (`bulletins.component.html:40`), so `.bulletin-body .bl-c-red` has its ancestor.
  - Plain specificity: `.bulletin-body .bl-c-red` (0,2,0) **beats** `.bulletin-body p { color: var(--brand-text) }` (0,1,1) at `_component-overrides.scss:243-246`, whether the class sits on the `<p>` or a `<span>` inside it. On paper the class should win.
- ~~**⚠️ Mechanism NOT pinned — two candidates, check in this order:**~~ **BOTH CANDIDATES WERE WRONG — kept only so the trail isn't re-walked. Candidate 2 in particular: `.bl-c-red` DOES reach the element and DOES paint the span; it just never inherits into the `<strong>` inside it.**
  1. **~~DEPLOY LAG~~ — FULLY ELIMINATED (Todd + Ann, 08-21).** Todd confirms *"dev was deployed"*; Ann confirms she **took these images a few minutes later, after the re-deploy**. So the screenshots show **current dev code carrying `333d40ec6`**. This is a **live bug**, the evidence is clean, and candidate 2 is the entire investigation.
     - **Corroborating, and worth stating**: a pre-`333d40ec6` build would strip inline styles and keep classes — predicting the **opposite** symptom (orange lost, red/blue surviving). Ann sees orange surviving and red/blue lost, which is only consistent with the **post-fix** build. **The evidence and the deploy timeline agree.**
  2. **PRIMARY CANDIDATE — the `.bl-c-*` rules aren't reaching the element.** They live nested under `.bulletin-body` in `_component-overrides.scss:306-308`. Confirm in devtools whether `.bl-c-red` is (a) present on the element at all — i.e. the pipe actually matched and converted — and (b) being applied or overridden. **If the RTE now emits `rgb(255, 0, 0)` rather than `#ff0000`, the map never matches**, the declaration stays inline, and it should render red — so if the element carries neither the class nor an inline colour, the loss is happening before CSS and the trail restarts at the stored HTML.
- ~~**NEXT STEP — the one piece of data that ends this**: `SELECT Text FROM Bulletins WHERE …`~~ **DONE 08-23 — that query is what pinned it. Both records dumped and read; the input shape is `<span style="color:#HEX"><strong>…</strong></span>`, hex not `rgb()`, no `<font>`, no Syncfusion class.**
- **Severity**: 🟡 Bug / authored-formatting fidelity — director sees one thing while authoring and another in production, the failure shape `333d40ec6` was written to eliminate
- **Status**: 🟡 **BUILT (Todd, 08-23)** — CSS-only, `_component-overrides.scss` `.bulletin-body strong` / `em`. `ng serve` was up so this hot-reloads; no build run. ✅ **VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 08-24) — CLOSED.** Authored colours render again on the ODU bulletins.
- **Ann's observation on closing: "the color palette is more muted but the colors show now." That muting is a SEPARATE, PRE-EXISTING, DELIBERATE behaviour — not a side effect of this fix, and she has ruled to LEAVE IT (08-24).**
  - `TranslateLegacyUrlsPipe`'s `COLOR_CLASSES` (`:26-31`) substitutes four exact legacy hexes for theme classes: `#ff0000` → `.bl-c-red` → **`var(--bs-danger)`**, `#0000ff`/`#0000cd` → `.bl-c-blue` → **`var(--bs-primary)`**, `#000000` → `.bl-c-black` → `var(--brand-text)` (`_component-overrides.scss:311-313`). So an author typing pure red gets brand red, not `#FF0000`.
  - **The rationale is dark-mode survivability**: a hard `#FF0000` typed years ago stays that exact red on a dark background, where it can be unreadable; the token shifts with the theme. The pipe's own comment calls it *"better than the literal hex the author typed."*
  - **It has always been happening** — it was simply invisible while `.bulletin-body strong` was killing the colour downstream regardless. Fixing the real defect exposed a design decision nobody had ever seen take effect.
  - **Scope is narrow**: only those four exact values are reinterpreted. Every other authored colour — the orange parking line, anything picked from the palette — passes through exactly as typed.
  - **Options offered and declined (Ann, 08-24: "leave it")**: drop the substitution so colours render literally; or narrow it to keep only the black mapping (the one that genuinely matters for dark mode) and let red/blue through. **Do not re-raise without a client complaint** — reversing it trades theme-safety for literal fidelity.
ORIGINAL: 🔴 OPEN — filed 08-21 with matched, post-deploy evidence; deploy fully eliminated as a cause.

### AR-016: [Search / Detail fly-ins] Open the player and team detail panels wide enough to show the full accounting section
- **Topic**: Search Registrations → player detail fly-in · Search Teams → team detail fly-in → accounting section width
- **Request (Ann, 08-21)**: The fly-in opens too narrow to show the full accounting section. **"Users don't know it is expandable, and almost everyone will leave it there once they know about it — so let's just have it fully expanded to begin with."**
- **✅ INTERPRETATION CONFIRMED (Ann, 08-21)** — it is the **panel width**, not a collapsed section. (Consistent with the code: `registration-detail-panel.component.html:556-566` renders `<app-accounting-ledger>` directly, ungated, and the ledger has no expand/collapse state.)
- **🎯 THE ACTUAL SPEC (Ann, 08-21), and it is not a fixed number**: *"whatever makes horizontal scrolling of accounting not necessary in whatever device is being used."* The target is **an outcome — no horizontal scroll on the accounting section — not a width.**
  - **⚠ This cannot be met by `[defaultWidth]` alone, and on small screens it may not be met by panel width at all.** The panel is capped at `maxWidth` 1100 (`resizable-panel.directive.ts:39`), and on ≤767.98px it is already forced to `width: 100vw !important` (`:23`) — i.e. **already as wide as the device allows.** If the accounting table still scrolls at full viewport width, no panel setting can fix it; **the table itself has to fit** (drop/combine columns, wrap, or a compact layout below a breakpoint). Widening the panel solves the desktop case only.
  - **So this is potentially two fixes**: (a) open wide enough on desktop that accounting fits without dragging — the easy half; (b) make the accounting table fit the viewport on smaller devices — the real half, and the one Ann's wording actually asks for.
  - **Measure first**: get the accounting ledger's natural minimum width (the point below which it scrolls), then decide whether 1100 is even sufficient, and whether `maxWidth` needs raising. Do not pick a number by eye.
  - **🔗 SEQUENCE AFTER AR-023 (Ann, 08-23).** AR-023 adds a **new Date column** to this very ledger, and Ann explicitly tied the two: *"adding this column won't be a problem, especially if we adjust the width of this flyin upon opening."* **Measuring before that column exists produces a number that is immediately stale.** Build AR-023's column first, then measure and set the width here. Note the extra column makes the no-h-scroll target *harder*, especially below 768px where the panel is already `100vw` — so it strengthens the case for the "make the table fit" half of this item (collapse the Date column back into the Transaction cell at narrow widths, or otherwise compact the layout).
  - **Note the precedent**: Ann raised a no-horizontal-scroll constraint on AR-006 too, and it was overridden there ("3rd party pushing off is perfectly fine"). **This is the second time — it is a consistent expectation, not a one-off preference.** Worth a deliberate answer rather than a second override.
- **Findings (Claude, 08-21) — the mechanism matches her description precisely:**
  - Both fly-ins use `appResizablePanel`, a drag-handle width resizer: `registration-detail-panel.component.html:5` (`storageKey="regDetailPanelWidth"`) and `team-detail-panel.component.html:5` (`storageKey="teamDetailPanelWidth"`).
  - **Neither sets `[defaultWidth]`**, so both fall back to the directive default of **560px** (`resizable-panel.directive.ts:36-38`; min 480, max 1100).
  - **Width persists in localStorage** (`:96`) — which is exactly why Ann says *"almost everyone will leave it there once they know about it."* Once widened it stays widened. The problem is purely discovery of the handle.
  - Setting an explicit default is the **established pattern**, not a new one: club rosters uses `[defaultWidth]="400"`, and both filter panels use `[defaultWidth]="520"`.
- **For Todd** — the ORIGINAL plan, **SUPERSEDED 08-23** by the percentage approach above. Kept for the reasoning; point 2 (stored width wins) still applies verbatim.
  1. Add `[defaultWidth]` to those two panels, sized to what the accounting ledger actually needs at full width. **Do not simply max it to 1100** — on a 1366px laptop that leaves almost none of the results grid visible behind the panel, which trades one complaint for another. Measure the ledger's natural width and size to that.
  2. **⚠ ~~The change will NOT reach anyone who has already dragged the panel.~~ — SOLVED 08-23 by versioning the storageKey; see the note below.** `readStoredWidth()` prefers the stored value, so a new default only applies to users with nothing in localStorage. That's correct behaviour (never stomp a user's deliberate choice) but it means **Ann herself may not see the change** if she has ever resized it — she can double-click the handle to reset to the new default (`:18`). Say so when handing it back, or it reads as "not fixed".
  3. No mobile impact: `@media (max-width: 767.98px)` forces `width: 100vw !important` regardless of stored or default width (`:23`).
- **Severity**: 🔵 UX / discoverability — nothing is broken, but a default that hides the main content behind an undiscovered affordance costs every user the same lesson
- **🟡 BUILT (Todd, 08-23) — commits `d3da5af3` (width + grip), `e144e7d0` (key versioning), `08bfdaf9` (typed panel keys). PUSHED, not deployed.** Todd reframed the item on 08-23: *"it all boils down to observation that initial width isn't what she wants, then how to address (either wider default or visual clue that is widenable)"* — and asked whether the width could be a **percentage of screen** rather than a picked number. Both halves were built:
  - **Percentage default.** New `defaultWidthPct` input on `appResizablePanel` (`resizable-panel.directive.ts`). When set, the opening width is `clamp(minWidth, pct × window.innerWidth, maxWidth)`; `defaultWidth` px still works for every existing caller. Resolution runs through one private `resolveDefaultWidth()` so the stored-width path and the double-click reset path agree.
  - Both detail panels now pass **`[defaultWidthPct]="0.62"` and `[maxWidth]="1400"`**. The raised cap matters: at the old 1100 the fraction stopped governing at ~1775px viewport, so every 1080p and wider monitor would have silently fallen back to a fixed number. 62% gives 847 on a 1366 laptop (grid keeps 519), 1190 at 1920, 1400 at 2560.
  - **Persistent grip.** `.resize-handle` gained an always-painted `::after` pill (4×44px, `--bs-border-color`, `--shadow-sm`) that brightens to `--bs-primary` and grows to 72px on hover/drag. The 6px bar was `background: transparent` until hovered — *that* is the discovery failure, not the width. Hover dimming switched from `opacity: 0.35` to `color-mix`, because opacity on the handle would have faded the grip too — the one part that must get *more* visible. Grip is `display: none` below 768px (panel is already full-bleed; an affordance that does nothing).
- **🔓 THE AR-023 SEQUENCING DEPENDENCY IS DISSOLVED — do not re-raise it.** The whole coupling rested on "measure the ledger's minimum width, then pick a panel width," so an added Date column would have invalidated the measurement. **A fraction of the viewport needs no measurement and does not go stale when the column lands.** AR-023 can now be built in either order.
- **⚠ Also worth recording: the filing's premise did not survive a code read.** `.ledger-table` is `width: 100%` with **no `min-width` and no fixed pixel width anywhere in 734 lines of SCSS**, and the Transaction cell is `nowrap` + `text-overflow: ellipsis` — it compresses rather than pushing the table out. The four-column read-only table has no natural floor to measure. If accounting genuinely scrolls sideways on desktop, the source is more likely the **inline payment/correction forms** or the **club-rep allocation table**, neither of which was examined when this was filed. **Nobody has actually seen the scrollbar** — it was inferred from Ann's wording. If she reports it still scrolls after this, get the screenshot and the device before touching the table.
- **⚠ And the mobile half is not what the filing said.** Below 768px `.panel-body` already sets `overflow-x: hidden` (`_flyin.scss:193`), so accounting **cannot pan sideways on a phone today — it clips instead.** The failure mode there is unreachable content, not scrolling, and no panel width touches it.
- **Verification**: `ng build` clean (94s). `verify:no-effect` OK, `verify:mobile` OK. **`verify:desktop-unchanged` FAILS on `_flyin.scss` and that is correct** — it is a mobile-readiness Phase-1 guard asserting a mobile pass changed no desktop CSS; this is a deliberate desktop change in its own commit, which is exactly what that script tells you to do. Not wired to any git hook.
- **🔑 STORAGE KEYS VERSIONED (08-23, after Todd tested and saw no change).** `regDetailPanelWidth` / `teamDetailPanelWidth` → **`-v2`**. Todd set the panels to 80% to prove the mechanism and the width did not move: he had a stored width from an earlier drag, and stored beats default. That is not a testing artifact — **Ann has used these panels for weeks, so she has one too, and she was the one population the fix could never reach.** Telling her to double-click a handle she never discovered is circular; the handle's undiscoverability IS the item. A new key drops every user onto the new default exactly once and their later drags persist normally. Clearing keys on startup was rejected — that stomps a deliberate choice; a new key does not. **This is the general technique for forcing any panel default change, documented on the directive.**
- **✅ VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 08-24) — CLOSED.** Fly-ins open wide enough that accounting is readable without dragging; the `-v2` key rename did its job and she landed on the new default without any reset step.
  - **Persistence confirmed working as designed.** Ann narrowed a wide panel and it stayed narrow on reopen, and asked whether that was correct. **It is** — the computed default applies until the user makes a deliberate choice, then the choice wins permanently. A default that reasserted itself would fight anyone who wanted the panel narrow. Double-clicking the grip clears the stored width and returns to the 62% default (`resizable-panel.directive.ts:113-117`), so the first-run appearance stays re-testable at will.
  - **⚠ ONE HALF REMAINS UNVERIFIABLE BY ANN, AND IT IS THE ORIGINAL DEFECT.** The item was never really about width — it was that a **transparent** 6px handle is undiscoverable. Ann now knows exactly where the grip is and cannot un-know it, so she cannot judge whether a first-time user notices the pill. **She will ask users as they use fly-ins and confirm that way.** Do not treat the closure above as evidence the discoverability fix works; that evidence has to come from someone seeing it cold.
- **Status**: ✅ **VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 08-24) — CLOSED on width.** Grip discoverability deliberately left to user feedback (Ann, 08-24) — reopen only if users report they still cannot find the handle. Filed 08-21, built 08-23.

### AR-015: [Communications / Bulletins] Bulletin Start/End need a TIME component (legacy parity) — and can that time-gate a Smart Bulletin to open/close registration?
- **Topic**: Communications → Bulletins → Start / End dates · and Smart Bulletins → registration visibility
- **Request (Ann, 08-21)**: Bulletin **Start and End dates need time added** — *"was available in Legacy… allows a bulletin to live at midnight for example."* Then the bigger question: **can that same time control be applied to a Smart Bulletin to control registration?** She ties it explicitly back to AR-007 (turning bulletins on/off) and notes **this matters to directors who want to go live or close registration at a specific time.**
- **⚠ These are TWO different problems with two different answers. Do not treat them as one.**

**PART A — director-authored bulletins: the time is ALREADY stored and ALREADY honored. Only the UI drops it.**
- `Bulletins.StartDate` / `EndDate` are **`DateTime?`**, not dates — the column has carried a time component all along. **No schema change.**
- The public filter already compares against a full timestamp: `BulletinRepository.cs:28-35` takes `var now = DateTime.Now` (**not** `.Date`) and applies `StartDate <= now` / `EndDate >= now`. **A bulletin stamped 23:00 would already stay hidden until 23:00 today.**
- **The blocker is one line of frontend**: the editor grid declares both columns date-only — `bulletin-editor.component.html:88-89`, `type="date" format="MMM d, yyyy"`. The director cannot enter a time, and a date-only picker writes midnight. **Fix = datetime picker + a format carrying time.** Frontend only; backend and DB need nothing.
- **⚠ TIMEZONE — the trap in this feature.** `BulletinRepository.cs:27` states outright: *"Bulletin start/end dates are stored in local AZ time, not UTC."* So a director in Eastern time asking for "close at midnight" would be entering **AZ time**, and a naive datetime picker will silently be 2–3 hours off. **Surfacing time without solving the timezone display is worse than date-only**, because midnight-off-by-three closes registration on the wrong day. Decide the presentation (label the field AZ/Mountain, or convert per-job) as part of the fix, not after.

**PART B — Smart Bulletins: the time control Ann wants does NOT apply, because an SB is not a row.**
- Per AR-007's finding, Smart Bulletins are **auto-derived from job flags**, not records in `Bulletins` — *"the SB sections have no durable keys, so there is nothing to hang a rule on."* There is no `StartDate` to add a time to.
- The flags an SB reads (`allowPlayerRegistration`, `allowTeamRegistration`, `allowStaffRegistration`, …) are **plain booleans** — no date dimension anywhere. So "close registration at 11:59pm" against an SB means **scheduling the flag**, which does not exist in any form today.
- **A time-window mechanism DOES already exist one level down, and it is fully time-aware**: `TeamSelfRosterAvailability.WindowContains` (`:51-55`) gates self-rostering on `Effectiveasofdate <= now && Expireondate >= now` — real timestamps, even handling sub-second insert-default pairs. Directors already close team-level registration by date/time this way (it is the documented way to reopen a past-dated job). **Worth deciding whether Ann's need is already met by that, or genuinely requires job-level flag scheduling.**

**WHY THIS REOPENS AR-007 — and on materially better ground.**
- Todd declined the SB off switch (08-19) on one specific objection: *"the kind of toggle flipped once and forgotten — a site quietly stops advertising registration while registration is open."*
- **A scheduled window is immune to that objection.** It is not a manual switch someone forgets; it **expires on its own** at a time the director chose deliberately. The failure mode Todd named cannot occur.
- It also does not undo the automation he was defending — the band still derives itself, it just respects an explicit open/close window the way every other dated thing in the system does.
- **This does not overturn his ruling** — he also recorded the correct future shape (a `smartbulletins` schema giving each SB a durable identity, *"build the schema first — do not retrofit a boolean"*). A scheduled window is exactly the sort of rule that schema is meant to hold. **Ann's request is evidence for the client need he said would be required to reopen it.**
- **For Todd — the question to answer**: does "directors want registration to open/close at a specific time" constitute the *real client need* you set as the bar for reopening AR-007? If yes, this is the first concrete use case for the `smartbulletins` schema. If no, say so and Ann has an answer.
- **For Todd — Part A is separable and should not wait on any of that.** Shipping the datetime picker for ordinary bulletins is small, honors what the DB and repository already do, and delivers the legacy parity Ann actually asked for first.
- **Severity**: 🟡 Part A = legacy-parity gap, frontend-only, timezone caveat · 🟢 Part B = feature request + a decision on AR-007's reopen bar
- **Status**: ⛔ **WILL NOT DO — Todd, 08-23.** *"WILL NOT DO, no need."* Applies to **both parts**: no time component on bulletin Start/End, and **AR-007 stays closed — Ann's "directors want to open/close at a specific time" did NOT meet the reopen bar.** Do not re-raise either half, and do not re-pitch the `smartbulletins` schema off this item. ✅ **Acknowledged (Ann, 08-24)** — she has seen the ruling on both parts, including that AR-007 stays closed. ORIGINAL: 🔴 OPEN — filed 08-21 at Ann's request.

**⚠ TWO LIVE DEFECTS FOUND WHILE TEEING THIS UP — NOT covered by the decline, NOT yet filed.** They are bugs in the existing date-only path, not the feature Todd declined. Recorded here so they are not lost or re-derived; promote to their own `AR-###` if he wants them worked.
1. **Every save truncates a stored time to midnight.** `formatDateForInput` (`bulletin-form-modal.component.ts:656-660`) cuts the loaded value to `YYYY-MM-DD`, and `BulletinService.cs:196-197` assigns `bulletin.StartDate = request.StartDate` unconditionally — no preserve, no merge. **1,163 active bulletins carry a non-midnight StartDate and 1,844 a non-midnight EndDate** (legacy wrote them). Editing one of those for any reason — a typo in the title — silently zeroes its time and it goes live at midnight instead.
2. **A new bulletin's default start date is computed in UTC.** `:461` does `formatDateForInput(new Date().toISOString())`, so after ~5pm Arizona (8pm Eastern) it resolves to **tomorrow**, while `BulletinRepository.cs:28` filters on server-local `DateTime.Now`. An evening-authored bulletin stays invisible for hours with nothing on screen explaining why.

**Banked findings (do not re-derive if either defect is ever taken):**
- The filing named the wrong file. `bulletin-editor.component.html:88-89` is the **read-only display grid**; the actual inputs are `<input type="date">` at `bulletin-form-modal.component.ts:193-211`.
- **Why the dates are correct today despite the UTC serialization**: saves go out as `new Date(dateOnly).toISOString()` (`:618`, `:638`) — UTC midnight **with a `Z`** — while the server filters on its own local clock. At date granularity the two errors cancel. **Expose a time and they stop cancelling, by 7 hours.** Any fix must drop `toISOString()` and send a naked local string.
- Schema needs nothing: `StartDate`/`EndDate` are already `DateTime?` and `BulletinRepository.cs:28` already compares a full timestamp, not `.Date`.
- **Rejected approach, do not propose**: re-basing the column to UTC with per-job conversion. Legacy reads this same database and compares its own local clock, so that desyncs 835 live rows across two apps.
- Part B's finding stands regardless: Smart Bulletins are Angular components derived from job pulse booleans — no row, no `StartDate`, nothing to hang a time on. `TeamSelfRosterAvailability.WindowContains` (`TeamSelfRosterAvailability.cs:51`) already does exactly this one level down, at team level.

### AR-014: [Reporting / X-Job Library] Add Tournament Start and End Dates to "Tournament Keys — ALL"

#### 📣 ANSWER FOR ANN — AR-014 (Todd, 08-24): ✅ **CLOSED. Nothing further is planned.**

**Both halves are done and live:** the tournament **start and end dates** were always in this report (columns **B** and **C**, `EventStart` / `EventEnd`) — they were buried under 261 blank rows because 70% of the jobs had no event date and SQL sorts blanks to the top. The report now lists **live jobs only, 151 instead of 375**. The **missing header row** was a separate exporter defect and is fixed (AR-029), live in staging and prod. Re-run "Tournament Keys — ALL" and you should see headings on row 1 and the dates in columns B and C.

**The one thing NOT done, deliberately: the column names have not been made friendlier.** `bSuspendPublic`, `bAllowMobileLogin`, `bClubRepAllowAdd` and the rest are the **literal config flag names**, and **Chelsea uses this report to QA how tournament jobs were built** — renaming them would make her job harder. Same reason the sort was left alone: a blank start date is a **finding**, so burying blanks at the bottom would hide it.

**Todd, 08-24: only come back on this if there is something that actually needs changing in code.** A preference about column labels is not that.
- **Topic**: X-Job Report Library → **Tournament Keys — ALL**
- **Request (Ann, 08-21)**: She needs a report in the X-Job Report Library carrying **tournament start and end dates**, and asks whether they can simply be added to the existing **"Tournament Keys — ALL"** rather than building something new.
- **ANSWER: yes — and it is a SQL-only change. No C#, no DTO, no API-model regen, no frontend edit.**
- **Findings (Claude, 08-21)**:
  - The report is catalog entry `xj-tournament-keys` (`x-job-report-catalog.ts:58`), declared **`kind: 'sp'`** → its entire output is whatever the stored procedure returns. Adding a column to the proc adds a column to the Excel.
  - Proc is **`[reporting].[TournamentKeyAttributes-ALL]`**, allowlisted for SuperUser cross-job execution at `GlobalSuperuserReports.cs:44`. That allowlist is name-only — it gates *who may run it*, not *what it returns*, so **the allowlist needs no edit**.
  - **The dates already exist on the Jobs table**: `Jobs.EventStartDate` and `Jobs.EventEndDate` (`Jobs.cs:212,214`, both `DateTime?`). No schema change, no new column, no backfill — the data is already there to select.
- **For Todd**:
  1. `ALTER PROCEDURE [reporting].[TournamentKeyAttributes-ALL]` to select `EventStartDate` / `EventEndDate`. **Append to the end of the column list** — Ann's users may have saved/《expected》column positions, and an SP-Excel dump has no header contract protecting mid-list insertions.
  2. **⚠ The proc body is NOT in source control.** No `.sql` file in the repo defines it (only `scripts/5) Re-Set Nav System.sql` mentions the name, for nav registration). So this is a **live-DB edit under the DB-first drill** — pull the current definition, review, apply by hand, and consider banking the definition in the repo while you're in there.
  3. **Confirm coverage before shipping**: `SELECT COUNT(*) FROM Jobs WHERE JobTypeId = 2 AND (EventStartDate IS NULL OR EventEndDate IS NULL)`. If tournaments routinely leave these null, the new columns will read blank and Ann will file this again as "the dates are empty" — better to know now whether the gap is data-entry rather than reporting.
- **ADDITION (Ann, 08-21): the report also needs COLUMN HEADINGS — "so data makes sense."** Same proc, same edit — **do both in one pass.**
  - **The Excel header row IS the SP's column names, verbatim.** `ReportingService.cs:969` builds the header from `reader.GetName(col)`, and `BuildExcelFromSheets` (`:993-995`) writes that as row 1. There is **no per-report header mapping anywhere in C#** — nothing to configure, nothing to add on the app side.
  - So a blank or cryptic heading means the proc is returning **unaliased columns**: an unaliased expression (`CASE…`, `COUNT(*)`, a concat) comes back with an **empty** name → blank header cell; a raw column comes back as the DB name (`bAllowSelfRostering`, `JobTypeId`) → technically present but not readable by a director.
  - **Fix**: alias every column in `[reporting].[TournamentKeyAttributes-ALL]` with a human-readable name (`AS [Tournament Start Date]`, `AS [Event End Date]`, …). **Alias the two new date columns the same way** — don't ship `EventStartDate` as a heading when the whole point of Ann's request is legible headings.
  - **Sanity check when done**: run the report and confirm row 1 has a readable label over **every** populated column, with no blanks. A blank header over real data is the exact symptom being reported.
- **Also worth a look while in there**: the sibling **"Job Key Attributes — ALL"** (`reporting.JobKeyAttributes-ALL`, same library, `GlobalSuperuserReports.cs:42`) may want the same two columns for non-tournament job types. Ann did not ask for it — **do not bundle it** without checking with her.
**✅ INVESTIGATED 08-23 — the premise is wrong: the dates were ALREADY in this report.**
- `[reporting].[TournamentKeyAttributes-ALL]` already selects `EventStart` and `EventEnd` (positions 2 and 3, formatted `2026-08-15 Saturday`). **Do NOT add the columns — you would get duplicates.**
- **Why Ann couldn't see them**: the proc ends `order by j.EventStartDate`, and **SQL Server sorts NULLs FIRST**. **261 of the 375 tournament/league jobs (70%) have no `EventStartDate`**, so the top of her spreadsheet is 261 blank rows.
- **The heading theory in the filing is also wrong for this report — no header is blank.** Every column comes back named; the two junk single-column result sets are already handled upstream (`ReportingService.cs:942-960`: the `QA Test:` one names the sheet, the bare-year one is swallowed). Ann's real complaint is that the names are **cryptic** — `countCR`, `crAdd`, `bVIEnabled`, `bApplyProcessingFeesToTeamDeposit`.
- Confirmed as filed: `kind:'sp'` ⇒ the Excel is verbatim whatever the proc returns · the `GlobalSuperuserReports.cs:44` allowlist gates *who runs it*, not *what it returns*, so no edit · X-Job always runs `bUseJobId=false`, so Ann gets the `Guid.Empty` branch — **all years, no expiry filter** · **the proc had no definition in source control.**

**RULING (Todd, 08-23): constrain the cross-job branch to `j.ExpiryAdmin > getdate()`. No year filter.**
- The predicate was already written in that branch and **commented out**; restoring it takes the report **375 → 151 rows** and the blank start dates **261 → 50**. Those 50 are live jobs missing an event date — a real build-QA finding instead of archive noise.
- Script: **`scripts/12-reporting-tournamentkeys-live-only.sql`** — `CREATE OR ALTER`, banks the previously-unversioned definition, one-line change, syntax-checked with `SET PARSEONLY ON`. **Not applied — Todd runs it (live-DB edit, DB-first).**
- **⚠ Sorting and headings deliberately NOT changed. Chelsea uses this report as QA on how tournament jobs were built** (Todd, 08-23), which flips both sub-asks: a NULL start date is a **finding**, so burying NULLs at the bottom would hide it; and `bSuspendPublic` / `bAllowMobileLogin` / `bClubRepAllowAdd` are the **actual config flag names** she checks builds against, so friendlier labels would make her job harder. **Ann and Chelsea want opposite things from one report — ask Ann what she uses it for before renaming anything.**
- **Severity**: 🟢 Feature / reporting enhancement (non-blocking; data exists, one proc to alter)
- **Status**: ✅ **CLOSED 08-24 — APPLIED — Todd ran the script on dev AND prod, 08-23.** Live in both. No deploy needed — it is a stored-proc change and the report is `kind:'sp'`, so the Excel picks it up on the next run. **Ann: re-run "Tournament Keys — ALL" — the start/end dates were there all along, and the report now lists only live tournament/league jobs (151, not 375).** **⚠ SUPERSEDED IN PART — see AR-029 (filed 08-24).** Ann re-ran the report on 08-24 and got **no headings at all** and could not find the dates. Root cause is in the **exporter**, not this proc: a bare single-column result set (`select convert(varchar, @thisYear)`) steals the sheet's header row, so the data's column names are silently discarded. **The two claims in this entry — that the bare-year set "is swallowed" and that "no header is blank" — do NOT hold through the Excel path.** The dates were always there, aliased `EventStart`/`EventEnd` in columns B/C. **✅ CLOSED (Todd, 08-24). The heading RENAME is declined by default — Chelsea's build-QA use of the literal flag names wins over friendlier labels, and the sort stays NULLs-first because a blank start date is a finding. Ann told: only return on this if something actually needs changing in CODE.** ORIGINAL: 🔴 OPEN — filed 08-21 at Ann's request.

### AR-013: [Payments / ARB] The "Expired/Terminated (Pay Balance Due)" dunning email sends families to a menu item they cannot see
- **Topic**: ARB Subscription Health → Behind in Payment → email template **"Expired/Terminated Subscriptions (Pay Balance Due)"**
- **What the parent sees**: the email instructs them to *"Under 'Player' in the upper right, select 'Pay Balance Due'"* (`arb-health.component.ts:57`). That menu item **is not rendered for them**. They sign in, don't find it, and call the director. This is the collections email — it lands on exactly the families who owe money.
- **Root cause (verified, Claude 08-20)** — the gate tests the wrong signal:
  - `AdnSubscriptionId` and `AdnSubscriptionStatus` are **separate columns** on `Registrations`. Expiring or terminating a plan changes the *status*; the **id is never cleared**.
  - `JobRepository.cs:1117` copies the raw column into the pulse with **no status filter** (`AdnSubscriptionId = reg.AdnSubscriptionId`).
  - Both "Pay Balance Due" surfaces suppress on **id presence**, not liveness: `client-header-bar.component.ts:139` and `registration-panel.component.ts:225`, each gated `&& !p.myAdnSubscriptionId`.
  - Net: any registration that *ever* had a subscription is treated as still-drafting forever, so the row is hidden for expired/terminated plans — the precise case the template targets.
- **The suppression itself is correct and must stay.** Both call sites carry the same comment: an ARB registrant's `OwedTotal` stays > 0 by design while the plan drafts, so a pay nudge would invite a **double payment**. The concept is right; the *condition* is wrong. It uses "has a plan id" as a proxy for "has a live plan" — identical for active plans, opposite for dead ones.
- **Intent is already documented, and the code contradicts it**: the template's own label says Pay Balance Due, and `public/help/arb-health/faq.html` explains Expired/Terminated go there *"because there's no live plan left to fix."* Help and code disagree; the help is the one that's right.
- **For Todd**: gate the "Pay Balance Due" row on subscription **status** (live = active/suspended) rather than on `myAdnSubscriptionId` being non-null. Likely wants `MyAdnSubscriptionStatus` (or a derived `MyHasLiveSubscription` bool) added to the pulse DTO alongside the id, then used at both call sites. **Two call sites — fix both**; they are independent copies of the same condition.
- **Secondary, same templates (stale vs shipped UI)**: all three ARB templates tell parents the amount is *"at the bottom of the screen"* and to *"Click Submit"* (`arb-health.component.ts:42-43, 73-74`). The shipped screen shows **Balance Due at the top** and a button reading **Update Card & Pay Balance**. The same stale wording is duplicated in `views/search/registrations/email-templates.ts:189, 212, 232, 314`.
- **~~Open question~~ — RESOLVED BY ELIMINATION (Ann, 08-21): the shortcut cannot be validated, so take the status gate.** The question was whether Update Payment Method completes for a *terminated* plan at Authorize.net — because if it did, the simplest fix would be routing **every** ARB family to "Update CC Info" and retiring the Pay Balance Due branch entirely. **Ann confirms no terminated subscription exists in the test data**, so that shortcut cannot be proven safe today. **Do not build on an unverified assumption about ADN's behavior on a dead subscription: implement the status-based gate above**, which is correct whether or not the shortcut would also have worked. Revisit the shortcut only if a terminated plan later becomes testable.
  - Related and now settled: AR-010 verified the *live*-subscription half of this flow end-to-end on 08-21, **including the same-card catch-up charge**. So the Update CC path is known-good for active/suspended plans; only the terminated case is unproven.
- **✅ BUILT 08-23 (Claude) — the status gate shipped; the matching Update CC swap was deliberately NOT taken.**
  - **One rule, two callers, cannot diverge**: new `TSIC.Domain/Constants/ArbSubscriptionStatus.cs` — `IsLive(id, status)` = id non-empty **AND** status not in `canceled/terminated/expired` (case-insensitive). `PaymentService` now delegates to it; `PartitionArbEnrolled` behavior is unchanged. Liveness stays anchored on the **id**: a status-only rule would read a registrant with no plan at all as live and suppress the nudge for every ordinary family who owes a balance.
  - **Pulse**: `GetPulseUserContextAsync` selects `AdnSubscriptionStatus` and computes `HasLiveArbSubscription` client-side (EF cannot translate the helper); `JobPulseUserContext.HasLiveArbSubscription` → `JobPulseDto.MyHasLiveArbSubscription`, populated in `JobsController`. **`MyAdnSubscriptionId` kept** — the Update CC route still needs it. Models regenerated: one field, one file.
  - **Four gates swapped** to `!myHasLiveArbSubscription`: `client-header-bar.component.ts:139` (player) and `:165` (staff), `registration-panel.component.ts:225`, `smart-bulletins.component.ts:137`. The bulletin line is the panel's **mount gate** — it must track the panel or the panel mounts empty. The original write-up said two call sites; it is four.
  - **Blast radius, deliberately one-directional**: of the seven status cohorts, six are identical before and after. Only *id present + dead status* changes, and there the change is a row **appearing**. Nothing is taken away from anyone.
  - **`client-header-bar.component.ts:152` "Update CC Info" left on the id gate ON PURPOSE.** ADN writes `expired` when a plan **finishes normally**, so swapping that line would hide the item from every family who ever completed a plan. Counts (dev DB, 08-23): `expired` = **4,581** registrations (2026: 2,183 · 2025: 2,238), only **59** of which owe anything — versus the cohort this item is about, canceled/terminated **with a balance** = **386** all-time ($948,715), of which **76** are in the live 2026/2027 seasons ($244,249). Roughly 12:1 against removing a visible affordance, on a branch nobody can test (no terminated subscription in the data, Ann 08-21). **Revisit only as its own item, with a ruling.**
  - **~~Not bundled~~ — BOTH DEFERRED HALVES ARE NOW RESOLVED (checked 08-26); nothing is outstanding under this item.**
    - **Stale template copy → ✅ FIXED 08-25 in `3969edbc`**, as a side effect of aligning the director drafts with the ARB sweep's family emails (the "two copies, identical words" ruling). All of `arb-health.component.ts:43,74` and `email-templates.ts:191,233` now read *"Balance Due is shown near the top"* → *"Click **Update Card & Pay Balance**"*. `grep` for "bottom of the screen" / "Click Submit" across `src/` returns nothing. The commit also caught a third error never filed here: the templates named the menu item *"Update CC Info (will also pay for failed auto-payments)"*; it is just **Update CC Info**.
    - **Club-rep `MyClubRepNonArbOwed` half → still id-gated, but UNREACHABLE. Split out to → AR-043**, filed 08-26 as latent with its precondition. Do not build it off this entry.
  - **Verification**: `dotnet build` green; frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean. **Tests not run — Todd runs them.**
  - **Ann verifies**: a family with a **cancelled plan and a balance** now sees "Pay Balance Due" and can pay · a family **mid-plan** still does not see it · a family with **no plan** sees exactly what they saw before.
  - **No sweep-health precondition — checked 08-23; the earlier warning here was WRONG.** A stalled `AdnSweepService` fails SAFE: plans only ever go active -> dead (re-enrollment mints a new id and writes "active"), so a stale status reads MORE alive, the gate reads live, and the row stays hidden = today's behavior, not a double payment. A double payment would need a status reading dead while ADN still drafts, i.e. a WRONG write, not a MISSING one, and no path does that. The dead statuses that matter are written INLINE by the cancel paths (`RegistrationSearchService:976`, `TeamSearchService:371`) in the same operation that cancels at ADN; the 24h sweep only catches ADN-side transitions. The dependency is not new either: `PartitionArbEnrolled` already reads this same column on the write side.
- **Severity**: 🔴 Bug — customer-facing, blocks self-service collections for expired/terminated ARB families. *(Severity describes the defect, not its state — this one is **BUILT**; see the status line.)* Promotion to the `PL-` queue is moot: built 08-23, ships with the next deploy.
- **✅ VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 08-24) — BOTH DIRECTIONS, which is what makes this safe:**
  - **Dead plan → row appears.** Signed in as a YJ Players 2027 player holding a **terminated** subscription: "Pay Balance Due" shows in **both** places (user menu and the landing Smart Bulletin / Manage panel). That is the defect fixed.
  - **Live plan behind in payments → row still absent.** An **active** ARB subscriber who is behind shows **Update CC Info and NO Pay Balance Due**. This is the half that matters most — it is the double-payment guard, and it held. A regression here would have charged families twice.
  - Both tested against a current dev deploy (Ann confirmed dev up to date 08-24).
- **🔻 FOLLOW-UP RAISED BY ANN (08-24) — the same liveness rule is not applied to "Update CC Info".** On that terminated-plan account **Update CC Info is still offered**, even though the family no longer has an active subscription. Her ruling: *"this needs to be handled everywhere possible, meaning any menu / smart bulletin."*
  - **Why it matters** (Ann called it "not a game changer", and it isn't — but it is a real dead end): the family now sees **two competing money actions for the same debt** and no way to tell which is right; the dunning email names one, the menu offers two. The Update CC screen loads a subscription to work against, and for a dead plan there is likely nothing to load — this FAQ entry already exists for that outcome: *"The page says it couldn't load my subscription."* And the **parent-facing help is now wrong for these families** — `public/help/registration-wizard/faq.html` and `public/help/arb-update-cc/faq.html` both tell them updating the card also settles anything past due, which is untrue once the plan is dead.
  - **THE SWEEP — audited 08-24, and it is small.** Only **one** place still gates on the raw id: **`client-header-bar.component.ts:152`** — `if (pulse.myAdnSubscriptionId && user.regId)` → "Update CC Info". Everything else already moved to the liveness flag (`:139`, `:165`, `registration-panel.component.ts:225`). **`MyAdnSubscriptionId` must stay in the DTO** — the route still needs the regId-based link; it is the *gate* that should change, not the field.
  - **The fix is one line**: use the same condition line 139 already uses — `pulse.myHasLiveArbSubscription` — since Todd's `ArbSubscriptionStatus.IsLive` and the pulse flag both already exist. No new rule, no new plumbing.
  - **⚠ Ann's "everywhere possible" is broader than this one line — treat it as a standing rule, not a single edit.** Any future menu item, Smart Bulletin, Manage row or CTA that acts on an ARB plan must gate on **liveness**, never on "an id exists". The id means *this registration once had a plan*; it has meant that since AR-013 was filed.
  - **⚠ Do NOT bundle the help-text correction blind.** If Update CC is hidden for dead plans, the two FAQ files above need their wording adjusted in the same pass, or they will describe a menu item those families cannot see.
  - **✅ ANN'S DECISION (08-24) — HIDE IT.** *"Yes if it's a dead plan, this creates a false path for them. Recommend we hide it in these situations."* So this is a decided change, not an open question: **Update CC Info is hidden when the plan is not live.** The framing to keep is hers — it is a **false path**: an action offered to someone that cannot do what they need.
  - **One risk, stated for the record**: hiding Update CC means a dead-plan family can no longer touch their stored card. That only bites if a plan can read dead while ADN still drafts — and Todd's own 08-23 analysis says no path writes a dead status while billing continues. Judged theoretical, but it is the thing to be wrong about.
- **Status**: ✅ **VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 08-24) — CLOSED.** Both directions confirmed on a current dev deploy: a dead plan now sees Pay Balance Due in menu *and* Smart Bulletin, and a live plan behind in payments still does not (the double-payment guard held). Built 08-23 (`3383b431`). **The "Update CC Info" follow-up was SPLIT OUT to → AR-027 (Ann, 08-24)** so this item closes clean rather than carrying mixed state; nothing outstanding here.

### AR-012: [Fees / Schema] A job with two leagues cannot hold per-league fees for the same role — `UX_JobFees_Scope` collapses the league tier
- **Topic**: `fees.JobFees` → league-scoped fee rows (the top tier of the base-fee cascade)
- **Origin**: found by Claude 08-19 while fixing the team-fee agegroup pin (`b2a81780`…`eb671a6e`). **Not an Ann report** — no user has hit it. Filed so it doesn't evaporate.
- **What's wrong**: `UX_JobFees_Scope` is UNIQUE on `(JobId, RoleId, AgegroupId, TeamId)`. A league-scoped row has **both** `AgegroupId` and `TeamId` NULL, and SQL Server treats NULLs as **equal** in a unique index — so only one `(JobId, RoleId, NULL, NULL)` row can exist per job per role. `LeagueId` is **not in the index**, so it cannot distinguish them. A job with two leagues therefore cannot carry a league-scoped Player fee for each; the second insert is rejected with a unique-key violation.
- **Blast radius today: ZERO — verified 08-19.** No `(job, role)` pair holds more than one league-scoped row, and no job that has league-scoped fees has more than one league. The condition has never been reachable in practice because multi-league jobs don't currently set per-league fees.
- **Why it's low priority**: it **fails loud, not silent** — a director would get a save failure, never a wrong price. Most jobs are single-league.
- **Fix when we take it**: put `LeagueId` in the key — replace `UX_JobFees_Scope` with UNIQUE `(JobId, RoleId, LeagueId, AgegroupId, TeamId)`. Schema change against a **live prod DB**, so it takes the DB-first drill: hand-written DDL, reviewed, applied by Todd. No code change needed.
- **Related, deliberately NOT bundled**: the same index also fails to constrain the **team** tier's real key `(JobId, RoleId, TeamId)` — which is what allowed the team-fee duplicate hazard fixed in `eb671a6e`. Same root shape both times: **the index describes storage columns, not what the tiers actually identify.** A redesign of the JobFees scope key would close both; neither is urgent enough to force one.
- **Severity**: latent schema defect — zero instances, fails loud, no money risk
- **ANN'S INPUT (08-21) — a STRONGER claim than the banked data, and it may close this outright**: *"I don't think we've ever had more than one league per job so not sure it's relevant. Will let Todd close this one out."*
  - **Note the difference in scope.** The 08-19 finding was narrower: no job **that has league-scoped fees** has more than one league. Ann is asserting **no job has ever had more than one league, period**. If that holds, the defect isn't merely unreached — it's **unreachable**, and this is a Won't Fix rather than a park.
  - **To settle it, one query**: `SELECT JobId, COUNT(*) FROM Leagues GROUP BY JobId HAVING COUNT(*) > 1`. Zero rows across live + legacy ⇒ close as Won't Fix on Ann's reasoning. Any rows ⇒ the park stands, and those jobs are the ones at risk the day someone sets per-league fees.
  - **Deferred to Todd for the close-out call at Ann's request** — she has seen it and is not asking for a fix.
- **Status**: 🔵 REVISIT — filed 08-19. **Awaiting Todd's close-out decision (Ann, 08-21).** Original un-park trigger still stands if it isn't closed: anyone reports a fee save failing on a multi-league job, or we take a deliberate pass at the `JobFees` scope key.

### AR-011: [Payments / Add Accounting Record] The Amount field cannot be cleared — a `0` reappears under the caret
- **Topic**: Payments → registrant/team fly-in → **Add Accounting Record** → AMOUNT field (Credit Card / Check / Correction)
- **Split out of AR-009 (08-17)**, which was the *disappearing modal* and is fixed. This is the other half of Ann's original write-up — the part about deleting the entry to retype it.
- **What the admin sees**: Amount shows `900`. Backspacing to retype gives `90`, `9`, then the moment the field goes empty **a `0` appears on its own** with the caret after it, so the new number is typed beside it and the field reads `0900`. The field fights the user.
- **Root cause (verified, Claude 08-17)**: one-way binding `[ngModel]="amount()"` + `(ngModelChange)="setAmount($event)"`, and `setAmount` coerces with `value ?? 0`. An empty number input emits `null` → stored as `0` → the signal now disagrees with the empty box, so Angular writes `0` back into it. The signal has no way to represent "empty".
- **NOT a money risk — deliberately deferred on that basis (Todd, 08-17).** `canSubmitPayment()` already refuses zero on every tab (CC needs `amt > 0`; correction returns false at `0`; check falls through to `amt !== 0`), so Save is disabled and a `$0` record cannot be booked. `0900` also parses to `900`, so even the ugly display submits the correct number. The failure mode is confusion and re-typing, not a wrong ledger amount.
- **Fix when we take it**: let the amount signal hold `null` while the field is empty and let the existing submit guard treat `null` as not-submittable. Two lines, one component, no backend, no DTO. **Money-entry binding — give it its own deliberate pass and its own verification; do NOT ride it along behind an unrelated fix.**
- **Severity**: UX friction on a money form (no incorrect-amount path)
- **🔓 UN-PARKED 08-21 — Todd's trigger condition has been met.** He parked this pending "Ann re-reports the field fighting her after the AR-009 backdrop fix lands." The backdrop fix landed (`83af23f6f`) and **Ann reproduced it deliberately on 08-21**: cursor at the end of an existing Amount, backspace to empty → **a `0` appears**. She can then type a new amount over it.
  - **Code confirmed unchanged (Claude, 08-21)**: `accounting-ledger.component.ts:475-477` still reads `this.amount.set(Math.round((value ?? 0) * 100) / 100)` — the `?? 0` is the defect — and both inputs still bind `[ngModel]="amount()"` + `(ngModelChange)="setAmount($event)"` (template `:224` and `:395`). The only commit to this component since 08-17 is the AR-009 backdrop fix, which never went near the amount box. **Nothing was fixed here; the earlier "behaves fine" was a non-repro, not a resolution.**
  - **Note two bound inputs share `setAmount`** (`:224` refund modal, `:395` add-record) — fix once, verify both.
  - **Severity confirmed unchanged**: still no wrong-amount path (submit guards refuse `0`; `0900` parses to `900`). This remains UX friction on a money form, not a ledger risk — but it is real, reproducible, and now demonstrated post-fix.
- **Status**: 🔴 OPEN — for Todd. Un-parked 08-21 on Ann's deliberate repro. Fix as scoped above (let the signal hold `null` for empty); **its own pass, its own verification — do not bundle.**

### AR-010: [Payments / ARB] Update CC for ARB subscribers — confirm it works, incl. behind-in-payments catch-up (legacy parity)
- **Topic**: Payments → ARB subscriptions → **Update CC** menu
- **Context**: This is a function we **couldn't test before but can now**. In **Legacy**, when an ARB registrant appeared under **"Look/Fall Behind in Payment"** (ARB Subscription Health → behind-in-payments list), going to the **Update CC** menu let them **make that overdue payment** at the same time — and they could **re-enter the same card** as before and still make the payment.
- **Verify — all 3 scenarios**:
  1. **Not behind in payments** → Update CC only (just swaps the card on the subscription, no charge).
  2. **Behind in payments** → Update CC **and make the past-due payment at the same time** (new card).
  3. **Behind in payments** → enter the **same CC** as before **and still make the payment** (legacy allowed re-using the identical card to catch up — confirm the new system doesn't reject "same card" and block the catch-up).
- **Test data**: **`YJ North Players 2026-2027`** currently has players who are **Behind in Payments** — use it for scenarios 2 & 3.
- **For Todd / testing**: confirm the Update CC flow (a) succeeds for the not-behind case, (b) offers/executes the catch-up charge for behind subscribers, and (c) permits the same-card re-entry catch-up. Flag if any scenario errors or silently fails to charge.
- **Severity**: Legacy-parity verification (test now that ARB behind-payment data exists)
- **Status**: ✅ VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 08-21) — **CLOSED.** Update CC confirmed working for ARB subscribers, legacy parity held.
  - **All three scenarios confirmed (Ann, 08-21)**, including the one most at risk: **scenario 3 — re-entering the SAME card while behind still executes the catch-up charge.** Legacy allowed it; the new system does too. It does not treat an identical card as a no-op and skip the payment.
  - **Terminated plans NOT covered — no terminated subscription exists in the test data (Ann, 08-21).** So AR-013's open question cannot be answered by testing today; see the ruling recorded there.

### AR-009: [Payments / Add Accounting Record] Deleting the Amount value boots you out of the popup
- **Topic**: Payments → registrant/team fly-in → **Add Accounting Record** popup → Amount field
- **Where**: the fly-in for a **Player**, a **Team Club Rep**, or a **Team** → **Add Accounting Record** modal (Credit Card / Check / Correction tabs).
- **Repro (Ann)**: In the **AMOUNT** field, place the cursor at the **end** of an existing number and **delete the entry** (backspace) to type a different amount → the **modal closes / boots you out** unexpectedly. See image (Check tab, Amount `900` being edited, arrow on the field).
- **Likely cause (to verify, Claude)**: a **Backspace/Delete keydown on the (now-empty) Amount input is bubbling to a modal-level handler** — either an Escape/close key binding, a `keydown` that treats empty-field backspace as "back/dismiss," or the value going empty/`NaN` tripping a guard that dismisses the dialog. Classic fix: `stopPropagation` on the input's keydown (or don't close the modal on backspace/empty), and treat an empty Amount as `0`/invalid inline rather than closing.
- **For Todd**: Amount input in the Add Accounting Record modal must let the user clear/retype freely — backspace at field start/end should never close the dialog. Confirm no `@keydown`/host listener is catching Backspace, and that clearing the field doesn't trigger a close/validation-dismiss.
- **Severity**: Bug / data entry (annoying + risks losing entered check number/comment on close)
- **ROOT CAUSE — the backdrop, not the keystroke (Todd's lead, 08-17).** The filed hypothesis is wrong and worth recording as wrong: **no Backspace handler exists anywhere in the frontend** except the unrelated score-entry modal, and modal visibility has only four inbound close paths (backdrop, Escape, X, submit) — a keystroke reaches none of them. Todd's observation that clicking outside the modal dismisses it is the actual mechanism, and it was by design: the overlay carried `(click)="closePaymentModal()"`.
  - **Why a mouse wobble is enough, and why Ann's box but not Todd's:** a `click` event fires on the **nearest common ancestor of the mousedown and mouseup targets**. Press inside the card, release a few pixels outside it, and the browser dispatches `click` on the *overlay* — so the modal closes even though the press began inside it. Triggers: pointer drift (Bluetooth jitter, low battery, trackpad), clicking near the card's edge, or **`appDraggableModal` sliding the card out from under a stationary cursor**. Intermittent and hardware-sensitive by nature — which is exactly why it wouldn't reproduce on a different machine. Her mouse is the aggravating factor; the code is what turned a 3-pixel wobble into lost data entry.
- **FIXED (Claude, 08-17) — awaiting Ann verify.** Backdrop dismissal now requires the press **and** the release to land on the backdrop itself (`(mousedown)`/`(mouseup)` pair + `event.target === event.currentTarget` on both). Press-inside/release-outside and press-outside/release-inside both fall through and do nothing. Escape, X, Cancel and submit unchanged; click-away still works when it's genuinely a click away. Applies to the **CC Refund** modal too, which shares the overlay — correct, not collateral. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.
  - Chose this over removing backdrop-close outright (also offered): it kills the failure mode without taking away a working affordance, and it holds up if her mouse is replaced tomorrow.
- **FOUND, NOT FIXED — the Amount field cannot be cleared.** `[ngModel]="amount()"` + `setAmount()` doing `value ?? 0` means deleting the last digit emits `null` → stores `0` → Angular writes `0` straight back under the caret. So "delete the entry and type a different amount" literally fights the user. Independent of the backdrop bug, and it IS the action Ann described. **Needs a go — not built.**
- **NOT SWEPT**: the same `(click)`-on-overlay backdrop pattern likely appears on other modals app-wide. Deliberately left for its own item rather than smuggled into this fix.
- **Status**: ✅ VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 08-17) — **CLOSED.** She is no longer getting the random disappearance (Todd relayed). The press-and-release requirement holds on the machine the bug was native to, which is the only box that could have proved it. Un-park AR-011 only if she re-reports the Amount field itself fighting her.

### AR-008: [Coach Registrations / Email] Direct-placement Staff confirmation email shows no teams — "Teams you will be rostered with:" is blank on tournaments/leagues
- **Topic**: Coach Registrations → confirmation email → team list token (`!STAFFCHOICES` / `!F-TEAMS`)
- **Applies to**: **Direct-to-roster** coach type (Tournament/League Staff). **Works correctly** for the Club funnel (coach → Unassigned Adult).
- **Observation (Ann)**: On tournament/league coach confirmations, the **"Teams you will be rostered with:"** section is **empty** — the staff-teams token doesn't render the assigned teams. See image: "Ann Massey … registered as a Coach for: Long Island Elite Lacrosse:Prime Time Recruiting Showcase 2026 / Teams you will be rostered with:" then **blank** before the Contacts table. The same token **does** populate for coaches who register as Unassigned Adults.
- **Hypothesis to verify (Claude)**: the two regimes store the team selection **differently**, and the token resolver likely reads the UA shape:
  - **Unassigned Adult (Club):** selections are non-binding **REQUESTS** written into **`SpecialRequests`** (structured JSON) — the token reads from there ✅.
  - **Direct-placement Staff (Tournament/League):** `AllowTeamRequests:false`, so **nothing is written to `SpecialRequests`**; instead one **Staff `Registration` per team** is minted with **`AssignedTeamId`** set (per `e6d3bd08`). If the confirmation token resolves off `SpecialRequests` (or off a single reg's AssignedTeamId) rather than aggregating the **per-team Staff rows**, it comes back empty ❌.
  - Note there IS a `BuildStaffTeamsHtmlAsync` path that replaces `!F-TEAMS` by querying active Staff rows by user+job — worth checking whether (a) the confirmation for direct placement actually goes through that path, and (b) it runs **after** all per-team Staff rows are committed (a timing gap would also yield empty).
- **For Todd**: make the tournament/league Staff confirmation aggregate the **per-team Staff Registrations (AssignedTeamId → team)** for this user+job and render them in the "rostered with" list, matching the UA behavior. Confirm which token the template uses (`!STAFFCHOICES` vs `!F-TEAMS`).
- **Severity**: Bug / confirmation correctness (non-blocking, but the coach gets an email that omits their teams)
- **ROOT CAUSE CONFIRMED (Claude, code + data, 08-17)** — the token is **`!F-STAFFCHOICES`**, and it resolved **only** off `Registrations.specialRequests`:
  - The job's `CoachReg_ConfirmationEmail` on `lielite-primetimerecruitingshowcase-2026` reads `...Teams you will be rostered with:<br><br>!F-STAFFCHOICES...` — the heading is **director-authored per job**, not ours.
  - `BuildStaffChoicesAsync` parsed the codified `{teams:[…]}` request JSON. Direct placement sets `AllowTeamRequests: false` and **never writes that column** (per `e6d3bd08`) — the teams live in one Staff row per team with `assigned_teamID` set. No requested ids → `string.Empty` → heading over a blank. Verified against live rows on that job: `assigned_teamID` **set**, `specialRequests` **NULL/empty**, on every Staff row.
  - **Not a timing gap** and **not** `BuildStaffTeamsHtmlAsync` — that path only serves `!F-TEAMS` and already aggregates the Staff rows correctly. The bug is that the *other* team token was never taught the second regime.
  - **Scope, by effective coach template (dev-restore DB):** Tournament (2) — **144 jobs on `!F-STAFFCHOICES`** (broken) vs 212 on `!F-TEAMS` (already correct). League (3) — **12** vs 3. That per-job template split is why the symptom isn't universal.
- **FIXED (Claude, 08-17) — awaiting Ann verify.** Fixed in the **shared resolver** (Todd's pick over pre-rendering at the adult chokepoint, so every consumer of the token benefits, not just the adult wizard):
  - `BuildStaffChoicesAsync` — when SpecialRequests yields no requested ids, fall back to `GetCoachTeamChoicesAsync` (every **active** Staff row for this user+job, one per team) and render the same `Club: Age: Team` list. **UA path byte-identical** — with requested ids present nothing changes. Dedupes on the label: the email lists teams, not rows.
  - `GetCoachTeamChoicesAsync` — two latent defects hardened in the same pass, both live only now that real confirmations route through it: added the **`bActive` filter** (a coach removed from a team must not see it on a resend), and the club-rep join for the club name is now a **left join** with a null-safe projection (that FK is nullable — a rostered team must never drop out of a confirmation for lacking a club rep; mirrors `GetTeamLabelsByIdsAsync`).
  - Backend only — no DTO change, no API-model regen. `dotnet build` green.
- **Rulings recorded**: coaches already mailed a blank list get **no resend and no backfill** (Todd) · the *"Teams you will be rostered with:"* heading **stays as authored** (Todd). Noted but NOT actioned: the same token on a **club** site renders *requested* teams under a heading promising placement — a per-job copy mismatch across ~323 club templates, a separate item if it ever matters.
- **Status**: ✅ VERIFIED PASSING (Ann, 08-21) — **CLOSED.** Tournament/league coach confirmation now lists every team; club-site "Requested Teams" path unchanged. Noted but still unfiled: the same token on a **club** site renders *requested* teams under a heading promising placement (~323 club templates) — separate item if it ever matters.

### AR-007: [Communications / Smart Bulletins] Allow SuperUser (and consider Director) to inactivate a Smart Bulletin
- **Topic**: Communications → Smart Bulletins (the auto-generated registration banner / quicklinks)
- **Request (Ann)**: Give **SuperUser** the ability to **inactivate (turn off) a Smart Bulletin** when they don't want it shown. **Consider extending the same option to Directors.**
- **Why (Ann's use case)**: A director may prefer a **customized, info-only banner** (announcement text without the registration quicklinks), or may **not want the Smart Bulletin to show at all**. Right now there's no way to suppress it, so they can't substitute their own banner or hide it.
- **For Todd — decisions**:
  1. **Scope** — SuperUser-only first, or SuperUser + Director? (Ann leans toward offering it to Directors too.)
  2. **Granularity** — a simple **on/off (active/inactive) toggle** per Smart Bulletin, vs. an "info-only" mode that keeps the banner but strips the registration quicklinks. Ann's phrasing suggests both are desirable (turn off entirely *or* show info without quicklinks).
  3. Confirm what "Smart Bulletin" maps to in the current model (auto-generated vs. director-authored bulletin) so the toggle lands on the right entity.
- **Severity**: Feature / director control (non-blocking)
- **MODEL ANSWER to Ann's Q3 (Claude, 08-19): a "Smart Bulletin" is NOT a record.** The ✨ SMART band is assembled at render time from the live pulse (`smart-bulletins.component.ts`); each section is a template branch gated on its pulse flag **and** the lifecycle phase. No row, no id, no Active column — *"the smart panels are read-only for all."* The hand-authored bulletins are the ones with rows, and they **already** carry a one-click inactivate for `isAdmin()` = Director/SuperDirector/SuperUser (`bulletins.component.ts:82`), so Ann's "SuperUser first, Directors maybe" is already answered in the more generous direction on the surface that has rows.
- **RULING 1 — suppression DECLINED (Todd, 08-19): "we built this for a reason, to alleviate club rep admin time, why the hell are we undoing that?"** A director-facing off switch undoes the automation the band exists to provide, and is the kind of toggle flipped once and forgotten — a site quietly stops advertising registration while registration is open. It also kills the dashboard's premise (the director sees exactly what the public sees). Same shape as the **AM-091** ruling: an auto-derived card is a *true readout*, not papered over. **Never re-propose an SB off switch.**
- **RULING 2 — reordering (bulletins above the band) REJECTED ON DATA (08-19).** Looked free (order is pure template — `bulletins.component.html` emits the band, then the "News & Updates" divider, then the manual `@for`; nothing data-driven). **But of 297 live bulletins across 187 jobs, 294 started >30 days ago and 269 started >180 days ago.** They are in-window because of a far-out EndDate, not because they are current — so flipping would put *last season's notice* above the live registration CTA and the game clock on 187 sites. The band also **self-hides when empty**, so SB-first never crowds a director without cause, while B-first pushes the band down unconditionally. The "it's the organizer's site, their voice leads" framing also optimizes for the organizer over the visitor, who came to register / check a schedule / find a game time — all of which live in the band.
- **RULING 3 — per-bulletin "pin above the band" REJECTED as a cosmetic schema change (Todd, 08-19).** A new `Jobs.Bulletins` column for landing-page ordering is presentation, not data, on a live prod DB shared with staging and legacy — and the freshness numbers say nobody is publishing timely notices they need to elevate. **Do NOT launder `BCore` or `ExpireHours` into this**: `BCore` is scaffolding-only (mapped in the DbContext of BOTH the new stack and the legacy TSIC-Unify reference, **never read or written by any code**; 3,474 true / 1 false / 470 null = an insert default, not a decision).
- **📌 THE CORRECT FUTURE APPROACH — recorded now, deliberately NOT built (Todd, 08-19): "this is just not the time to do it."** The blocker was never storage, it was **identity**: the SB sections have no durable keys, so there is nothing to hang a rule on. The right shape is a **new `smartbulletins` SQL schema** (a schema namespace, sibling to `Leagues`, `Jobs`, `widgets`, `nav` — *not* "a schema change" in the loose sense) that **tracks the individual SB types as rows** and **allows overrides** against them. That gives each smart bulletin a first-class identity, at which point suppression / precedence / per-job and per-role overrides all become ordinary rows instead of columns bolted onto `Jobs` or `Jobs.Bulletins`. **When this is picked up, build the schema first — do not retrofit a boolean.** (For reference: `widgets.JobWidget` already models a per-job/per-role override tier with `IsEnabled` + `Config`, but it is **empty** — 0 rows — and the band is hard-placed in `job-landing.component.html:72`, never routed through the widget outlet, so the widget tables are NOT a shortcut here.)
- **Status**: ✅ CLOSED (Todd, 08-19) — suppression declined on principle, reorder rejected on data, pin rejected as cosmetic schema. **Reopen only with a real client need**, and then via the `smartbulletins` schema above. ✅ Acknowledged (Ann, 08-21).

### AR-006: [LADT] Surface "Self Roster" state in LADT — Age Group column + info buttons; clarify vs Team "Active"
- **Topic**: LADT (League / Age-Group / Division / Team editor) → self-rostering visibility
- **Request (Ann)**:
  1. **Age Group level** — add a **SELF ROSTER** column (checked / unchecked) so a director can see at a glance whether self-rostering is turned on for that age group.
  2. **Team level clarity** — the Team editor shows **Active**, but it's ambiguous: does **Active** mean self-rostering is on, or is **Self Rostering a separate toggle** that also has to be turned on? Make the distinction explicit.
  3. **Info buttons on both levels** — Ann assumes self-roster is **hierarchical** (set at a higher level, inherited down, like the payment-phase cascade). Add an **info (ℹ) button at both the Age Group and Team levels** noting **where the setting is actually controlled** (which level owns it, what's inherited).
- **For Todd — confirm the model first**: pin down the real self-roster flag(s) and their hierarchy before building the UI — is there an age-group-level self-roster flag, a team-level one, both, and how do they cascade? **⚠ Roster-visibility semantics were just changed in `e6d3bd08` (consent-gated `BAllowRosterViewAdult`)** — confirm whether "self roster" here is that same flag or a distinct player-self-rostering toggle, so the new column/info copy describes the right thing. Use design-system info-button pattern; no hardcoded colors.
- **⚠ Constraint (Ann)**: adding the SELF ROSTER column **must NOT introduce horizontal scrolling** on the Age Group grid. Fit it within the existing width (tighten/absorb other columns, use a compact checkbox/icon cell) — no h-scroll.
- **Severity**: UI clarity / director usability (non-blocking)
- **MODEL CONFIRMED (Claude, 08-19) — Ann's hierarchy assumption is WRONG.** `Agegroups.bAllowSelfRostering` and `Teams.bAllowSelfRostering` are **OR'd, not a cascade** (`TeamSelfRosterAvailability.cs:39`) — neither level overrides the other; an age-group check is a *floor* enabling every team under it regardless of that team's own unchecked box. `Active` is **necessary but not sufficient**: the real rule is Active **and** a self-roster flag **and** not a WAITLIST/Dropped bucket **and** inside the team's `Effectiveasofdate`/`Expireondate` window (null/zero-width window PASSES). **The ⚠ is resolved: this is NOT the `e6d3bd08` flag** — `BAllowRosterViewAdult` governs who may *view* a roster; `bAllowSelfRostering` governs whether a player may *add themselves*. Different tables, different purpose.
- **Data (SQL, 08-19): the age group IS the answer.** Of 51,411 teams, **44,249** are self-rosterable via their age group's flag and **exactly ONE** is enabled by a team-level checkbox alone (`stepscalifornia-players-2026-2027`, agegroup `Summer Season ONLY`, live). The 861 team-level flags are 26 jobs / ~2 customers (LI Yellow Jackets, TLC Lacrosse) propagating via clone `CopyRosterSettings`. Safe to build **display** on the age-group flag; never **logic**.
- **BUILT + PUSHED `32b4eead` (08-19) — Todd: "the answer is very simple, just show the boolean col here."** One entry in `AGEGROUP_COLUMNS` (`ladt-grid-columns.ts`), 85px, matching the identical column the **Team** grid already carried. No backend, no DTO, no regen — `AgegroupDetailDto` already ships `bAllowSelfRostering` and the sibling grid's boolean+headerTooltip branch already renders this shape (3rd Party is the precedent). The ⓘ states the real rule (OR'd, not a cascade; window still gates). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Verified by Todd on `stepsgirls-players-2025-2026`.
